- Position G&L delivery around accountability (weekly deal-flow targets, LOIs sent, exit-readiness milestones), since buyers and sellers mostly lack follow-through, not information.
- Mirror his positioning in G&L marketing: "we've done this exact deal type before" — specific, local expertise sells better than generalist advice.

## My $5M/mo Sales Onboarding Tutorial (internal company vid) — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MBj6qrUx9W8
**Gist:** The internal onboarding video every new account executive watches. It sets expectations for a 100-lead trial on the cheapest (tier 3) leads, where reps must close at least 2 deals while showing lead efficiency, independence and selling ability. Reps who pass are promoted to tier 1–2 leads with double or triple bookings and setter support.
**Nuggets:**
- `[SALES-HIRING]` Pair a pre-recorded expectations video with a careers page so reps start with confidence and the wrong ones self-select out before wasting time and leads. ([0:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MBj6qrUx9W8&t=0s))
- `[SALES-MGMT]` Trial = 100 leads allocated by marketing; pass = at least 2 closed deals. Evaluated on lead efficiency, independence and actual selling. ([0:30](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MBj6qrUx9W8&t=30s))
- `[KPIS]` Show rate separates reps. Passive reps who wait for cold-traffic tier-3 leads get ~30% show; top reps double it by texting, emailing and calling pre-call, and following up fast on no-shows. That's twice the live calls from an identical lead pool. ([1:31](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MBj6qrUx9W8&t=91s))
- `[SALES-MGMT]` Never drop a booked call. If you can't take it, hand it off in the dedicated "hot transfer" Slack channel. Example: a rep left after 2 minutes for lunch, the lead arrived at minute 3, found no one, and left a bad Trustpilot review. A dropped call ends the trial. ([2:34](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MBj6qrUx9W8&t=154s))
- `[SALES-MGMT]` Hot transfers both ways: post double-booked calls to the channel, and pick up others' calls when you get a no-show. Transfers are often tier 1–2 leads, so reps who never check the channel don't last. ([3:05](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MBj6qrUx9W8&t=185s))
- `[SALES-HIRING]` Independence: start on the day you say you will; finish the 2–5 day training quickly (drag for a week and the offer may be rescinded); solve tech problems yourself; use executive judgement on unique prospect questions. ([4:08](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MBj6qrUx9W8&t=248s))
- `[SALES-HIRING]` Tracked data point: the top 5 reps never asked a single question beyond the onboarding material and the short onboarding call with the sales lead. ([5:09](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MBj6qrUx9W8&t=309s))
- `[SALES-MGMT]` Trial on the cheapest, lowest-quality (tier 3) leads on purpose. It limits money wasted on reps who burn leads, and anyone who can close them can close better leads. Rebuttal to "they're broke/unsophisticated": broke owners need marketing most, and a sophisticated rep should easily close an unsophisticated buyer. ([5:39](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MBj6qrUx9W8&t=339s))
- `[SALES-MGMT]` Lead tiering: setters/qualification assign tier 1/2/3 leads; reps are promoted up tiers as they perform. ([7:11](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MBj6qrUx9W8&t=431s))
- `[SETTING]` Post-trial perks: double or triple bookings every hour (pick the better prospect from pre-call info and setter qualification), a dedicated appointment setter or calendar management, and one of the highest commission ceilings in online B2B transactional sales. ([7:41](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MBj6qrUx9W8&t=461s))
- `[SALES-HIRING]` "Everyone sounds great in an interview." Live-lead trials reveal who can put all the pieces together. ([8:44](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MBj6qrUx9W8&t=524s))
- `[SALES-MGMT]` Publish trial records as benchmarks: fastest close was the first lead in the first hour; best trial was 5 deals; fastest graduation was 2 days (13 leads → 3 closes); best first month was $46K commission on tier-3 leads only. ([9:15](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MBj6qrUx9W8&t=555s))
- `[SALES-HIRING]` Expect reps to come "batteries included." The company can't fund year-long ramp periods at current lead costs; high comp ceiling, high expectations. ([11:24](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MBj6qrUx9W8&t=684s))
**Short quotes:**
- "We expect you to come batteries included, and the starting comp for the opportunity reflects that." ([11:24](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MBj6qrUx9W8&t=684s))
- "If they are broke, they need marketing more than anyone else." ([6:40](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MBj6qrUx9W8&t=400s))
**G&L angle:**
- Record a G&L "AE onboarding & trial" video plus a careers page: X leads on the lowest tier (e.g. buy-side applicants with smaller capital or less experience), with a defined close minimum to pass.
- Tier leads from application data. Tier 1: owners with >$1M EBITDA wanting to exit, or buyers with capital and operating experience. Tier 3: early-stage aspiring acquirers. Promote reps up tiers based on performance.
- Make reps own show rate (text, email, call before every call; immediate no-show follow-up) and set up a hot-transfer channel so no booked owner call ever goes unattended. A no-show on a 60-year-old business owner damages reputation.
- Measure "questions asked" during onboarding as a proxy for independence, and keep training to 2–5 days of written material plus recorded calls.

## I just made 5 million dollars in one month. — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=jm14bVak6rA
**Gist:** A record $5M month came from one small onboarding change that cut 7-day refunds from about 40% to about 5%. He had identified the fix months earlier and delayed it, at an estimated cost of about $6M. The lesson: decisions and willingness to take risks beat hard work and IQ.
**Nuggets:**
- `[SCALING-STAGES]` They were stuck at $3–4M/mo for months because scaling wasn't profitable enough. One constraint change added about $2M/mo of profitable revenue, even though it went live mid-month. ([1:34](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=jm14bVak6rA&t=94s))
- `[MINDSET]` He had named the exact fix in February and didn't act on it. The opportunity cost was about $6M. Take identified single-constraint fixes seriously and implement them immediately. ([0:30](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=jm14bVak6rA&t=30s))
- `[PRICING]` Recap: an earlier two-minute billing change (from MRR to upfront) took them from $1.3M to $3M/mo in 30 days, with no extra hours or headcount. ([2:34](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=jm14bVak6rA&t=154s))
- `[FULFILLMENT]` The new fix was a same-day onboarding-process change made during the 7-day money-back window. Refunds dropped from about 40% to about 5% overnight, so retained revenue went from about $2M to nearly $3M a month. The exact change wasn't disclosed. ([3:35](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=jm14bVak6rA&t=215s))
- `[GUARANTEE]` If you offer a trial or refund guarantee, the first 7 days of onboarding are where revenue is won or lost. Instrument and optimise that window. ([3:35](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=jm14bVak6rA&t=215s))
- `[LEADERSHIP]` With about 100 staff, his own hours and intelligence don't move outcomes. His job is to make the right decisions while specialist teams (tech, ops, fulfilment, sales) each do their part better than he could. ([4:05](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=jm14bVak6rA&t=245s))
- `[MINDSET]` The third ingredient besides work ethic and intelligence is belief and appetite for risk: willingness to give something up short-term and change what already works. ([6:08](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=jm14bVak6rA&t=368s))
- `[PARTNERSHIP]` He repeats the investment and partnership offer, now for marketing companies at $1–30M/yr, with details shared under NDA. ([7:10](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=jm14bVak6rA&t=430s))
**Short quotes:**
- "Success has nothing to do with how hard you work." ([4:05](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=jm14bVak6rA&t=245s))
- "Don't waste time on hesitation, doubt, and reflection." ([8:10](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=jm14bVak6rA&t=490s))
**G&L angle:**
- Engineer the first week after a G&L client pays: a fast kickoff call, a visible first deliverable (deal criteria, buy box, first deal-flow list, or a valuation snapshot for sellers) and early team contact. This prevents buyer's remorse and refunds.
- Track refund and cancellation rate by cohort as a headline KPI next to CAC and close rate.
- When a constraint is identified (e.g. show rate, refund rate, financing approvals), fix it the same week.

## You're so scared of being broke that you will never be rich — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=XJZf_eMSfSo
**Gist:** A mindset video about risk and reinvestment. Owners cap profitable ad spend at arbitrary round numbers, and Daniel reinvested almost every dollar of profit for nearly three years. Revenue volume buys customer data, and that data is the moat competitors can't copy.
**Nuggets:**
- `[ADS]` If ads are returning several times their cost, they are a money printer. Partners refusing to scale profitable spend out of fear is the bottleneck. ([0:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=XJZf_eMSfSo&t=0s))
- `[ADS]` Spend caps like $1K, $5K or $10K/day are arbitrary round numbers that set an artificial ceiling on the business. Scale on returns, not comfort. ([1:01](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=XJZf_eMSfSo&t=61s))
- `[MINDSET]` Risk is openness to opportunity. More opportunity requires more risk. ([2:02](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=XJZf_eMSfSo&t=122s))
- `[FINANCE]` For nearly three years almost every dollar of profit went back into testing. In year one he spent more on testing than the business made, burning cash reserves from a previous business to buy customer data. ([3:04](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=XJZf_eMSfSo&t=184s))
- `[FINANCE]` Keep a small safety net, but if you want maximum upside, reinvest profit into expensive Meta tests, new campaigns, experimental hires, and improvements to product, marketing and positioning. ([4:05](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=XJZf_eMSfSo&t=245s))
- `[OTHER]` Private equity pays higher multiples for bigger businesses partly because more revenue means more customer data and more stability. Their data exceeds their three largest competitors combined. ([5:06](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=XJZf_eMSfSo&t=306s))
- `[OTHER]` Competitors copy their ads, landing pages and case-study design word for word, but can't copy the back-end knowledge: contract structure, pricing, and customer insight. ([6:08](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=XJZf_eMSfSo&t=368s))
- `[MINDSET]` Know your client better than they know themselves. Without paid-for market data you are playing chess blindfolded. ([6:38](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=XJZf_eMSfSo&t=398s))
- `[MINDSET]` The test is whether you can go months without taking money out of the business. If not, expect to stay small. ([8:39](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=XJZf_eMSfSo&t=519s))
**Short quotes:**
- "You invest money in Facebook and get more through Stripe." ([0:31](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=XJZf_eMSfSo&t=31s))
- "The information exists. You just need to buy it." ([9:09](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=XJZf_eMSfSo&t=549s))
**G&L angle:**
- Once G&L's Meta → VSL → call funnel shows positive ROAS on closed revenue, scale spend on that number and drop arbitrary daily caps.
- Reinvest early profit into testing hooks for each ICP (buyers vs sellers), VSL variants and application questions. Treat that spend as buying data.
- G&L's moat is proprietary data from call notes, application answers, deal outcomes and vendor-finance terms. Competitors can copy the ads but not this.
- Agree partner draw policy up front so a profitable funnel isn't throttled.

## Organic content vs paid ads — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Hk0k66Pc5Dk
**Gist:** Daniel, who both gets millions of organic views and spends $1M+/month on Meta ads, argues the choice depends on resources and whether your offer can sell to strangers: organic costs time and builds trust (easier sales and fulfillment), paid ads need an offer built for cold traffic and a heavier ops/support machine. Best answer is both — organic tests hooks for free and warms audiences, ads retarget and scale.
**Nuggets:**
- `[ORGANIC]` Starting from scratch with little money, start with organic — free to post, worst case you lose time; every business (even Boeing/Lockheed) benefits from posting. ([1:31](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Hk0k66Pc5Dk&t=91s))
- `[ADS]` "Good at organic → should run ads" is false: his organic-converting offer didn't convert paid cold traffic, costing ~$500k of debt and ~6 months rebuilding the offer from scratch. ([3:04](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Hk0k66Pc5Dk&t=184s))
- `[OFFER]` Paid-ads readiness test: can you sell your product/service to a stranger on the street? E-com sells itself on the checkout page; a lawyer (selling "you") must prove they're better — much harder on cold traffic. ([4:06](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Hk0k66Pc5Dk&t=246s))
- `[ORGANIC]` With good content and earned trust you can launch almost any product at your price (MrBeast/Logan Paul beating legacy brands). ([5:08](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Hk0k66Pc5Dk&t=308s))
- `[ADS]` Ads have diminishing marginal returns as you scale toward $1M/month — return on ad spend becomes logarithmic; there's a real ceiling of interested people scrolling that day. ([5:38](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Hk0k66Pc5Dk&t=338s))
- `[ADS]` Spending ~$1M/month creates brand ubiquity ("I see your ads every time I open my phone") — the same omnipresence organic can create, but reaching it with ads takes tens of millions in spend; ads just front-load the cost. ([7:09](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Hk0k66Pc5Dk&t=429s))
- `[FULFILLMENT]` Organic-sourced clients are easier to fulfil (they trust you; a free extra coaching call smooths issues); cold-traffic clients need a more complex ops/support team and brutal attention to detail — they're far less forgiving. ([8:10](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Hk0k66Pc5Dk&t=490s))
- `[SCALING-STAGES]` Organic-only = simpler, high-margin, lean business but dependent on content performance (less control). Paid = harder but bigger; he went from 2–3 staff to ~100, signing 100+ new clients/month. ([8:42](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Hk0k66Pc5Dk&t=522s))
- `[ADS]` Do both (50/50): organic opens new audiences for free you can retarget cheaply; ad viewers then get fed your organic content, building trust faster. ([10:14](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Hk0k66Pc5Dk&t=614s))
- `[CREATIVE]` Use organic as a free creative testing lab — find winning formats/angles/hooks, then run the same video as an ad. Companies spend 20–50% of ad budget on testing; skipping that can halve your costs. Some of their best ads started as organic posts. ([11:14](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Hk0k66Pc5Dk&t=674s))
- `[FUNNEL]` Free Skool group with courses, AI tools and gear recommendations as the lead magnet for people who can't yet afford help. ([11:45](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Hk0k66Pc5Dk&t=705s))
**Short quotes:**
- "Can you sell your product or service to a stranger on the street? If yes, you are going to crush it at paid ads." ([4:06](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Hk0k66Pc5Dk&t=246s))
- "Organic lets you get your foot in the door with a new audience completely for free, and then just retarget that audience with ads." ([10:44](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Hk0k66Pc5Dk&t=644s))
**G&L angle:**
- M&A advisory is a trust-based "sell you" service like the lawyer example — expect cold traffic to be hard; build proof (deal case studies, closed acquisitions, seller exits) into ads and the VSL.
- Run an organic content engine (short-form clips on vendor finance deal structures, deal breakdowns, exit-readiness tips) and promote proven organic winners as Meta ads to skip testing spend.
- Retarget organic video viewers and VSL watchers with ads; feed ad-sourced leads organic content to warm them before calls.
- Expect paid-sourced clients to need tighter onboarding and expectation-setting than referral/organic clients.

## Why I don't get views — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=LjswhfXCmCo
**Gist:** Daniel explains why his current channel gets a few thousand views when his old personal-finance brand got 100M views a month. He deliberately narrowed to a wealthy, business-owner audience because who watches matters more than how many. His agency still sells "views" to businesses as the front-end, then steers them toward revenue.
**Nuggets:**
- `[ORGANIC]` His old personal-finance brand (800K YouTube subscribers, at one point 100M views a month for six months) was monetised as a billboard: brands like PayPal or Amazon paid tens of thousands per 10–20 second post. ([1:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=LjswhfXCmCo&t=60s))
- `[ORGANIC]` The goal of a personal brand for a high-ticket business is money, not views. What matters is who the views come from. ([2:01](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=LjswhfXCmCo&t=121s))
- `[ORGANIC]` Purify the audience. Deliberately exclude beginners and make longer, slower content for financially savvy owners who have built and scaled businesses. Fewer views, but much more valuable viewers. ([2:33](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=LjswhfXCmCo&t=153s))
- `[ORGANIC]` You can change who sees you by refining the message and consciously reducing reach to a narrow market segment. ([4:06](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=LjswhfXCmCo&t=246s))
- `[ORGANIC]` Niche service businesses that need only a few clients should do the opposite of viral creators: aim for a few views from the right people. Copying million-view top-of-funnel videos is the wrong goal for them. ([5:38](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=LjswhfXCmCo&t=338s))
- `[OFFER]` Sell what the market thinks it wants (views), demonstrate it, then show it doesn't produce profit and deliver the revenue they actually want. ([6:08](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=LjswhfXCmCo&t=368s))
- `[KPIS]` Measure content by bank balance, not subscribers. His views are down, but his income is roughly 3× higher. ([8:40](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=LjswhfXCmCo&t=520s))
**Short quotes:**
- "It's not about how many people see you, it's about who sees you." ([4:06](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=LjswhfXCmCo&t=246s))
- "If you measure it by your bank account, it's crazy." ([8:40](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=LjswhfXCmCo&t=520s))
**G&L angle:**
- G&L's organic content should target capitalised professionals, operators and owners 55+ with long-form deal breakdowns. Judge it by qualified applications and closed revenue, not views.
- Low view counts on detailed vendor-finance and exit-valuation content are fine if the right buyers and sellers watch and apply.
- Ads can lead with what people think they want ("buy a business with no money down"), then on the call steer toward what actually drives results (the right deal, structure and operator).

## $100K/m VS $1M/m offers — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=29qOh8ZnF0k
**Gist:** Every big jump in Daniel's monthly revenue came from changing the offer, not from marketing tactics. An offer that only works on warm, organic traffic stops working when you pay for ads. He walks through Eugene Schwartz's awareness levels and shows how to rebuild an offer around the objections cold buyers raise: payment terms, guarantees, a refundable trial, adding services, and a back-end that is given away free.
**Nuggets:**
- `[OFFER]` Each revenue step-change came from an offer change: $0→$120K→$300K→$500K/mo in the first three months, then two offer changes broke the $1M ceiling and took them from $1.3M to $3M/mo in about 30 days. ([0:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=29qOh8ZnF0k&t=0s))
- `[OFFER]` A good offer sells itself, makes the sales team's job easier, keeps clients longer, and above all converts cold strangers. Converting cold traffic is what lets you choose how much you make each month. ([1:01](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=29qOh8ZnF0k&t=61s))
- `[ADS]` Warning sign: early revenue looked like it came from ads, but less than half did. The rest came from organic fans buying because they liked him. Raising ad spend added almost no sales, and he ended up about $500K in debt. Check which channel actually produces closed revenue before you scale spend. ([2:32](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=29qOh8ZnF0k&t=152s))
- `[OFFER]` "Work with me, I'll help you" works for warm fans but means nothing to a stranger scrolling Instagram. A cold offer has to stand on its own without your personal brand. ([3:03](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=29qOh8ZnF0k&t=183s))
- `[ADS]` Ignore the "touchpoint count" definition of warm vs cold. Use Schwartz's awareness spectrum instead: unaware ("lost"), problem-aware, solution-aware, product-aware, most-aware. If you can sell to the unaware, you can sell to everyone further along. ([4:04](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=29qOh8ZnF0k&t=244s))
- `[SCALING-STAGES]` Most-aware fans are the easiest to sell (pick a price and launch), but that audience takes years to build and can't be doubled next month. Product-aware buyers come from referrals and word of mouth, and you can't control when they arrive. ([5:35](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=29qOh8ZnF0k&t=335s))
- `[ADS]` Solution-aware buyers are reached with search (Google, YouTube, ChatGPT, directories). It scales, but prospects have already spoken to two or three firms, compare you, and push on price. Ranking above five competitors doesn't help if they pick a sixth. ([6:36](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=29qOh8ZnF0k&t=396s))
- `[ADS]` Problem-aware traffic is where interruption ads (Meta) work. Name their pain in the feed and present yourself as the solution before they have looked at any competitor. There is less competition and fewer fixed expectations, so even an average offer can close quickly. ([8:06](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=29qOh8ZnF0k&t=486s))
- `[CREATIVE]` You can't sell a fix to the unaware because they don't know they have a problem, so sell the goal ("dream house") rather than escape from pain ("burning building"). Their ad: "How much better would your business be if a million people saw it?" They then frame the gap as not enough people seeing the business, offer the solution, and back it with proof (1,000+ companies served, 1,000 five-star reviews). ([10:09](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=29qOh8ZnF0k&t=609s))
- `[SALES-CALL]` The founder should run sales early. Log every objection, then change the offer so that objection never comes up, instead of just moving on to the next call. ([12:42](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=29qOh8ZnF0k&t=762s))
- `[PRICING]` For budget objections, change the terms rather than the price: payment plans, or half upfront with the rest in instalments. Same total, more closes. ([13:13](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=29qOh8ZnF0k&t=793s))
- `[OFFER]` "I've been burned by agencies" was answered by positioning as an anti-agency: they set the system up, hand it over, and help hire the in-house editor. They charge more upfront to cover early exits, and many clients choose to stay anyway once they see how much work it is. ([13:43](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=29qOh8ZnF0k&t=823s))
- `[CREATIVE]` Objections can become ad angles. "Ads don't work for me" became "get clients without paid ads", and they have spent several million dollars on that angle at strong conversion. ([14:44](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=29qOh8ZnF0k&t=884s))
- `[GUARANTEE]` The main cold-traffic objection is "sounds great if it works". Guarantee the specific outcome they want in writing (e.g. a million views) or they don't pay. Alternatives are pay-per-lead, profit share, or pay on delivery. Keep the conditions simple: pay on time and participate. ([15:15](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=29qOh8ZnF0k&t=915s))
- `[GUARANTEE]` For trust objections, add a second guarantee: a 7-day refundable deposit that gives access to the actual delivery team. This turns "I need to learn more before I buy" into "I buy to learn more". ([16:16](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=29qOh8ZnF0k&t=976s))
- `[OBJECTIONS]` For "I need to ask my spouse/partner", take a refundable deposit now and invite the decision-maker to the onboarding strategy call, where it's risk-free. ([16:46](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=29qOh8ZnF0k&t=1006s))
- `[OBJECTIONS]` Budget, time and authority objections from cold leads mostly mean "I wasn't looking for this, so I don't need it badly enough". When a prospect states exactly what must happen before they buy, stop and treat that as offer research. ([17:17](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=29qOh8ZnF0k&t=1037s))
- `[OFFER]` Grow the offer one objection at a time. Scripts only → added editing ("I need an editor first") → studio/equipment setup → creating social profiles → building websites. Each addition opened a colder audience and justified a higher price. ([18:19](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=29qOh8ZnF0k&t=1099s))
- `[FULFILLMENT]` Owning a large part of the client's critical infrastructure (organic, ads, website, automation, leads) makes them very sticky and keeps churn low. ([19:20](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=29qOh8ZnF0k&t=1160s))
- `[OFFER]` Back-end strategy: sell cold traffic only the narrow thing it asks for. Once inside, diagnose what they actually need. Don't pitch upsells to skeptical new clients because they will ask for refunds. Include extras for free, and after proving results (e.g. a 1M-view video) propose the next step (turn it into an ad) and charge for it. Back-end can earn 3–5× the front-end. ([20:20](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=29qOh8ZnF0k&t=1220s))
**Short quotes:**
- "Every part of your offer must overcome the objections you hear during the sale." ([12:42](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=29qOh8ZnF0k&t=762s))
- "From 'I can't buy, I need to learn more first' to 'if I want to learn more, I need to buy first.'" ([16:16](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=29qOh8ZnF0k&t=976s))
**G&L angle:**
- Check what share of closed G&L deals come from organic and referrals versus Meta. If paid looks weak, rebuild the cold offer around a goal rather than Bendik's personal brand.
- Unaware ad angles should sell the dream: "own a $2M business without your own capital", or for sellers "what would your exit be worth if a buyer paid full value?" Skip "fix your exit-readiness problem", which unaware owners don't think they have.
- Log every sales-call objection and change the offer to remove it. Examples: "I don't know how to find deals" → include deal sourcing. "I can't get finance" → include a lender/vendor-finance structuring service. "My spouse needs to agree" → refundable deposit plus a joint onboarding call.
- Add a 7-day refundable deposit and an outcome guarantee (e.g. LOI submitted or deal under contract within X months, conditional on participation).
- Keep the front end narrow (e.g. "buy your first business"). Include valuation, integration, and roll-up add-ons free at first, then charge once trust is earned.

## I'll invest in your business — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=4VoddZacLrM
**Gist:** Daniel and his partner pitch a growth-equity partnership to marketing agencies doing $3M–$30M/yr. They offer to plug in Viral Launch's ops, legal, tech, ads and sales infrastructure, with three conditions for who they will work with. The video is useful as a template for a partner-acquisition recruitment pitch.
**Nuggets:**
- `[PARTNERSHIP]` Target: online marketing agencies (SEO, LinkedIn, ads, cold email, organic), including competitors, at $3–30M/yr revenue with product-market fit but struggling with marketing, sales or logistics. ([0:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=4VoddZacLrM&t=0s))
- `[PARTNERSHIP]` Why they are doing it: the business now runs without them, so their spare time is the biggest lever left. They frame it as "the video we'd make if we sold the company", without selling. ([0:30](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=4VoddZacLrM&t=30s))
- `[PARTNERSHIP]` What they contribute: operations, logistics, technical and legal teams that scaled them past $4M/mo, plus private know-how (shared only under NDA) about the moves that took them from $500K debt to millions in profit. ([1:31](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=4VoddZacLrM&t=91s))
- `[SALES-HIRING]` Benchmark: their sales reps each bring in about $1M/mo for the company while earning $50K–$70K themselves. ([4:04](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=4VoddZacLrM&t=244s))
- `[ADS]` Scale benchmark: about $1M/mo on Facebook ads and over $20M spent on Meta in 3 years. That volume of tests is why they know which messages, funnel, application questions and VSL-or-not choices work. ([4:35](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=4VoddZacLrM&t=275s))
- `[TEAM]` "Borrow our talent": companies at $100K–$1M/mo can't attract top specialists, so the partner lends them until the company is big enough to hire its own. ([5:36](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=4VoddZacLrM&t=336s))
- `[PARTNERSHIP]` Condition 1: profit comes first. Owners who put mission first still won't lose a penny for it, and profit is what pays for purpose, staff and giving. ([6:06](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=4VoddZacLrM&t=366s))
- `[PARTNERSHIP]` Condition 2: the owner admits weaknesses and is willing to restructure processes and follow advice. If they think everything is under control, it's a no. ([8:09](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=4VoddZacLrM&t=489s))
- `[PARTNERSHIP]` Condition 3: a niche fit (marketing agencies only, not dentists or lawyers), because they know every KPI in that deal cycle. ([8:39](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=4VoddZacLrM&t=519s))
- `[APPLICATION]` CTA: a short application form, then the founders call personally, and they only engage if confident they can change the business. ([9:41](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=4VoddZacLrM&t=581s))
- `[MINDSET]` Closing frame: geniuses learn from other people's mistakes, so a partner shortcuts years of pain. ([10:42](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=4VoddZacLrM&t=642s))
**Short quotes:**
- "Profit comes before the goal. Profit comes before everything." ([6:06](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=4VoddZacLrM&t=366s))
- "The simple solution is to simply borrow our talent." ([5:36](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=4VoddZacLrM&t=336s))
**G&L angle:**
- Use this as a model for G&L's sell-side and partner-acquisition content. Use an explicit revenue band (e.g. $1–10M owners 55+), name the gaps you fix, and list the conditions for a fit.
- Explicit disqualifiers (open to change, profit-focused, in a niche you serve) make the content act as a filter before the application.
- For buy-side clients, "borrow our team" (legal, finance, ops/integration specialists) answers the "I can't run or close this alone" objection.
- Founder-call CTA after a short form: personal calls from Bendik on high-value seller leads.

## Top 5 mistakes when hitting $1M / month — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=XhqDVGpyXFw
**Gist:** Recorded during a $6M month, this covers five costly mistakes: not knowing revenue attribution, copying broke gurus, MRR billing, handing off sales too early, too many price options, and over-optimising (a cheaper-lead campaign that cost about $500K in a week).
**Nuggets:**
- `[KPIS]` Mistake 1: not attributing every sale to its source (organic vs paid, which platform, which ad, down to the specific video). Without that you can't steer revenue. Fixing it drove the $1M→$3M/mo jump in 30 days. ([0:30](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=XhqDVGpyXFw&t=30s))
- `[KPIS]` The volume-only "100 reps / 1,000 cold calls / a million emails" approach makes money you can't reproduce or double next month. ([1:31](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=XhqDVGpyXFw&t=91s))
- `[MINDSET]` Mistake 2: copying people who look rich. He modelled his offer, prices, pitch and sales process on online coaches who turned out to be broke (leased Lamborghinis). Verify who is actually profitable before learning from them. ([2:02](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=XhqDVGpyXFw&t=122s))
- `[PRICING]` Mistake 3: MRR. For agency or education services, take the largest possible upfront deposit, or a long-term position based on results, instead of a monthly subscription. ([3:03](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=XhqDVGpyXFw&t=183s))
- `[SALES-HIRING]` Mistake 4: outsourcing sales too early. About six months in, after hundreds of his own calls, he hired a $20K/mo sales manager and a team lead without experience in his product, then stepped out of sales entirely. ([4:34](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=XhqDVGpyXFw&t=274s))
- `[LEADERSHIP]` The founder must understand each department well enough to pick the right people to delegate to, and keep watching them rather than hoping. ([5:05](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=XhqDVGpyXFw&t=305s))
- `[PRICING]` Mistake 5: too many price options. They had three offers with five sub-tiers, which caused decision fatigue at close and led prospects to ask the rep "what do you think we should do?" ([5:35](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=XhqDVGpyXFw&t=335s))
- `[SALES-CALL]` The fix: one product at one price, and every call reaches the same conclusion. The rep acts as a consultant who tells the prospect what to do. Customisation happens after the sale, in delivery. Their only exception is in-house financing, which is priced slightly differently but pitched the same way. ([6:37](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=XhqDVGpyXFw&t=397s))
- `[SCALING-STAGES]` Over-optimising a working machine is dangerous. Once things run well, a bored founder tinkers with a precision tool and can break it for weeks. ([8:08](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=XhqDVGpyXFw&t=488s))
- `[FUNNEL]` Case: a new campaign cut cost per qualified call from about $400 to about $100. Volume quadrupled, but quality dropped, and there was too little pre-qualification, friction and disqualification. Calendars flooded, most no-showed, those who showed were unqualified, and a week's revenue was lost (about $500K+). ([9:39](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=XhqDVGpyXFw&t=579s))
- `[APPLICATION]` Before scaling into cheaper leads, make sure there is enough filtering infrastructure (qualifying questions, friction, disqualification) to protect the sales team's calendar. ([10:10](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=XhqDVGpyXFw&t=610s))
**Short quotes:**
- "If you are a sales representative, you have to tell them what they should do." ([6:37](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=XhqDVGpyXFw&t=397s))
- "You can over-optimize something, break something, and then it takes weeks to fix it." ([9:39](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=XhqDVGpyXFw&t=579s))
**G&L angle:**
- Tag every G&L deal with source, ad ID and creative in the CRM from day one.
- Present one buy-side program at one price and one sell-side program at one price. Reps prescribe the program and tailor it after the sale. Financing or payment plans are the only variation.
- Bendik should stay on calls until the script and closing rates are proven, and hire sales leaders who have sold high-ticket advisory or financial services.
- If a new ad cuts cost per call sharply, tighten the application first (liquidity, credit, timeline, owner revenue or EBITDA for sellers) so cheap, unqualified "no money down" leads don't flood the calendar.
- Be wary of chasing lookalike "acquisition gurus". Check who is actually closing deals before copying their offer.

## agencies suck — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=AGR4eedP0ms
**Gist:** Daniel runs a multi-million/month agency but argues most marketing agencies are a bad idea, because they add a middleman to the function everything else depends on. Hire an agency to learn what good looks like and have them set up the infrastructure, then bring the work in-house.
**Nuggets:**
- `[HIRING]` An agency hires an employee and resells that person's work at a markup. For marketing, the function at the top of the funnel, adding a middleman is risky: if leads stop, sales and fulfillment stop too ([0:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=AGR4eedP0ms&t=0s)).
- `[HIRING]` Full-time employees are always better on cost because you don't pay the middleman's cut ([1:31](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=AGR4eedP0ms&t=91s)).
- `[HIRING]` What an agency is good for: access to talent you can't find or recruit yourself, and its knowledge of best practice across hundreds of clients ([2:02](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=AGR4eedP0ms&t=122s)).
- `[OPERATIONS]` Hire an agency to learn its systems and see what success looks like, have it build infrastructure you can adopt, then let it go and run the work in-house more cheaply. Daniel's own agency pitches "hire us, then fire us" as one of its strongest offers ([2:33](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=AGR4eedP0ms&t=153s)).
- `[HIRING]` Expect to go through one or two bad agencies before finding a good one. That is still easier than screening 100 employees, which is the work the agency has already done ([3:03](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=AGR4eedP0ms&t=183s)).
**Short quotes:**
- "you should hire this agency so that they understand all the best practices... so that you can then fire them" ([2:02](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=AGR4eedP0ms&t=122s))
**G&L angle:**
- If G&L uses a media-buying or funnel agency, set it up as a time-boxed build: in-house staff shadow the agency, document the SOPs, and take over ads and setters within a set period.
- Treat lead generation as core in-house capability, since every closed deal depends on it.

## Building A Business With My Wife: Q&A — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=b_j_83cglBA
**Gist:** Daniel and his wife and co-founder Samantha (the CEO) answer viewer questions. Topics: running ads on $100/day, their three-person leadership structure, the cost of a founder leaving sales too early, sales as the deciding factor in funnel economics, what makes a top performer, copying competitors over theorising about niches, and mixing marriage with business. About 80% of their revenue comes from ads.
**Nuggets:**
- `[ADS]` On a $100/day budget, open the Meta Ad Library, find your largest competitors' longest-running ads (six months running probably means profitable), make about 10 close versions and run them. That skips the testing phase ([0:34](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=b_j_83cglBA&t=34s)).
- `[CREATIVE]` Daniel's benchmark: with fewer than 100 creatives in the library "you are essentially doing nothing". You need at least about 100 to compete ([1:04](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=b_j_83cglBA&t=64s)).
- `[TEAM]` Three-part leadership: one head of acquisition (marketing/sales), one head of retention (account management/ops), and Samantha as CEO over both. Everyone else reports up through those three ([1:35](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=b_j_83cglBA&t=95s)).
- `[SALES-CALL]` Daniel stopped taking sales calls at $250k/month and calls it a mistake: founders should keep selling until at least $500k/month. Founder calls are the easiest sales and the fastest way to learn the customer and the gaps in the process ([2:06](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=b_j_83cglBA&t=126s)).
- `[CREATIVE]` He still listens to sales calls for marketing insight. Their most important copywriter came from the sales team after thousands of calls taught him why people hesitate to buy ([2:38](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=b_j_83cglBA&t=158s)).
- `[SALES-MGMT]` Sales decides whether the business works. Clients with the same ads, tracking and creative get very different cost per result because of how the offer is presented and how the sales process runs. The CEO must be directly involved in sales ([8:46](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=b_j_83cglBA&t=526s)).
- `[KPIS]` Funnel KPIs at each stage and ROAS are fairly predictable by industry (doctors, lawyers, accountants). Performance at the bottom of the funnel (closing and CPA) decides everything ([10:18](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=b_j_83cglBA&t=618s)).
- `[PRICING]` Pricing is relative as well as absolute: know whether you're the highest or lowest in your market. Raising price has to be balanced against keeping close rate healthy, which is a funnel-economics question ([8:15](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=b_j_83cglBA&t=495s)).
- `[HIRING]` Top performers put in the most hours and volume: the best rep takes the most calls and the best AM manages the most accounts. More reps produce more data, which makes them better ([11:19](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=b_j_83cglBA&t=679s)).
- `[HIRING]` Outstanding vs exceptional: outstanding people do the job description perfectly. Exceptional people see the whole business and spot problems and bottlenecks outside their role ([13:54](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=b_j_83cglBA&t=834s)).
- `[OFFER]` Copy competitors doing $1M, $5M or $10M/month a few steps ahead of you rather than trusting your own view on niche vs broad. They started with coaches/consultants/course creators, found it saturated after 6–8 months, and grew once they targeted all business owners ([19:40](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=b_j_83cglBA&t=1180s)).
- `[ADS]` 80/20: about 80% of sales come from paid ads (over $1M/month spend) because ads scale and can be profitable immediately ([21:47](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=b_j_83cglBA&t=1307s)).
- `[OTHER]` Before the business, Daniel bought off-market Alaska real estate with creative financing and a few thousand down. Deal flow capped him at about five deals a year. Within 90 days of starting social media he was earning more than from property, and he sold the portfolio over 3–4 years for long-term capital gains ([5:10](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=b_j_83cglBA&t=310s)).
- `[LIFE]` On working with a spouse: be professional in the office, but don't try to separate work and life. A shared goal holds the relationship together. If you need separation, choose a partner who doesn't ([23:20](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=b_j_83cglBA&t=1400s)).
**Short quotes:**
- "I think it would be best not to step away from sales until you achieve at least $500,000 in monthly sales." ([2:06](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=b_j_83cglBA&t=126s))
- "If you don't have more than 100 ad creatives in your ad library, you are essentially doing nothing." ([1:04](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=b_j_83cglBA&t=64s))
**G&L angle:**
- G&L founders should keep taking calls until about $500k/month and feed objections and phrasing from those calls straight into ads and VSL copy. A setter or closer with real call experience may become the best copywriter.
- Search the Meta Ad Library for long-running ads from business-acquisition and exit-planning competitors (acquisition coaches, brokers, exit planners), adapt the angles, and build toward 100+ creatives.
- Daniel's own story (creative-finance real estate with a few thousand down) is close to G&L's vendor-finance pitch. Deal flow is the cap, so build systems for sourcing off-market businesses at volume.
- Set up leadership as acquisition (ads + sales), retention (deal fulfillment/advisory delivery), and a CEO over both.

## VSL's are dead — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=AATxUdXv4tI
**Gist:** Daniel's company did $4M/month selling B2B services through a sales-call funnel while removing the VSL from most pages in favour of a short text landing page. A VSL exists to fill a trust gap for offers whose outcome is conditional (coaching), and buyers now recognise the format as a sign of exactly that. The real fix was making the offer clear enough that it sells without personal trust in the founder.
**Nuggets:**
- `[VSL]` A VSL's job is to borrow trust from a founder/CEO for a product whose outcome is unclear or conditional. High-trust purchases with clear outcomes (realtor, accountant, lawyer, a $50k car) sell without one ([0:30](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=AATxUdXv4tI&t=30s)).
- `[VSL]` Buyers have now seen so many VSLs that the format itself tells them "this product needs a pitch to make sense". It works as a negative signal and builds less trust than it used to ([2:32](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=AATxUdXv4tI&t=152s)).
- `[VSL]` VSLs still work, and some companies do $10M+/month with them. Daniel's point is that you may do better without one, so test it with your own money ([3:03](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=AATxUdXv4tI&t=183s)).
- `[LANDING-PAGE]` They took the VSL off most pages and replaced it with a landing page of short text explaining what they do. Daniel still appears on some pages as company spokesperson ([3:33](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=AATxUdXv4tI&t=213s)).
- `[CREATIVE]` Ad-to-page consistency matters. A founder-on-camera ad flows well into a founder VSL. A text-on-screen ad or a UGC-creator ad that drops people onto a VSL of an unknown founder converts worse, because the viewer suddenly has to watch a video from someone they don't know ([3:33](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=AATxUdXv4tI&t=213s)).
- `[UGC]` With many UGC creators producing ads, sending all of that traffic to one founder VSL adds inconsistency. A neutral text landing page fits every creator's ad ([4:04](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=AATxUdXv4tI&t=244s)).
- `[OFFER]` If your marketing depends on a VSL, the real problem is an offer that can't be described simply. Fix the product and offer so it makes sense to people who don't already trust you ([4:35](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=AATxUdXv4tI&t=275s)).
- `[OFFER]` The $4M/month didn't come from the funnel tweak or the roughly 20% conversion lift. It came from fixing the offer so they can work like a car dealership: people may dislike the sales process and still spend $50k happily because the product is good ([5:05](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=AATxUdXv4tI&t=305s)).
**Short quotes:**
- "People created VSLs to fill this trust gap" ([2:32](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=AATxUdXv4tI&t=152s))
- "fixing the product, fixing the offer and making it make sense to people who don't trust us inherently" ([5:35](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=AATxUdXv4tI&t=335s))
**G&L angle:**
- Make the deal easy to explain in a line or two, e.g. "we help you buy a profitable business using seller finance" or "we prepare your business for sale and run the sale", so the landing page sells without a 20-minute VSL.
- A/B test a short text landing page against the VSL, and split results by ad type (founder-on-camera vs UGC/text). Send founder ads to the VSL and creator/text ads to the text page.
- M&A advisory sits closer to the realtor/accountant category (a known professional service) than to coaching. Lean on that: show deal structure, fees and process clearly instead of persuading with a long video.

## How I scaled to $1m/mo in adspend and 1,000 active ads (content flywheel) — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=xvOnEA48xZY
**Gist:** Daniel says most ad accounts fail for lack of creative diversity and volume: 10–30 near-identical ads filmed on the same day. His "content continuum" takes one idea through every format (short text, long text, looping on-screen-text video, short talking head, long case study, podcast clips, training), then multiplies the winners with a dozen UGC creators reading the same script. Ideas are tested organically first and only winners get paid spend.
**Nuggets:**
- `[CREATIVE]` Failing accounts have 10–30 ads in the same format and message with slightly different hooks, often filmed the same day by the same person. Meta needs variety because buyers differ in awareness, objections and buying stage. He says creative diversity and volume can halve ad costs ([0:30](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=xvOnEA48xZY&t=30s)).
- `[CREATIVE]` The content continuum runs short text → long text → short looping video with on-screen text → short talking head → long talking head/case study → podcast → training. Start wherever you're strong and push each piece into 2–3 other formats. Example chain: YouTube video → email/LinkedIn long text → short text → short video ([1:33](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=xvOnEA48xZY&t=93s)).
- `[CREATIVE]` Short formats are quick to make but build less trust and face more competition. Long formats build trust but cost more. Further along the continuum, volume and competition both drop ([2:35](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=xvOnEA48xZY&t=155s)).
- `[CREATIVE]` Quick start: write your offer in one sentence in the phone notes app, screenshot it and run it as a 9x16 ad. You can make about 10 in five minutes. Your website headline and FAQs are ready-made ad copy too ([3:07](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=xvOnEA48xZY&t=187s)).
- `[CREATIVE]` Longer on-screen text is harder for competitors to copy and can take the reader further along the buying journey within one ad ([4:10](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=xvOnEA48xZY&t=250s)).
- `[CREATIVE]` Long text: add proof to the one-line headline. Paste winning emails into a notes-app screenshot. For very long text, put a hook on the image and tell people to read the caption. Split paragraphs into a carousel ([4:41](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=xvOnEA48xZY&t=281s)).
- `[CREATIVE]` Image-only ads are very crowded. An advertiser who runs only statics probably won't be profitable now ([5:13](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=xvOnEA48xZY&t=313s)).
- `[CREATIVE]` A 5-second looping video of real footage (you walking, working, on stage, or a customer) with the text on screen. One of his best ads is someone walking down the street with text over it and no production budget. Walls of text over stock footage fail, which he says he has tested hundreds of times ([5:44](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=xvOnEA48xZY&t=344s)).
- `[CREATIVE]` Short talking head: load your best email or post into a teleprompter app and read it as a 15–30 second script. Most competitors avoid the camera, so the competition is thinner and trust higher than with statics ([6:15](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=xvOnEA48xZY&t=375s)).
- `[CASE-STUDIES]` The best long-form ad is a case study walkthrough of 5–20 minutes: who the client was before, the problem, what changed, the result. Even better if the client confirms it on camera. His plumber "Jared" case study (he turned off $60k/month in ad spend and earned the same through organic) has had over $1M of ad spend and was turned into emails and LinkedIn posts ([7:46](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=xvOnEA48xZY&t=466s)).
- `[CREATIVE]` A podcast feels more authentic because the viewer watches a conversation rather than a pitch. Don't run full podcasts as ads; cut clips of you talking naturally about the offer. They don't look like ads, Meta rewards that with cheap placements, and ROI is higher ([9:17](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=xvOnEA48xZY&t=557s)).
- `[ORGANIC]` Long training content (he made courses of 2 hours and 1.5 hours) converts audiences short-form never reaches, but only make it once short-form clips on the same topics have proven demand. His had millions of views ([10:50](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=xvOnEA48xZY&t=650s)).
- `[ORGANIC]` Test ideas organically for free first. A bad hook fails organically too, so you don't need to spend hundreds on ad tests. Most of his most profitable ads started as top organic posts; he took the hook and body, added a CTA and ran it ([11:51](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=xvOnEA48xZY&t=711s)).
- `[ADS]` Example ad hook: "a million views guaranteed or you don't pay; click to see if you qualify". The longer version adds proof: 3,000+ clients across 250 industries, an average of 15.5M views after three months, and 1M+ leads in two years ([13:25](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=xvOnEA48xZY&t=805s)).
- `[OFFER]` Four-step mechanism used in long-form copy: 1) identify the bottleneck, 2) build the marketing system, 3) launch, test and adjust in the first weeks, 4) guarantee growth with a full written refund ([14:57](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=xvOnEA48xZY&t=897s)).
- `[GUARANTEE]` Guarantee: "if we can't get you a million views, you don't pay", backed by a full written refund. The CTA is to apply on the next page and see if you qualify, after which the team calls ([15:28](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=xvOnEA48xZY&t=928s)).
- `[CREATIVE]` Scale a proven winner by moving it through every format first (static, text-only, short video, carousel, long). That gives Meta many assets for different consumption habits, and he went from one winner to dozens in each format ([16:29](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=xvOnEA48xZY&t=989s)).
- `[UGC]` Then hire about 12 UGC creators with different faces and energy to read the same winning scripts, going from 10–20 variations to hundreds. A 45-year-old plumber and a 25-year-old entrepreneur respond to different presenters of the same offer ([17:31](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=xvOnEA48xZY&t=1051s)).
- `[ADS]` This is how they publish hundreds of ads a week: one proven concept in many formats, with many people, backgrounds and filming styles (FaceTime-style vs studio). Meta can then show a prospect a different version each time until they convert ([19:03](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=xvOnEA48xZY&t=1143s)).
- `[CREATIVE]` Mine your existing sales assets (headline, emails, sales-call pitch) for angles that already convert. Testing protocol in Meta is a separate topic ([20:05](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=xvOnEA48xZY&t=1205s)).
**Short quotes:**
- "Find out what works for free, then invest money in it and let Facebook do the rest." ([12:23](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=xvOnEA48xZY&t=743s))
- "It's the same winner multiplied by 100. And that's the whole game." ([19:03](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=xvOnEA48xZY&t=1143s))
**G&L angle:**
- Take G&L's core one-liners ("Buy a profitable business with little or no money down", "Retiring in 5 years? Find out what your business is really worth") into notes-app statics, looping walking videos with on-screen text, and short teleprompter talking heads.
- Make long-form case-study ads from real deals: the buyer's background, the target business, the deal structure (price, deposit, vendor-finance terms), and the outcome, with the client confirming on a Zoom clip.
- Hire UGC creators who look like each audience: 30–45-year-old corporate professionals wanting to buy, and 55–70-year-old owners wanting to exit. They respond to different faces.
- Post angles organically on LinkedIn, YouTube and Shorts first, then promote the winners with a "see if you qualify" CTA.

## I booked 100,000 sales calls. Here's what works in 2026 — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MPhvqxVGzkw
**Gist:** Daniel's end-to-end system for selling high-ticket services to cold traffic: a pre-call contact system that also scores intent, setter "speed to lead" BANT pre-qualification (and the trap of over-qualifying), different openings, discovery and pitch styles for B2B vs B2C, the six-question discovery flowchart, a four-option payment ladder presented one option at a time, instant onboarding after payment, objection handling for each BANT category with the 7-day money-back guarantee as the universal closer, and short- and long-term follow-up (some reps close half their deals in follow-up).
**Nuggets:**
- `[SALES-CALL]` The system is built for cold traffic. Selling to strangers is what separates big businesses from warm-audience-only sellers. In year one Daniel could book calls but couldn't close, and survived only on volume ([0:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MPhvqxVGzkw&t=0s)).
- `[SETTING]` Within 5 minutes of booking, reach the prospect on three channels (SMS, email and a real phone call) and send the meeting link plus pre-call material. Reminders: email 24h before, SMS 4h before (with link), email 1h, SMS 30 minutes, final SMS at 5 minutes saying "the meeting has started" ([2:33](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MPhvqxVGzkw&t=153s)).
- `[KPIS]` Engagement with the pre-call sequence predicts quality (data from 1,000+ closes): opened no email and answered no text gives a 3% chance of showing and being qualified; opened email but no reply, 14%; opened and replied to the rep's text, 42% ([3:36](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MPhvqxVGzkw&t=216s)).
- `[SALES-MGMT]` Use engagement to protect top reps' time. Move non-engaging prospects to juniors or setters, chase them until they confirm, or cancel likely fake bookings. Reps should reply to engaged leads with thanks and a screenshot of their website to show they've done their homework ([4:38](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MPhvqxVGzkw&t=278s)).
- `[SETTING]` Setters are "glorified receptionists" doing speed to lead. They call within minutes of booking to check qualification, offer an earlier slot, gather notes for the closer, and send relevant case studies, testimonials, YouTube videos or a deck to build excitement ([7:12](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MPhvqxVGzkw&t=432s)).
- `[SETTING]` A soft opener so busy owners don't feel spammed: "Hi, I just got your application, took a quick look at your business, I really like what you do, I had a couple of questions so we're better prepared" ([7:42](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MPhvqxVGzkw&t=462s)).
- `[APPLICATION]` The setter double-checks application answers. A "$1M monthly revenue" applicant turned out to be a kid in his mother's basement quoting potential revenue ([9:14](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MPhvqxVGzkw&t=554s)).
- `[SETTING]` Setter BANT order on a 5–15 minute call. Authority: "do you run it yourself or have a team?". Need: "what's not working / what's the strategy?", moving from goal answers to quantified problems. Budget, indirectly: "how do you get leads? Cost per lead? How many a month?" (so about $10k/month spend). Timing: "if this goes well, when would you implement?" ([10:17](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MPhvqxVGzkw&t=617s)).
- `[SETTING]` Over-qualification trap: 80% of 10-minute setter calls ended on a budget objection, but budget came up in only 20% of 60-minute closer calls. Asking "can you afford our minimum package?" before showing value burned leads, and closers were left with near-certain deals that earned easy commissions. Check that objection frequency on setter calls matches closer calls ([13:20](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MPhvqxVGzkw&t=800s)).
- `[SALES-HIRING]` Setting needs real skill, and the higher the ticket, the more skill. Done badly, it burns the lead before the closer ever speaks to them ([14:51](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MPhvqxVGzkw&t=891s)).
- `[SALES-CALL]` The closer's job is to finish what the system started: moving an interested but undecided prospect off the fence. Adapt to B2B (ROI-driven owners who've sat through many sales calls) vs B2C (emotional, personal outcomes like fitness, relationships, pain) within the first seconds by matching energy, pace and directness ([16:24](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MPhvqxVGzkw&t=984s)).
- `[SALES-CALL]` B2B opening: go straight in and show you've done your homework: "I've analysed your business, I know where you are, I'm confident we can help, you're in the right place". Set the agenda, including that a decision will be made at the end. If they resist, name it openly ("this is a sales call") and either take control or let them leave and save an hour ([18:29](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MPhvqxVGzkw&t=1109s)).
- `[SALES-CALL]` First discovery question: "Give me an idea of what prompted you to reach out?". Keep it broad; narrow questions limit the answers you get ([20:01](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MPhvqxVGzkw&t=1201s)).
- `[SALES-CALL]` B2C opening: warm, curious, trusted-advisor framing. Let them talk, and set the expectation of a decision gently: "learn where you are, share what we do, if it fits talk next steps". Read the temperature and switch modes if a business owner turns personal and vulnerable ([20:32](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MPhvqxVGzkw&t=1232s)).
- `[OBJECTIONS]` Discovery is where calls are won or lost. Every question is advance objection handling that collects "ammunition" you can quote back at the close, so they can't say "we're not starting till next year, send it to my CMO" in the last 30 seconds ([23:36](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MPhvqxVGzkw&t=1416s)).
- `[SALES-CALL]` B2B discovery takes 5–10 minutes (confirm what you already know, because owners want to get to the point). B2C discovery takes 30–45 minutes because it builds the trust and emotional clarity the buyer needs. Rushing B2C discovery is a top reason deals are lost: "like proposing on the first date" ([25:09](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MPhvqxVGzkw&t=1509s)).
- `[SALES-CALL]` You won't get through every branch of the flowchart in an hour, and a 3-hour discovery would exhaust the prospect. Skill is choosing which objections to dig into, which to skim and which to skip ([28:44](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MPhvqxVGzkw&t=1724s)).
- `[OBJECTIONS]` Authority branch: "just me" is an instinctive answer, so push ("no one else helps with marketing? an assistant, a COO, a spouse on weekends?"). If that person should be on the call, disqualify and reschedule; if not, make them explain why so it can't come up at the close. "No time" leads to "can we hire someone to do the heavy lifting?". If they can contribute no time at all, disqualify ([30:16](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MPhvqxVGzkw&t=1816s)).
- `[SALES-CALL]` For prospects starting something new: "how do you get clients now? Why not double that?". Then "why now? What blocked you before?". Good answers: freed-up time, a newly discovered constraint, saw the testimonials. "Just found you" means pressing further, including a disqualifying challenge (about 5 hours/month of your time, and it's expensive; worth it?) ([33:21](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MPhvqxVGzkw&t=2001s)).
- `[SALES-CALL]` "What's the strategy so far?" then "how's that working?". Whether it's working or not, the follow-up is the same: "what makes you look for help now?". Raise the resource and time requirement early rather than 40 minutes in ([36:28](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MPhvqxVGzkw&t=2188s)).
- `[SALES-CALL]` Dream outcome: "Who in your industry is crushing it?". Quantify it (earnings, followers, eight or nine figures in the bank, how they feel in the morning) and make the gap as large and concrete as possible ("go to the moon, baby"), so the price looks like an international flight, not a walk ([38:32](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MPhvqxVGzkw&t=2312s)).
- `[OBJECTIONS]` Budget handled indirectly through lead sources: paid ads ("why not double the spend?"), referrals ("you have a traffic problem, not a business problem"), small ambitions ("is that phase one? What about in a year?"), ROAS ("why is it low? What if nothing changes in 6 months?"), plan B ("cold email? Then go do that"; the push-away brings out the real reason) ([42:39](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MPhvqxVGzkw&t=2559s)).
- `[OBJECTIONS]` Timing: reject a reflexive "immediately" ("why now? What blocked you last week, last month, last year?"). For "next week" or "on holiday": "if we handled it while you're away, would you start now?", or rebook for when they're back. Window shoppers: "if you found exactly that, would you buy right away?". Specific criteria (e.g. "5 case studies from US doctors") are easy wins ([49:50](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MPhvqxVGzkw&t=2990s)).
- `[OBJECTIONS]` "It depends on cost": the cheapest investment rarely gives the best return, and value is judged through testimonials, reviews and guarantees; "what's the most valuable thing you hope to get?". Any answer leads to "start with the 7-day money-back guarantee"; no answer is a hard disqualification ([56:01](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MPhvqxVGzkw&t=3361s)).
- `[SALES-CALL]` B2B pitch: don't save proof for a big reveal at the end. Owners will ask "just tell me the price". Weave case studies in the moment a problem is mentioned, and be ready to discuss price early. Being transparent about what's included and what it costs builds trust faster than perfect discovery ([57:04](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MPhvqxVGzkw&t=3424s)).
- `[SALES-CALL]` B2C pitch comes second to last, after they feel fully understood. Every benefit should map to a problem they stated in their own words, with no extra information. "Oven, not microwave" ([59:40](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MPhvqxVGzkw&t=3580s)).
- `[CASE-STUDIES]` Build a proof library and match case studies to the specific problem, not just the industry. Their plumbing-consultant client (hundreds of millions of views, 100k+ followers) gives four different stories: reach, time saved (40 hours/month down to 4), replacing $60k/month of ads with organic, and revenue/backend. Know your deck like a Vegas dealer ([1:02:15](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MPhvqxVGzkw&t=3735s)).
- `[SALES-CALL]` B2C close: "Do you think what we discussed is the solution to get you XYZ?". Let them confirm, then state the price in the same conversational tone and don't switch into presenter mode. B2B close: state the price plainly without softening it, treat it as a challenge to the owner ("you've wanted the price all call, here it is"), and hold them to the decisiveness they claim ([1:04:20](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MPhvqxVGzkw&t=3860s)).
- `[OBJECTIONS]` Stalling owners: "I'm sure you don't take these calls to window shop; your time is valuable and so is mine". Reps show their fully booked week: "every other owner on my calendar shows up ready to decide" ([1:06:56](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MPhvqxVGzkw&t=4016s)).
- `[PRICING]` Payment ladder: (1) pay in full by wire at 15% off (saves card fees and admin); (2) half now, half in 1–4 weeks at 5% off, a decoy that makes paying in full look better; (3) first month as deposit, then weekly payments from day 28, with a setup fee and in-house financing; (4) third-party financing over a year or more. Present one option at a time, starting with paid-in-full, and only move down after each is declined, to avoid decision fatigue ([1:08:29](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MPhvqxVGzkw&t=4109s)).
- `[SALES-CALL]` Collect payment and contracts on the call. More deals are lost to "I'll pay right after the call" than are won in follow-up ([1:12:36](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MPhvqxVGzkw&t=4356s)).
- `[FULFILLMENT]` After payment, thank them and give immediate access: account manager emails, a Slack connection, an onboarding meeting already on their calendar, and the first discovery or intake questions. For products, confirm shipping. For events, take a shirt size. For digital, make sure they're logged in. Christmas-tree analogy: people pay for speed ([1:13:07](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MPhvqxVGzkw&t=4387s)).
- `[OBJECTIONS]` Every objection at the close points to something missed in discovery (a forgotten marketing assistant, a month-long holiday). All objections reduce to budget, authority, need or timing ([1:15:40](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MPhvqxVGzkw&t=4540s)).
- `[OBJECTIONS]` Budget: usually doubt about value, not money ("if you sold them a mansion for $1k they'd find the money"). Isolate it: "money aside, do you think this would work for you? Why?". If it's a cash constraint, offer the payment ladder ("how much can you put down today?"). If it's doubt about value, use the 7-day guarantee: "you can't know the value from the outside; I'm so confident our team would work free for 7 days" ([1:16:41](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MPhvqxVGzkw&t=4601s)).
- `[GUARANTEE]` The 7-day guarantee only works if your first 7 days of delivery are excellent. Otherwise you'll do a lot of free work and refunds ([1:20:16](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MPhvqxVGzkw&t=4816s)).
- `[OBJECTIONS]` "I need to ask my partner" is usually fear and wanting someone else to blame. "If your partner were fully on board, is this the right decision for you? Why?" or "if they said no but you needed it, what would you do?". Otherwise, start under the guarantee and bring the partner to the onboarding or strategy call so the work persuades them ([1:20:47](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MPhvqxVGzkw&t=4847s)).
- `[OBJECTIONS]` "Not sure I need it" means the gap is too small. You can't move the destination after the pitch, but you can move the starting point: "maybe stay where you are for a couple more years?". Then use the guarantee ([1:21:49](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MPhvqxVGzkw&t=4909s)).
- `[OBJECTIONS]` Timing: separate logistics (holiday: "anything else stopping you? We'll extend your programme by those two weeks and start prep now") from lack of urgency, where you put a number on the real cost of delay (money wasted daily, 5% a year property appreciation) or the opportunity cost. The guarantee clock starts when they're ready, with a deposit to reserve a spot ([1:23:51](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MPhvqxVGzkw&t=5031s)).
- `[SALES-CALL]` Make the risk-free trial the default for undecided prospects: a deposit lets them "think about it for another week" inside the programme. Not deciding should look like turning down a free trial ([1:27:26](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MPhvqxVGzkw&t=5246s)).
- `[SALES-MGMT]` Follow-up: some reps close up to half their deals there. Short-term (about a month): the rep sends material plus personalised calls, texts and emails tied to the objection ("how did the board meeting go?", "we've expanded financing"). Any sign of interest gets an immediate phone call, never an email thread ([1:28:28](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MPhvqxVGzkw&t=5308s)).
- `[SALES-HIRING]` Long-term follow-up: after a month, SDRs call every paid-for lead that didn't convert. It's a training ground for new reps on lower-stakes, already-warmed leads with CRM notes, alongside automated SMS and email drips and social retargeting ([1:30:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MPhvqxVGzkw&t=5400s)).
**Short quotes:**
- "every mistake that You commit at the end of the call will leave You've revealed a mistake you made earlier in the call" ([1:16:10](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MPhvqxVGzkw&t=4570s))
- "you can put the best sales representative of the world in a broken system and yet is going to lose deals" ([2:03](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MPhvqxVGzkw&t=123s))
**G&L angle:**
- G&L straddles both modes. Sellers aged 55+ are B2B-ish, but the decision is deeply personal and emotional (legacy, retirement, family), so use B2C-style long discovery (30–45 minutes). Corporate-professional buyers buying their first business are emotional, identity-driven buyers too. Don't rush discovery.
- Copy the pre-call engagement scoring (3%/14%/42%) and the 3-channel/5-minute contact. Watch for setter over-qualification: don't ask "can you afford $X?" on 10-minute calls.
- Payment ladder for G&L programmes: paid in full at 15% off, half now and half after the first deal review at 5% off, deposit plus monthly, and third-party financing. Present them one at a time.
- Authority is critical: spouse, business partner or co-owner, and for sellers often an accountant or adult children. Get them on the call or make the prospect explain why not.
- Build a G&L case-study library tagged by problem (no capital, no deal flow, financing rejected, owner wants to retire but no successor, low valuation) so closers can match proof to the exact pain. Consider a "first 7–14 days" guarantee only if onboarding (deal-sourcing portal, first deal reviews) delivers value immediately.

## The Complete 2026 Meta Ads Blueprint COURSE — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eEiyrvM1nB8
**Gist:** A full Meta ads course: the offer must work on cold traffic, creative is ~90% of the game because Meta prioritises user experience, and Andromeda "contextual targeting" means the words in your script do the targeting. Covers a 4-part ad script (hook, mechanism, proof, CTA), angles vs variations, simple account structure, KPI math from LTV, testing/scaling rules, the ad-ID social-proof trick and ban-avoidance compliance.
**Nuggets:**
- `[OFFER]` When ads fail the real problem is almost always the offer: what converts warm referrals rarely converts cold strangers. Fastest fix is to model an offer that's already scaling in your industry — "originality on cold traffic is extremely expensive"; stop endlessly "refining the offer." ([1:32](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eEiyrvM1nB8&t=92s))
- `[ADS]` Meta's priority order: (1) user experience, (2) its own ad revenue (98% of revenue), (3) advertiser success. Ads people actually want to watch get cheaper CPCs/CPLs and more reach; clickbait, lazy AI images and poor edits are punished with higher costs. ([3:04](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eEiyrvM1nB8&t=184s))
- `[CREATIVE]` Put ~90% of focus on the creative (the uploaded video/image), not targeting, colours or endless split tests. Make the ad worth watching even if people knew it was an ad (Super Bowl-ad standard); ads that look like content get platform help, ads that look like ads fight the platform. ([6:39](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eEiyrvM1nB8&t=399s))
- `[CREATIVE]` A cold stranger must finish the ad knowing exactly what you do, how you help them, and a reason to believe you. The ad should be among the highest-quality content the company produces — it is the first touchpoint before VSL, funnel, emails. ([9:12](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eEiyrvM1nB8&t=552s))
- `[ORGANIC]` Test hooks, topics and formats organically first (free); their most profitable ads started as organic posts that outperformed. Build a free creative library, then put spend behind proven winners. ([9:43](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eEiyrvM1nB8&t=583s))
- `[ADS]` Use the Meta Ads Library: search competitors by Facebook page name, study ads that have been running for months (long run time = proven winner with big spend behind it) — headline, copy, video, landing page. Save links and remake them in your own style. ([10:43](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eEiyrvM1nB8&t=643s))
- `[ADS]` Andromeda = contextual targeting: Meta's AI picks the audience per ad from the words, graphics and context in it. Mentioning names like Hormozi/Grant Cardone/Gary V in the script reaches people who follow them; legacy interest targeting is outdated. Video beats image partly because it carries more contextual signal. ([14:22](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eEiyrvM1nB8&t=862s))
- `[CREATIVE]` Use your customers' slang: swapping "home service business owner with under five employees" for "one-truck Chuck" cut cost per lead ~20%. Vague ads give Meta too broad a pool. ([15:57](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eEiyrvM1nB8&t=957s))
- `[ADS]` Don't build 50 ad sets of demographic splits; if performance is bad it's the creative (CPL too high) or the offer (lead-to-sale too low). Manual control in 2026 is on budget, not audiences. ([16:58](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eEiyrvM1nB8&t=1018s))
- `[CREATIVE]` Write your own scripts — Hormozi told them he writes every piece of acquisition.com copy himself. AI/copywriters can help brainstorm but can't build founder trust. ([17:59](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eEiyrvM1nB8&t=1079s))
- `[CREATIVE]` Script framework part 1 — Hook: call out a behaviour, not a demographic (e.g. "If you're struggling to write, film, edit and post all your content by yourself, here's exactly what we do"). Relatable beats polished. ([19:32](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eEiyrvM1nB8&t=1172s))
- `[CREATIVE]` Part 2 — Mechanism: a clear result plus *how* you deliver it. The #1 cold-traffic sales-call objection is "I can't picture how this works for me." Use the "First, Next, Then" 3-step narrative so the prospect visualises the process. ([21:04](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eEiyrvM1nB8&t=1264s))
- `[CREATIVE]` Part 3 — Proof: keep it tight, 1–2 specific believable results with names and outcomes ("Jack and Lisa each dropped three pant sizes in 90 days"); save deep proof for VSL, landing page and sales call. ([23:07](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eEiyrvM1nB8&t=1387s))
- `[CREATIVE]` Part 4 — One CTA only, e.g. "Click below and I'll show you how to get started" / "If this sounds even remotely interesting, I'll show you exactly how it works. Just click the button." Then audit each script for all four elements. ([24:09](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eEiyrvM1nB8&t=1449s))
- `[ADS]` Headline/primary copy matters little for video ads — they've reused 1–2 headlines/body copies for ~$5M of spend; copy tests were never statistically significant. Spend ~95% effort on video, 5% on copy (images are the exception and need stronger copy; videos outlast images before fatigue). ([25:10](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eEiyrvM1nB8&t=1510s))
- `[ADS]` Primary copy structure: short, one sentence per paragraph, no more than 1–2 scrolls. State the problem exactly as prospects voice it on sales calls, then the solution, address pains they want to avoid (e.g. "not an agency," no lifelong retainer), "custom implementation, guaranteed results," single CTA. ([27:14](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eEiyrvM1nB8&t=1634s))
- `[CREATIVE]` Angle = different desire/positioning (watermelon: family vs health); variation = different execution of the same angle. Need 2–10 angles as you scale; each extends the life of the offer. ([29:49](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eEiyrvM1nB8&t=1789s))
- `[CREATIVE]` Test angles before variations. They burned ~$200K testing variations of the wrong angle ("going viral/views") before finding the real one (content that makes money fast). If the angle fails, 100 variations will too. ([32:25](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eEiyrvM1nB8&t=1945s))
- `[ADS]` Keep account structure consolidated — a $1M+/mo client runs exactly two campaigns. Fragmented accounts (15 campaigns × 10 ad sets) starve the algorithm of data and underperform. ([33:57](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eEiyrvM1nB8&t=2037s))
- `[KPIS]` Ignore generic benchmarks; derive KPIs from LTV. Service rule of thumb: CAC ≤ 30% of LTV. Example: LTV $5,000 → CAC $1,500; 20% close, 50% show → 20 booked calls per 2 clients → target $150 cost per booked call (then adjust for commissions, fees, refunds, rep differences). ([35:31](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eEiyrvM1nB8&t=2131s))
- `[ADS]` Test one variable at a time, in order of speed: creative (fastest, highest leverage, daily), landing page (needs several hundred thousand dollars of spend for significance), sales process/offer (slowest, multi-month). ([38:07](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eEiyrvM1nB8&t=2287s))
- `[ADS]` Don't kill tests early: budget 3–5× target KPI per ad (e.g. $450–$750 at $150/call) and wait at least a week (85% of their prospects book within 4 days, plus post-call follow-up). Longer sales cycles need longer windows. ([40:41](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eEiyrvM1nB8&t=2441s))
- `[ADS]` Judge using all-time, last 7 days and last 3 days — never a single day. Before cutting a former winner, lower its budget: ads profitable at $1K/day can break at $2K and recover when pulled back. ([42:13](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eEiyrvM1nB8&t=2533s))
- `[SCALING-STAGES]` Run many creatives at small budgets rather than hunting one monster ad; narrow $50/day ads can be as or more profitable. Don't scale until margins are great — spend ≠ success ($200K spend for $100K profit beats $1M spend for $100K); diminishing returns make results worse as you scale. ([43:45](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eEiyrvM1nB8&t=2625s))
- `[ADS]` Ad-ID trick (seen to cut costs up to ~20%): reuse the same post/ad ID across ad sets and campaigns so likes/comments/shares pool into one social-proof-heavy post. Caveat: copy can't be edited later, so keep guarantees/new deliverables out of evergreen posts. ([46:21](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eEiyrvM1nB8&t=2781s))
- `[ADS]` Avoid bans: check every funnel link before launch, no misleading/income-claim headlines (move result/guarantee claims into the VSL or lower on the page), ad and page must match, no instant redirects, no fake play buttons, no "you"-based personal-attribute callouts (finance/health), no swearing or deliberate misspellings. ([49:29](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eEiyrvM1nB8&t=2969s))
