- `[CASE-STUDIES]` Prospects pay based on what they believe you can do for them, and case studies are the proof. Daniel calls them the most valuable asset in the company. ([0:31](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=bxtXMHvdArs&t=31s))
- `[CASE-STUDIES]` Before writing any, map the 3–5 buckets your service creates value in. Viral Coach uses four: reach (views/followers), time efficiency, authority (looking professional, press, "internet famous") and direct business impact (leads, ROAS, revenue). Then each win just gets dropped into one or two buckets. ([1:33](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=bxtXMHvdArs&t=93s), [2:35](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=bxtXMHvdArs&t=155s))
- `[CASE-STUDIES]` Package the same result four ways so case studies don't sound identical: a raw number (10M views), a percentage (+900%), a multiple (100×, which hits harder than "+10,000%"), or tied to a timeframe (10M views in 30 days, his favourite). One client can read as "made $100K", "+60% revenue", "10× from ads" and "4× the business in 6 months". ([4:08](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=bxtXMHvdArs&t=248s), [5:40](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=bxtXMHvdArs&t=340s))
- `[CASE-STUDIES]` Run a company-wide #client-wins Slack channel that gets posts every day, weekends included: views milestones, lower CPL, better-looking studio setups. Collecting material after an engagement ends is nearly impossible because ex-clients ghost. ([6:12](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=bxtXMHvdArs&t=372s))
- `[SALES-MGMT]` The wins channel keeps closers motivated. They close 12–15% of live calls, so about 90% of a rep's day is hearing no, and seeing wins from clients they sold two weeks earlier gives them conviction to tell the next prospect they're losing money by not signing. ([7:14](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=bxtXMHvdArs&t=434s))
- `[FULFILLMENT]` It also keeps support from assuming everything is on fire. Wins outpace tickets, which helps them advocate for difficult clients instead of letting them churn. ([7:45](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=bxtXMHvdArs&t=465s))
- `[CASE-STUDIES]` Treat each posted win as a thread to pull. One client's 1.7M-view post led to a full-account review: in the 90 days before, 4 posts and 22K views; in 90 days with them, 362 posts (+9,000% output), over 5M views (190×) and 50K followers. ([9:16](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=bxtXMHvdArs&t=556s))
- `[FULFILLMENT]` Own attribution or you lose credit (and renewals). Clients judge you on the last 30 days of cost against the last 30 days of results. Daniel added paid ads to the service partly to get CRM and ad-manager access. Account managers ask which deals a client closed recently, then trace each one back to an ad or organic touch. ([10:51](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=bxtXMHvdArs&t=651s), [11:54](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=bxtXMHvdArs&t=714s))
- `[SALES-CALL]` Record every sales call, for compliance and to capture what the client said they wanted. ([13:28](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=bxtXMHvdArs&t=808s))
- `[FULFILLMENT]` Use an onboarding intake survey to record baselines: followers, monthly views, posting frequency, CAC, ad spend vs revenue. A baseline goal like "I just want one 20K-view video" turns a later 1.5M-view video and 100K followers into a story of exceeding expectations. ([13:28](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=bxtXMHvdArs&t=808s), [13:59](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=bxtXMHvdArs&t=839s))
- `[CASE-STUDIES]` One-slide format with five elements: (1) client name and face, (2) what they do, for pattern-matching, (3) a visual before/after, (4) one headline result that stops the scroll, (5) supporting stats as "free dessert". Simpler converts better. ([15:03](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=bxtXMHvdArs&t=903s))
- `[CASE-STUDIES]` Don't explain the strategy in case studies. Early 15-minute walkthroughs either went unwatched or gave prospects the know-how to try it themselves. When someone asks how you did it, the answer is "pay me and I'll show you." ([17:07](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=bxtXMHvdArs&t=1027s))
- `[OBJECTIONS]` "I'm not a plumber" objection: prospects who ask for a more relevant case study want the outcome, not the same industry. One client (Jared: 200M views, 427K followers) covers all four buckets, so pick the angle that matches the prospect's pain: 40→4 hours a month on content, replacing $60K a month in ad spend, most-followed plumber. ([17:39](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=bxtXMHvdArs&t=1059s), [18:39](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=bxtXMHvdArs&t=1119s))
- `[CREATIVE]` Repurpose each case study: the slide as a static ad, then a short-form video, a long-form video, an Instagram carousel, an email, Facebook and LinkedIn posts (reworked with Claude). Do the work once and it runs on every channel every month. ([19:41](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=bxtXMHvdArs&t=1181s))
- `[SALES-CALL]` Build a 30-slide case-study deck for pre-call material and for reps to pull up live, segmented by prospect type (female founders, starting from scratch, biggest businesses), so reps always have relevant proof. ([20:44](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=bxtXMHvdArs&t=1244s))
**Short quotes:**
- "Your prospects only pay you based on what they believe you can do for them. And case studies are how you prove it." ([0:31](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=bxtXMHvdArs&t=31s))
- "One result told from the right angle can speak to almost anyone." ([19:10](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=bxtXMHvdArs&t=1150s))
**G&L angle:**
- Define G&L's value buckets. Suggested: (1) deal acquired with little cash down (purchase price vs cash in), (2) speed (offer to close in X days), (3) cash flow and ROI (EBITDA bought, payback period), (4) for sellers, exit valuation or multiple uplift and time to exit.
- Frame results as multiples and with timeframes: "bought a $1.2M-EBITDA business with 5% down in 90 days" or "sold at 4.5× vs a 3× opening offer".
- Run a #deal-wins channel (LOIs signed, financing approved, closings, seller valuation lifts). Record baselines at intake (buyer's capital and experience; seller's EBITDA, owner dependency, initial valuation).
- Build a segmented proof deck for setters and closers: first-time buyers, corporate-exit buyers, roll-ups, retiring 55+ sellers. Send it as pre-call material.
- Don't reveal the vendor-finance structuring playbook in case studies. Show the outcome and keep the "how" for paying clients.

## The reality of scaling a $5M/mo business with my wife — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=feWJhxN9M5g
**Gist:** An unscripted conversation between Daniel and his wife Noel (CMO) about running a 100+ person, $5M/month company as a married couple. They cover data-driven decisions (whoever has more information decides), why business and marriage can't be compartmentalised, handling conflict with a spouse differently from conflict with an employee, getting through near-bankruptcy, and how their revenue forecasting works.
**Nuggets:**
- `[LEADERSHIP]` Tie-break rule between partners: "who has more information?" The person with more context makes the call and the other defers completely. Because decisions are data-driven, arguments stay rare. ([3:36](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=feWJhxN9M5g&t=216s))
- `[PARTNERSHIP]` A married couple in business spends roughly an extra 8 hours a day together. Daniel's math (which he asks viewers to check) makes that ~180× the conversation time of an average couple, so communication gets heavily practised. They went in with about 10 years of trust already built. ([1:01](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=feWJhxN9M5g&t=61s), [10:49](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=feWJhxN9M5g&t=649s))
- `[PARTNERSHIP]` You can't fully separate "business at the office" and "marriage at home". Each bleeds into the other, and both are personal-development tools. ([6:40](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=feWJhxN9M5g&t=400s))
- `[TEAM]` To bring a spouse or partner into the business: invite them in, define their role, and push them to grow in it. Noel was an economics professor with no marketing experience. She became CMO and ran $70K of ad spend in month one. The company went from 3 people (Daniel, Noel and the current COO) to 100+. ([9:48](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=feWJhxN9M5g&t=588s), [11:21](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=feWJhxN9M5g&t=681s))
- `[LEADERSHIP]` Between partners, keep the same high standard as for the team, but handle failure differently. The "your job's on the line" lever used on underperforming sales reps can't be used on a spouse or co-owner, so treat their failure like your own. ([22:15](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=feWJhxN9M5g&t=1335s), [23:48](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=feWJhxN9M5g&t=1428s))
- `[FULFILLMENT]` Conflict post-mortem: a big new client needed about 700 ad creatives uploaded. Noel estimated 20–30 minutes and it took 5+ hours. The lessons: start urgent client-onboarding tasks the same day, and when you can't, surface the blockers clearly (sign-offs, CRM and tracking issues). The CEO's frustration was about not having visibility. ([16:03](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=feWJhxN9M5g&t=963s), [19:40](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=feWJhxN9M5g&t=1180s))
- `[LEADERSHIP]` Never fire shots by text message. It's one of the worst ways to communicate, especially in the middle of a stressful day. ([20:10](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=feWJhxN9M5g&t=1210s))
- `[LIFE]` Hire out every household task so the only things you do are run the business and be with your kids. ([13:27](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=feWJhxN9M5g&t=807s))
- `[MINDSET]` They once had $400 in the bank and ~$500K of uncollateralised debt. With no plan B and full commitment, they kept going where others would have gone back to a job. A big shared goal also shrinks petty conflicts. ([14:31](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=feWJhxN9M5g&t=871s), [12:56](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=feWJhxN9M5g&t=776s))
- `[MINDSET]` No fixed five-year plan. They play "the growing game" (which buttons make it bigger, faster, more profitable) and aren't chasing a dollar target, which keeps them satisfied and focused. The business started 3 years earlier as an idea for a social-media course. ([26:55](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=feWJhxN9M5g&t=1615s), [25:53](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=feWJhxN9M5g&t=1553s))
- `[KPIS]` They forecast revenue with five separate models: revenue per lead, revenue per call, a percentage-holding model (garbled in captions), total calendar availability, and others. All converged on $5.1–5.3M, which they hit. ([27:26](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=feWJhxN9M5g&t=1646s))
**Short quotes:**
- "When you're digging yourself out of half a million dollars in debt, who took out the trash today is completely irrelevant." ([12:56](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=feWJhxN9M5g&t=776s))
- "The person with more information takes over." ([4:06](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=feWJhxN9M5g&t=246s))
**G&L angle:**
- Between G&L partners, adopt "whoever has more data decides". For example, the marketing lead owns funnel calls and the deal lead owns structuring calls.
- Forecast monthly revenue several independent ways (revenue per lead, revenue per call, closer calendar capacity) and check that they agree before committing to spend or hiring.
- Many clients are family-owned businesses run by couples (common among 55+ sellers). Use this video's framing on spousal roles and conflict when discussing succession and exit readiness with them.
- For new clients, start onboarding deliverables the same day (data room, valuation inputs, deal-sourcing setup), and give the lead partner visibility of blockers rather than vague time estimates.

## Scaling 9 figure sales teams (Raw Conversation) — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=JEUmEI4REu0
**Gist:** Daniel interviews Matt Ryder (CEO of Dialer IO, formerly ran sales at 7th Level and Sales Sniper and for Russell Brunson) about outbound and setter operations and sales-floor management. Topics: compliant dialing, which roughly doubled answer rates; the key dialer KPIs (unique dial rate, contact rate, hours, scheduling against lead arrival); triage-call length; adjustable qualification levels; capacity and call-stacking limits; ROI-based rep scoring with merit-based lead routing. The last third covers content: organic for buyers rather than views, and how ads and organic reinforce each other.
**Nuggets:**
- `[SETTING]` At 7th Level they got about 50,000 leads/month (5–6k buyers) but reached only about 8% of them. The instinct was to buy more leads; Matt now says he'd have cut lead flow by 60% and focused on speed-to-lead ([1:02](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=JEUmEI4REu0&t=62s)).
- `[TOOLS]` Many dialers run on Twilio and push multi-line or auto-dialing and double-dialing that break carrier and consumer-protection rules (calling outside state hours, too often, double-dials to get past iPhone Focus mode). Carriers judge numbers by behaviour, so non-compliant dialing gets numbers flagged and lowers answer rates ([4:38](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=JEUmEI4REu0&t=278s)).
- `[TOOLS]` Daniel's answer rate was 20–25% on Kixie, GHL and other auto-dialers and about 45% on a compliant dialer that makes each number behave like a human's. Compliance doubled results ([7:13](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=JEUmEI4REu0&t=433s)).
- `[SETTING]` Setters are entry-level and often don't understand the legal exposure, e.g. three calls at 9pm in the prospect's time zone. Build the rules into the system rather than trusting the reps ([8:15](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=JEUmEI4REu0&t=495s)).
- `[KPIS]` Unique dial rate is the share of calls going to distinct leads; 30–40% is ideal. At 10–15% you badly need more leads (about six calls per lead). When Matt saw Daniel's high lead volume and low unique dial rate, he advised hiring about 12 more SDRs, and revenue rose ([10:48](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=JEUmEI4REu0&t=648s)).
- `[KPIS]` Contact rate (share of leads actually spoken to) matters more than answer rate. Aim for 60%+ on dialing alone, about 80% with SMS and email added; 50%+ is good, and the best he has seen is 92%. Overloading the system with leads drags contact rate down ([12:20](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=JEUmEI4REu0&t=740s)).
- `[KPIS]` Measure setters on hours online, not calls made. 6–7 quality hours a day per setter keeps speed-to-lead high (the HubSpot/MIT study found contacting within 5 minutes made a sale 100x more likely). Cadence is about 3 calls a day and at most about 10 a week. Leads hit the dialer through the API about 0.1 seconds after opt-in, so setters must stay logged in ([13:53](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=JEUmEI4REu0&t=833s)).
- `[SETTING]` Schedule shifts to when leads arrive and prospects can answer. One client selling to women stopped calling at school pickup (3pm), dialled hard until 2pm, and reached a 91% contact rate. Many opt-ins arrive 6–8pm while teams finish at 5pm, so staff the evening. Schedule changes alone have lifted revenue about 30% ([15:26](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=JEUmEI4REu0&t=926s)).
- `[SETTING]` Triage or setting calls should take 7–10 minutes; longer calls showed no correlation with better results. Track calls ended under 15 seconds (the opener needs work; "I'm busy" is a brush-off because busy people don't answer unknown numbers) and under 2 minutes (moving from opener to a clear problem) ([17:31](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=JEUmEI4REu0&t=1051s)).
- `[APPLICATION]` Keep 2–3 levels of qualification questions and switch them weekly with capacity. When reps are full, use level 3 (strictest). When volume drops, use level 1 and "send in the hordes", accepting a lower close rate for more opportunities. Use ChatGPT to write tighter timing and budget questions. Script them word for word, because 22-year-old setters will improvise ([19:04](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=JEUmEI4REu0&t=1144s)).
- `[SALES-MGMT]` Target about 80% calendar utilisation (show-ups against available slots), double-book if needed, and use a floating closer to pick up overflow (Cole Gordon model) ([19:35](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=JEUmEI4REu0&t=1175s)).
- `[SALES-HIRING]` Full-stack outbound closers, who work leads, call and close themselves, were paid more than inbound closers at 7th Level and judged purely on ROAS: hit 10x and you get better leads ([22:12](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=JEUmEI4REu0&t=1332s)).
- `[SALES-MGMT]` Closers perform best at 70–80% of capacity. At 100% they took shortcuts and lost deals. Daniel found that past a certain level, extra ad spend stopped producing extra revenue because closer capacity was the bottleneck ([23:45](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=JEUmEI4REu0&t=1425s)).
- `[SALES-MGMT]` Back-to-back limits: Matt gave about 200 reps Oura rings and found recovery score correlated with close rate. Calls 6, 7 and 8 in a row closed at about 2%, so cap at five in a row and use patterns like three calls, a break, three calls ([24:15](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=JEUmEI4REu0&t=1455s)).
- `[KPIS]` Reps fill in a two-second form after each call. After a month, build a heat map of show-up by time of day. One two-hour block might run at 10% show-up against 80% elsewhere; move team training into that block ([25:48](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=JEUmEI4REu0&t=1548s)).
- `[SALES-CALL]` In two years of 7th Level data, about 90% of sales closed within 45 minutes (only 8% after). Booking 45-minute instead of 60-minute calls adds about three calls per rep per day, shortens time-to-call and lifts show-up ([26:19](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=JEUmEI4REu0&t=1579s)).
- `[SALES-MGMT]` Reps who want 12 calls a day: let the data persuade them ("let's try nine and do them well"). Fewer, better calls raise hourly earnings and ROI ([29:26](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=JEUmEI4REu0&t=1766s)).
- `[KPIS]` Rate reps on ROI: revenue generated against the cost of the leads given to them. Every other metric can be gamed, and the only way to game ROI is by bringing in referrals. Clear obstacles for top ROI reps, e.g. give them a VA for admin ([30:28](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=JEUmEI4REu0&t=1828s)).
- `[SALES-MGMT]` Route by merit, like a sports team: the best closer ("Steph Curry gets the ball") is booked every hour, and a weaker rep's confirmed call gets swapped to the top rep's unconfirmed slot. Fill the best rep's calendar first, then the next. No equal round-robin ([31:29](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=JEUmEI4REu0&t=1889s)).
- `[APPLICATION]` Daniel routes by historical revenue per lead based on application answers. High stated income (e.g. $250k+/month) goes to top reps; "just browsing, not deciding within 30 days" goes to the weakest. Matt had two reps handling only website leads, the best source ([32:31](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=JEUmEI4REu0&t=1951s)).
- `[SALES-MGMT]` Hide application answers from closers. Their bias about whether a lead will close becomes self-fulfilling, and blind testing confirmed it. Daniel's reps wrongly avoided real-estate leads that had closed five times that month ([33:32](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=JEUmEI4REu0&t=2012s)).
- `[APPLICATION]` Discard applications submitted between 11pm and 3am. One operator (Spencer) found 57% of completed applications were fake ([34:33](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=JEUmEI4REu0&t=2073s)).
- `[SALES-MGMT]` Daniel's style is hands-off: he judges reps on revenue per lead (monthly and rolling 90 days) and lets them run calls however they like within compliance, with the 7-day refund (no commission on refunds) as the backstop. Matt is strict with new reps ("do it my way until you're top") and gives freedom only to the best ([35:33](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=JEUmEI4REu0&t=2133s)).
- `[OBJECTIONS]` Treat show, no-show, reschedule and cancel as separate problems. Cancellations are usually online reputation (people Google you and find Reddit). Fix it with a brand-neutral testimonial site such as "xyzreviews.com" that you keep adding to until it outranks Reddit ([36:34](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=JEUmEI4REu0&t=2194s)).
- `[SALES-MGMT]` New reps should memorise the script first, then the process, then the funnel. A memorised script frees attention to listen. Most reps' internal monologue drowns out the prospect ([38:38](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=JEUmEI4REu0&t=2318s)).
- `[SALES-MGMT]` Coaching: give one most-important fix per review, not 20 notes, and check again after a dozen calls. Twenty notes leave reps stuck at intermediate level ("The Hard Thing About Hard Things": work on one thing while nine stay suboptimal) ([40:10](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=JEUmEI4REu0&t=2410s)).
- `[ORGANIC]` Daniel's earlier channels had 800k YouTube subscribers, 1.3M on TikTok and 100M+ views a month but earned far less. Content aimed at business owners who can pay now earns him dramatically more per view. Measure link clicks to the website rather than views: technical videos got about one-tenth of the views and about 100x the clicks ([42:42](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=JEUmEI4REu0&t=2562s)).
- `[ORGANIC]` Matt makes about $100k/month from 2 hours of content a week and a VA, with no funnel or salespeople. Sophisticated content lets people judge for themselves whether you know your stuff ([44:47](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=JEUmEI4REu0&t=2687s)).
- `[ORGANIC]` Keep short and long form consistent. Short-form (Instagram) is top of funnel, where most people first find you, and should reflect the depth of your long-form (YouTube) rather than generic Hormozi-style clips. YouTube is where people binge your catalogue and come to trust you ([48:24](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=JEUmEI4REu0&t=2904s)).
- `[ORGANIC]` Podcasts: 31 episodes (about 7 months weekly) puts you in the top 5% because most quit. A prospect who says "I saw your podcast" is close to a guaranteed sale. A 1,000-view reel has brought deals worth $300–400k/month ([50:57](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=JEUmEI4REu0&t=3057s)).
- `[CREATIVE]` Dave Ramsey example: the video with 10x the views had far lower ROAS than the one built to convert. Decide whether a piece is meant for reach or for revenue ([51:28](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=JEUmEI4REu0&t=3088s)).
- `[ADS]` Ads plus organic works out to more than the sum (Daniel's "2+2=10"). Retargeting people who found you organically converts much better than cold traffic, and people who were creators first make far better ads. When they paused long-form YouTube for about 10 days, Meta ad costs jumped ([53:01](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=JEUmEI4REu0&t=3181s)).
- `[ADS]` Platform demographics (Pew): of US households earning $100k+, about 90% use YouTube and about 36% TikTok. TikTok gives cheap views and fewer conversions; YouTube has higher CPMs and higher ROAS. Match content to the platform: memes on TikTok/IG, more sophisticated long-form on YouTube ([56:06](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=JEUmEI4REu0&t=3366s)).
- `[ADS]` Meta puts user experience first, so spammy or deceptive ads cost more to deliver. Ads that would work as organic content get cheaper distribution. If "Meta ads didn't work", ask how much time they've spent on organic ([57:07](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=JEUmEI4REu0&t=3427s)).
- `[ORGANIC]` Film short form specifically for vertical rather than cutting it from long form when you can. Matt's numbers rose about 47% in 60 days after switching to dedicated short form. Allow 6–12 months to get comfortable on camera ([59:11](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=JEUmEI4REu0&t=3551s)).
- `[ORGANIC]` Daniel's closing thesis: don't dumb content down. Respect the audience's intelligence, because sophisticated content attracts buyers ([1:03:23](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=JEUmEI4REu0&t=3803s)).
**Short quotes:**
- "If you do 10 in a row, you're no better than if you fail 10 in a row." ([28:23](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=JEUmEI4REu0&t=1703s))
- "the more views you get from the general population, the lower your revenue per view will be" ([44:15](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=JEUmEI4REu0&t=2655s))
**G&L angle:**
- Setter dashboard for G&L: unique dial rate (30–40%), contact rate (60%+), hours online (6–7/day) and speed-to-lead under 5 minutes. Staff evening shifts, since employed buyer prospects opt in after work. Keep dialing compliant (state calling hours, no spammy double-dials).
- Route the best leads (high liquid capital or high-EBITDA sellers) to the top advisor and fill that calendar first. Hide application details from closers and test whether close rates rise.
- Book 45-minute rather than 60-minute strategy calls, cap closers at about five calls in a row, and aim for 70–80% calendar utilisation. Build a show-up heat map by time slot.
- Fight cancellations with a G&L reviews/case-study site and Trustpilot so searches for "G&L M&A reviews" land on proof.
- Publish technical acquisition content (deal structures, vendor-finance terms, valuation multiples, due-diligence checklists). It gets fewer views but brings serious buyers and sellers. YouTube reaches 90% of $100k+ households, which suits 55+ owners.

## How I manage $1m per month in adspend — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=tJWuC8HG4Ps
**Gist:** Daniel's manual Meta budget system for lead-gen at about $1M/month. One ad per ad set (ABO), three saved filters, custom columns, eight round-number budget tiers driven by cost per booked call, and a routine of all-time/7-day checks once a week plus a rolling 3-day check every day. Only ever move one tier per ad per day. He stresses that the real scaling lever is having hundreds of winning creatives, not button-mashing.
**Nuggets:**
- `[ADS]` Why lead-gen breaks Meta's "trust the algorithm" advice: there are fewer data points ($1K might buy one or two qualified calls vs 20 e-com purchases), the data arrives late (closes happen weeks later), and the proxy pixel on a booked call is mostly wrong signal. Viral Coach closes only about 7% of booked calls, so 93% of "wins" sent to Meta weren't buyers. ([1:32](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=tJWuC8HG4Ps&t=92s), [3:05](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=tJWuC8HG4Ps&t=185s))
- `[ADS]` Optimising for booked calls skews toward people with free time. High-value prospects guard their calendars and rarely jump on booking links. ([3:05](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=tJWuC8HG4Ps&t=185s))
- `[ADS]` Structure: ABO with one ad per ad set so you control each creative's budget. In CBO one ad takes about 90% of spend, and ads CBO ignored sometimes became top performers once given budget. ([4:39](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=tJWuC8HG4Ps&t=279s))
- `[ADS]` Saved filters: "had delivery" (≥1 impression), "one result" (exactly one booked call), "scale" (more than one result), "no results". Custom columns ("Daniel's analytics"): budget, amount spent, results (booked calls), cost per result, date last edited. Conditional colour formatting marks which action to take. ([5:42](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=tJWuC8HG4Ps&t=342s), [6:13](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=tJWuC8HG4Ps&t=373s))
- `[ADS]` Budget ladder (his account): every new ad launches at $10/day; first result → $50/day; cost per call under $700 → $100/day; under the $500 account-average cost per call → up to $1,000/day; capped at $3,000/day. Most ads top out around $2K/day. ([7:45](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=tJWuC8HG4Ps&t=465s))
- `[ADS]` Cap per-ad budget (around $3K/day for him). Pushing too high exhausts the people likely to convert, Meta then serves poor fits, and that is what usually gets called "fatigue". Lowering distribution can bring the ad back into a profitable range. ([8:48](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=tJWuC8HG4Ps&t=528s), [9:18](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=tJWuC8HG4Ps&t=558s))
- `[TOOLS]` Derive your own tier brackets: export the last 30 days of delivered ads to CSV and give it to a GPT along with your daily target (example: $35K/day). Use round numbers, expect ±10% from target, and double-check the output. ([9:49](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=tJWuC8HG4Ps&t=589s))
- `[ADS]` Kill rules: turn off any ad set that has spent more than $1,000 with zero results, or more than $1,000 with only one result. Two-result ads that are still too expensive also get cut. Sort the scale view to find cheap, profitable ads that are starved of budget. ([13:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=tJWuC8HG4Ps&t=780s), [14:01](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=tJWuC8HG4Ps&t=841s))
- `[ADS]` Move only one tier at a time, and make at most one change per ad per day (the "date last edited" column prevents double changes across 500+ creatives). Big jumps destabilise delivery: launching at $10K/day only spent about $2K by midday instead of $5K. ([14:33](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=tJWuC8HG4Ps&t=873s), [15:04](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=tJWuC8HG4Ps&t=904s))
- `[KPIS]` Cadence: run the all-time and 7-day lookbacks across all three filters once a week (housekeeping: kill losers, promote proven ads). Run a rolling 3-day lookback daily for faster up/down moves. One day is too noisy for lead-gen and 7 days is too slow to catch a $1K/day problem. Pick 3–5 days depending on spend. ([12:29](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=tJWuC8HG4Ps&t=749s), [18:37](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=tJWuC8HG4Ps&t=1117s))
- `[ADS]` The daily pass takes about 90 seconds: decrease anything over KPI by one tier, and don't add budget to single-result ads until more data comes in. ([19:38](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=tJWuC8HG4Ps&t=1178s))
- `[CREATIVE]` Budget management alone won't get you to $1M/month in spend. Hundreds of winning ads (some spending $3K/day each) will. Creative volume is the real lever. ([21:14](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=tJWuC8HG4Ps&t=1274s))
**Short quotes:**
- "Out of every 100 wins that we report to Facebook, 93% of them were wrong." ([3:36](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=tJWuC8HG4Ps&t=216s))
- "You can't min-max this to a million dollars a month in ad spend profitably. But having hundreds of winning ads to put money behind can do that." ([21:14](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=tJWuC8HG4Ps&t=1274s))
**G&L angle:**
- G&L's sales cycle is long and buyers (especially sellers aged 55+) are rare, so the booked-call pixel is a weak signal. Use ABO with one ad per ad set, and consider firing the conversion only on qualified bookings (for example, a capital threshold met or a seller with EBITDA above X) to clean up the signal.
- Set budget tiers from G&L's own average cost per qualified call. Start every ad at a small test budget, promote one tier per day, cap the per-ad budget, and set a kill threshold of roughly 2× target cost per call with zero results.
- Run separate campaign sets for the buyer offer and the seller (exit) offer, since their cost-per-call benchmarks will differ widely.
- Invest in creative volume (many hooks and angles about buying with vendor finance, or exit readiness) rather than tinkering with the ads manager.

## The math behind crazy profit margins — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=W0pUXPxvQyI
**Gist:** A five-minute accounting lesson: know six definitions (revenue, COGS, gross profit, overhead, net profit, gross margin) and use gross margin as the profit lever. Raising prices on new customers is far easier than cutting delivery costs, and doubling price — even if you lose half your clients — can multiply profit ~7× while halving complexity, which is exactly what private equity does after buying small businesses.
**Nuggets:**
- `[FINANCE]` Six definitions for a business under ~$10M/yr: revenue; cost of goods sold (direct delivery cost); gross profit; overhead (software, admin — not attributable to one client); net profit; gross margin = gross profit ÷ revenue. ([0:31](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=W0pUXPxvQyI&t=31s))
- `[PROFIT]` Example client: $3,000 price, $2,000 delivery cost (contract coach), $500 overhead → $1,000 gross profit, $500 net, 33% gross margin, 17% net margin. ([1:01](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=W0pUXPxvQyI&t=61s))
- `[PROFIT]` Lifting gross margin from 33% to 50% triples net profit per customer ($500 → $1,500). Two ways: cut costs or raise prices. ([2:02](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=W0pUXPxvQyI&t=122s))
- `[PRICING]` Cutting delivery cost 25% means asking staff to take a pay cut — a people decision most owners can't stomach. Raising price 33% ($3K → $4K) hits 50% margin; new customers don't know what you charged yesterday. ([2:34](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=W0pUXPxvQyI&t=154s))
- `[PRICING]` Index prices to inflation: she hadn't raised prices in 6+ years while healthcare costs rose ~7%/yr (~50% cumulative), so a 50% increase would only have kept pace. ([3:37](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=W0pUXPxvQyI&t=217s))
- `[PRICING]` Doubling price ($6K) → $3,500 net per customer (+700%). Even losing half the clients (500 → 250), revenue is unchanged, profit soars, and there's half the delivery/client-management load — capacity to grow back to 500 without new staff, and reinvesting in marketing could reach ~7× annual profit. ([4:07](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=W0pUXPxvQyI&t=247s))
- `[PROFIT]` Private equity buyers run this exact math on acquisitions: change one number (price/margin), multiply profit, move on. Profit — not revenue, headcount, client count or years in business — determines whether a business is worth owning. ([5:09](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=W0pUXPxvQyI&t=309s))
- `[SALES-CALL]` Diagnostic selling: on the call he spotted underpricing as the real bottleneck before any marketing — showing you understand the prospect's economics (not just "leads, cost of leads") builds trust. ([6:10](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=W0pUXPxvQyI&t=370s))
**Short quotes:**
- "Profit is the number that determines whether your business is actually worth owning. It's not revenue." ([5:40](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=W0pUXPxvQyI&t=340s))
- "Raising prices is almost always way easier than you think." ([5:40](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=W0pUXPxvQyI&t=340s))
**G&L angle:**
- Make this the core of G&L exit-readiness: many 55+ owners haven't raised prices in years; a price/gross-margin fix before sale increases EBITDA and, via the multiple, the exit price — a powerful, concrete promise for the VSL and sales call ("we do what PE would do *before* you sell, so you keep the upside").
- For acquirers, it's the post-acquisition value-creation thesis: buy an underpriced business with seller finance, raise prices to industry rates, and the extra profit services the vendor note. Use the $3K → $6K worked example in content.
- Apply it to G&L's own pricing: check gross margin per engagement (advisor time, legal, valuation costs) and raise retainers/success fees for new clients rather than squeezing delivery staff.
- On sales calls, run a quick margin diagnostic on the seller's business (gross margin, price history) to demonstrate expertise and surface the gap G&L closes.

## These emails made me $720K (just copy them) — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=SGeD3rpjlBA
**Gist:** His five best-converting nurture emails, which earned over $500K in about 6 months. The playbook: reuse your best-tested landing-page headlines as subject lines and PS lines, add sales-side incentives (a free trial) to email, send existing YouTube videos, and send client case studies to the whole list.
**Nuggets:**
- `[EMAIL]` Make the subject line of your first email the highest-converting landing-page headline (theirs: "1 million views or you don't pay"). This keeps the message consistent from ad to page to email, and the prospect sees the same proven words a third time. ([0:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=SGeD3rpjlBA&t=0s))
- `[EMAIL]` Use the second- and third-best tested headlines as subject lines for the next emails ("great results with very little time"). Emails cost nothing to send. ([1:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=SGeD3rpjlBA&t=60s))
- `[EMAIL]` Body template: "fresh stats from the team this morning" → average client result, counting only clients who did the minimum work (e.g. 15+ videos over 3 months) → the best client result as a high anchor so the average seems achievable → common thoughts business owners have → "it's not luck, it's a system" → what the system looks like → CTA. ([1:32](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=SGeD3rpjlBA&t=92s))
- `[GUARANTEE]` Everyone reads the PS. Use it to restate the performance guarantee or best headline ("if it doesn't work, you don't pay"). Use the PPS for a wall of reviews and testimonials. ([2:33](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=SGeD3rpjlBA&t=153s))
- `[SALES-CALL]` A 7-day trial on sales calls cut commitment friction and raised top-line revenue 30%. Business owners hear many pitches and hesitate even when offer and price are right. ([3:03](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=SGeD3rpjlBA&t=183s))
- `[EMAIL]` Use every incentive that works on calls (trial, discounts) in the front-end marketing and emails too. The trial email became one of their best performers. ([3:35](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=SGeD3rpjlBA&t=215s))
- `[EMAIL]` Short paragraph plus a link to an existing YouTube video: about 5 minutes to write and $144K in 6 months. It also grows YouTube and builds Google Ads retargeting audiences. They now send one catalog video a week. ([4:05](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=SGeD3rpjlBA&t=245s))
- `[CASE-STUDIES]` Send every client win to the entire email list, not just to the client or the team Slack. Case-study emails are their 5th through 9th most profitable. Format: before (344 followers) → after 90 days (100K followers, 10M views in 30 days) → screenshots → "click here to work with us" → PS with the guarantee. ([5:07](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=SGeD3rpjlBA&t=307s))
**Short quotes:**
- "Take your landing page copy, as long as you know it works, make it an email, and make more money because of it." ([1:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=SGeD3rpjlBA&t=60s))
- "Never forget the PS." ([6:09](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=SGeD3rpjlBA&t=369s))
**G&L angle:**
- A/B test VSL page headlines, then use the top 3 as subject lines for the first 3 nurture emails and repeat the winner in every PS.
- Send each closed deal as a case-study email, e.g. "Bought a $2.1M HVAC business with $120K down and 60% seller finance" or "65-year-old owner exits at 4.5× after 9 months of exit prep." Include anonymized screenshots where possible.
- Consider a low-friction entry incentive (e.g. a paid deal-review or 7-day "deal sprint" trial) if prospects stall on commitment, and mention it in both sales calls and emails.
- Send one existing YouTube or VSL-section video a week to the list. It is cheap content that builds retargeting audiences.
- In the stats email, count only clients who did the minimum work (e.g. reviewed X deals or submitted LOIs) and use the best result as the anchor.

## Why my average sales rep makes $20K/mo (sales hiring playbook) — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=oLR7G2L6bDg
**Gist:** Daniel shows the full Viral Coach closer-hiring system: a job post that proves earnings with real 90-day commission data, a Loom-video application that replaces interviews, an "interview" that's really the candidate's Q&A, lead tiers by rep performance, a 100-lead sink-or-swim trial with an SDR fallback, tiered commission paid in the month of close, and a 3–5 day handbook + mock-call onboarding.
**Nuggets:**
- `[SALES-HIRING]` Treat recruiting like a sales funnel: big claim + proof. The careers page shows the last 90 days of every full-time rep's leads contacted, deals won, revenue and commission (avg ~$20k/month, top reps ~$40k). ([2:04](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=oLR7G2L6bDg&t=124s))
- `[SALES-HIRING]` Job-post language reps love: 100% inbound B2B lead flow, full pipeline provided, short sales cycle, performance-rewarded culture, mentorship, access to top call recordings, an outlined one-week ramp. Remote 1099 commission role. YouTube content itself drives dozens of applicants/week. ([1:01](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=oLR7G2L6bDg&t=61s))
- `[SALES-HIRING]` Application = short Loom intro (camera presence is the #1 screen since calls are on video) + 3 questions: most recent relevant experience (ideally selling online to high-level owners); "what is the most important part of a sales call — be specific" (tests concise monologue delivery); "what have you changed your mind about in selling?" (only wrong answer: "I've never been wrong"). ([2:35](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=oLR7G2L6bDg&t=155s))
- `[SALES-HIRING]` A strong Loom (professional background, prepared, rehearsed) lets them skip interviews; the follow-up call tells the candidate they have the job and is used for their questions — which also reveals their discovery skill. ([4:08](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=oLR7G2L6bDg&t=248s))
- `[SALES-MGMT]` Lead tiers: new reps start on tier-3 leads (least qualified per application, hardest, cheapest, ~$1,000 revenue per lead benchmark). Prove yourself to move to tier 2 then tier 1. ([5:08](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=oLR7G2L6bDg&t=308s))
- `[SETTING]` Top-tier reps get extra resources: appointment setters pre-calling their leads to qualify, double-booking so a no-show doesn't waste the hour, and pre-confirmed calls. ([6:11](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=oLR7G2L6bDg&t=371s))
- `[KPIS]` 20+ rep metrics visible to the whole team. Coaching example: high-volume rep with low revenue per call → model the best closer's calls; low revenue per lead → improve pre-call messaging and DQ judgment. ([7:12](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=oLR7G2L6bDg&t=432s))
- `[OBJECTIONS]` Track lost calls by objection type (budget, authority, need, timing) per rep and team-wide; fix individual pitches or run team trainings on that objection. Budget objections led the manager to get three new financing options added. ([8:14](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=oLR7G2L6bDg&t=494s))
- `[GUARANTEE]` 7-day money-back guarantee does two jobs: lowers friction for reps ("try our team; if not, full refund") and acts as a fail-safe against hot-selling — if a client was oversold they refund and the rep earns nothing. Fulfillment rushes as much value as possible into those 7 days. ([9:46](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=oLR7G2L6bDg&t=586s))
- `[KPIS]` 7-day refund rate brought from ~20% down to 10% then 8.8% over six months. ([11:18](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=oLR7G2L6bDg&t=678s))
- `[SALES-HIRING]` Sell candidates on company stability/growth story (Fortune 100 track record, $120k → $200k → $500k → first $1M month within 12 months). ([11:49](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=oLR7G2L6bDg&t=709s))
- `[SALES-HIRING]` Trial period: each new rep gets 100 leads and is benchmarked on revenue per lead/call vs. the team; keep a dedicated sales budget for testing reps. ([13:55](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=oLR7G2L6bDg&t=835s))
- `[SETTING]` Failing closers can move to SDR (outbound/setting with near-free leads) and retry later; top SDR made $24k commission in a month. ([14:27](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=oLR7G2L6bDg&t=867s))
- `[SALES-HIRING]` Tiered commission per deal by monthly volume: $250–500k closed → $1,500/deal; over $500k → $2,000/deal. Paid in full in the month closed (retention is fulfillment's job). Rewards full-timers and top performers, pushes out low performers, halves ramp cost in month one. ([14:58](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=oLR7G2L6bDg&t=898s))
- `[SALES-HIRING]` Onboarding: 1099 contract → company email → Slack → sales lead gives a ~20-page handbook plus ~100 pages of training videos (3–5 days) and hundreds of recorded one-call closes. ([16:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=oLR7G2L6bDg&t=960s))
- `[SALES-CALL]` Script is an optional outline; any style is fine if congruent with the process and never promises what you can't deliver. Best reps borrow pieces from many recorded closes. ([17:02](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=oLR7G2L6bDg&t=1022s))
- `[SALES-MGMT]` Mock call with the sales lead before the inbound calendar is turned on; fail two mock calls and you go to SDR for a month. Calls then populate straight into Google Calendar. ([18:02](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=oLR7G2L6bDg&t=1082s))
**Short quotes:**
- "I don't really care what the most important part of the sales call is to you. I just want to see you deliver an argument very eloquently." ([3:07](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=oLR7G2L6bDg&t=187s))
- "All I care about are the performance and the numbers." ([17:32](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=oLR7G2L6bDg&t=1052s))
**G&L angle:**
- Build a G&L closer careers page showing real rep earnings and inbound volume; ask for a Loom + the same three questions (add: experience selling to business owners/investors or in finance/M&A).
- Score applications into tiers (e.g., liquid capital, business-owner status, timeline for buyers; revenue/EBITDA and age 55+ for sellers) and route tier-1 leads to proven closers with setters and pre-confirmation.
- Benchmark every new rep on revenue per lead over their first 100 leads; keep a testing budget and an SDR/setter fallback path.
- Log lost-deal objection type in the CRM; if budget dominates, add financing/payment-plan options for the advisory fee.
- Pay commission in the month cash is collected with volume tiers; pair with a short, live-on-onboarding refund window to police over-promising (critical in M&A, where promised "no-money-down deals" must be realistic).

## This landing page makes me $116,000 per day (copy it) — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=w5bYv2CBDZE
**Gist:** A walk through Daniel's full lead-to-call funnel in the order a prospect experiences it: separate funnels per traffic source, a simple application landing page (headline, optional VSL, application, proof), application friction and routing that also train the pixel, a booking window of 2–7 days, a multi-channel pre-call sequence that scores lead heat, and calendar economics that give the best reps the best leads.
**Nuggets:**
- `[FUNNEL]` Flow: traffic (paid/organic) → application page (direct offer, no opt-in gate) → application with friction/routing → booking page → pre-call page that sets expectations → pre-call reminder/qualification sequence → calendar-availability economics → sales call (discovery + close). ([1:31](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=w5bYv2CBDZE&t=91s))
- `[KPIS]` Build identical but separate funnels per source (Meta, Google, IG organic, YouTube organic, email) to track lead quality and revenue by source. Their organic leads convert ~3× better than paid. ([2:33](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=w5bYv2CBDZE&t=153s))
- `[FINANCE]` Cautionary tale: blending sources hid that ~60% of revenue came from organic. He scaled ads from ~$50K to $500K+/mo at ~$700K/mo topline and was nearly personally bankrupt within 3 months because cold traffic wasn't closing. Minimum: separate organic and paid funnels. ([3:04](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=w5bYv2CBDZE&t=184s))
- `[LANDING-PAGE]` Page structure (tested over hundreds of iterations and millions in spend): brand name/logo, quick reputation plug, headline, optional video, apply button, the application, then case studies and testimonials for skeptics. No complex layout or walls of text. Hormozi workshops, Codie Sanchez's Contrarian Thinking and Hyros use the same structure. ([3:35](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=w5bYv2CBDZE&t=215s))
- `[LANDING-PAGE]` The headline is the most important copy in the business: the whole offer in 1–2 lines, built around the single thing that sells best now (even if you offer a dozen services), e.g. "Get all your content done quickly" or "1 million views or you don't pay." ([5:40](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=w5bYv2CBDZE&t=340s))
- `[VSL]` A VSL isn't always needed — skip it where before/afters say more (landscaping, cosmetic). Test it for coaching, consulting and marketing, where the person on camera must build trust. Always test without a VSL first, because a good one is hard to make. ([6:11](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=w5bYv2CBDZE&t=371s))
- `[VSL]` Structure: 0–15s what you do and the benefit; 15–30s why it's better or different from what they've tried; 30–45s proof/testimonials; 45–60s recap and CTA. The remaining 3–10 minutes repeat, word for word, the lines that get the best responses on sales calls. ([7:13](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=w5bYv2CBDZE&t=433s))
- `[VSL]` From social traffic, attention drops around 2.5–3 minutes, so cram the critical info into that window, then add ~2 minutes for skeptics: budget/authority/need/timing objections, value reinforcement and comparison with alternatives. End with one CTA (complete the application below). ([8:13](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=w5bYv2CBDZE&t=493s))
- `[CASE-STUDIES]` "No amount of proof beats promise" — in testing, a flood of testimonials beat bold guarantees and promises almost every time. With no client results yet, use your own result or one from a previous company, organised in one place. ([9:17](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=w5bYv2CBDZE&t=557s))
- `[APPLICATION]` Friction: qualifying questions (e.g. monthly revenue) raise cost per lead but improve lead quality — people willing to disclose are less skeptical and easier to sell. ([9:49](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=w5bYv2CBDZE&t=589s))
- `[APPLICATION]` Routing by revenue: <$10K/mo → free training (no product for them); $10–50K → reps with more time per lead; $50K+ (~$1M/yr) → most experienced reps; $250K+ → flagged for special treatment. ([10:20](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=w5bYv2CBDZE&t=620s))
- `[ADS]` Each routing outcome lands on a different thank-you page with its own pixel, so Meta only gets positive feedback from qualified leads. Meta optimises for the cheapest leads, which overlap heavily with the worst, so routing is how you "put up the dartboard/bullseye." Current gates: revenue, recent marketing spend capacity, high client LTV, ready to start now (not price shopping). ([11:21](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=w5bYv2CBDZE&t=681s))
- `[APPLICATION]` Two levers when leads are too expensive or too poor: friction (add/remove qualifying questions) and routing (which leads fire the pixel). Adjust them to marketing costs and sales-team availability. ([12:53](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=w5bYv2CBDZE&t=773s))
- `[FUNNEL]` Booking window: 2–7 days out (occasionally 8–9), never weeks — show rates collapse. B2C needs shorter windows; home services should call back the same hour. ([13:24](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=w5bYv2CBDZE&t=804s))
- `[SETTING]` Within 5 minutes of booking: confirmation email with the meeting link plus an intro text from the assigned rep ("this is me, quick question…") asking a qualifying question; ideally a setter/assistant intro call to confirm the time and gather info so the rep arrives prepped. ([14:25](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=w5bYv2CBDZE&t=865s))
- `[EMAIL]` Reminder cadence: email 24h before, text 3h before, email 1h before, text 30 min before, each with the link — alternating channels. 100+ automations at scale, but start simple. ([15:28](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=w5bYv2CBDZE&t=928s))
- `[KPIS]` Engagement predicts show + qualified: no email open and no text reply ≈ 3%; engaging on only one channel ≈ 14–16%; opened email and replied to the rep's text ≈ 42%. ([15:59](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=w5bYv2CBDZE&t=959s))
- `[SALES-MGMT]` Use that heat score to move cold leads from top reps to newer reps or an assistant to pre-qualify and rebook; unreachable leads get cancelled so the slot goes to a responsive prospect. Goal: best reps get the most qualified leads every day. Once a lead confirms, ease off reminders. ([16:29](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=w5bYv2CBDZE&t=989s))
- `[LANDING-PAGE]` Simple sells: their VSL isn't highly produced, and friends doing $3–10M/mo use near-blank white pages with a video. The page that converts wins, not the fanciest; test a few variations and let the data decide. ([18:04](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=w5bYv2CBDZE&t=1084s))
**Short quotes:**
- "This spot on your website should have the most important line of text in your entire business." ([6:11](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=w5bYv2CBDZE&t=371s))
- "It's not the fanciest page that wins. It's the one that converts the best." ([18:34](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=w5bYv2CBDZE&t=1114s))
**G&L angle:**
- Build separate funnels and pixels for paid Meta, organic (LinkedIn/YouTube/podcast) and referral/email so you know which source actually produces buyer and seller mandates — don't scale paid on the back of organic-driven revenue.
- Application friction and routing for G&L: sellers by revenue/EBITDA, age/exit timeline and ownership; buyers by liquid capital, credit and timeline. Route low-capital buyers to free training or a low-ticket product, mid-tier to newer closers, and $1M+ EBITDA sellers or well-capitalised buyers to the senior advisor. Fire the pixel only on qualified outcomes.
- Headline = the one line that sells best (e.g. "Buy a profitable business with little money down using seller finance" / "Sell your business for top dollar in 12 months"); split into buyer and seller pages rather than listing every service.
- VSL: first 60 seconds = what/why different (vs brokers, vs SBA-only)/proof (closed deals)/CTA, then 2+ minutes using winning sales-call word tracks and B/A/N/T objections; lead with deal testimonials over guarantees.
- Book calls 2–7 days out, rep intro text within 5 minutes, alternate email/SMS reminders, and use engagement scores to route cold bookings to a setter.

## Brutally Honest Advice I Wish I Heard When I Started — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=VhlBAF8isF8
**Gist:** A mindset short (after $10M in 90 days): hate losing more than you want to win, treat attempts as free lottery tickets, let the "burning house" push you more than the "dream home" pull, go all-in with no plan B, and use past rock-bottom to stay even-keeled about small problems.
**Nuggets:**
- `[MINDSET]` Context: the company made $10M in the last 90 days. ([0:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=VhlBAF8isF8&t=0s))
- `[MINDSET]` Hate losing more than you want to win. Wins are rare, so if wins are your only measure of progress you won't last the years it takes. ([0:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=VhlBAF8isF8&t=0s))
- `[MINDSET]` Attempts are free lottery tickets you can buy unlimited amounts of: a post or a cold DM costs only time. People avoid trying out of fear of failure. ([0:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=VhlBAF8isF8&t=0s))
- `[SALES-CALL]` "People will run out of a burning house faster than they run towards their dream home" (a line from one of his sales consultants). The push (pain, urgency) always beats the pull (goals); make your current position uncomfortable enough to force change. ([0:30](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=VhlBAF8isF8&t=30s))
- `[MINDSET]` Sometimes you win by taking more risk than anyone else (his mother emigrated from Ukraine with nothing). He lost the $1M liquid he started with plus another $500K and kept going because he had no backup plan. Real commitment means no plan B, C or D, like marriage. ([2:03](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=VhlBAF8isF8&t=123s))
- `[LEADERSHIP]` Having hit rock bottom (near bankruptcy, family at risk) gives perspective. Employees' mistakes, even theft or poor performance, rarely upset him because none exceed his own past mistakes; that keeps expectations grounded and him even-keeled. ([3:35](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=VhlBAF8isF8&t=215s))
- `[MINDSET]` His only regret is not learning, and risking, sooner. Hard things are the price of success; the more you're willing to pay, the likelier the prize. ([5:09](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=VhlBAF8isF8&t=309s))
**Short quotes:**
- "People will run out of a burning house faster than they run towards their dream home." ([0:30](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=VhlBAF8isF8&t=30s))
- "You have to hate losing more than you ever want to win." ([0:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=VhlBAF8isF8&t=0s))
**G&L angle:**
- Use "burning house" over "dream home" in G&L copy and on calls. For buyers: job insecurity, a wage ceiling, AI layoffs. For 55+ owners: no succession plan, health risk, a business worth less every year it isn't exit-ready. Lead with the push and use freedom and retirement as the pull.
- Train closers to quantify the cost of staying put before presenting the dream outcome.
- For the team: frame volume of outreach and content as free lottery tickets. Reward attempts, not just closed deals, during long M&A cycles.

## Business lessons I learned scaling to 3 million a month — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=kkqcPEj0-po
**Gist:** A long interview-style list of lessons from scaling Viral Coach to $3M/month. Topics: pick a market that already buys (switching from coaches to traditional businesses doubled revenue monthly for six months), take bigger risks as you grow (20% of ad spend for testing), use organic plus paid together, price by doubling until you lose half the customers, fund aggressive reinvestment, measure everything, cost of acquisition vs LTV, hire for context, and why copycats fail without fulfilment.
**Nuggets:**
- `[OFFER]` Choose a market that already buys. "Viral Coach" first targeted coaches and consultants, who mostly have no money. After switching offer and audience to traditional businesses (already big buyers of marketing), revenue doubled every month for six months: $80k to $160k to about $300k to about $600k to $1.2M ([0:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=kkqcPEj0-po&t=0s)).
- `[OFFER]` Market and offer can't be separated; change both together. The coaching/info market is like Etsy: a few winners, a poor majority, and a lot of showing off ([1:33](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=kkqcPEj0-po&t=93s)).
- `[OFFER]` Validate by moving proven demand down-market. Fortune 50 brands (PayPal, Amazon, Fidelity, Robinhood, Epson) paid him $10–20k per 30-second video year after year. He took the same offer to small and mid businesses ($1–50M revenue), where it doubled or tripled their marketing efficiency ([3:37](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=kkqcPEj0-po&t=217s)).
- `[OFFER]` Next he plans to specialise in offline US service businesses based on ability to pay. He left influencer work because the feast-or-famine brand budgets (Q4 heavy, Q2–Q3 dry) meant he couldn't control his income. Build a business where you control the lead flow ([5:39](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=kkqcPEj0-po&t=339s)).
- `[OFFER]` Saturated markets give you many companies to model. Unsaturated ones let you borrow the best ideas and beat weak competition. Avoid anything completely untested as a beginner ([7:42](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=kkqcPEj0-po&t=462s)).
- `[SCALING-STAGES]` Founders stuck at $2–4M/month have become risk-averse because they now have something to lose. Set an explicit risk budget, like a stop-loss, and stick to it ([9:13](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=kkqcPEj0-po&t=553s)).
- `[ADS]` Why people plateau: they stop digging into data, and they won't fund testing once ROAS starts dropping with scale. Set aside a ruthless 20–50% of budget for experiments. At about $1M/month spend, 20% ($200k) goes to testing that is expected to lose money ([11:16](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=kkqcPEj0-po&t=676s)).
- `[HIRING]` Bad hires are also a cost of risk: they recently let go an operations director and a full-time recruiter after investing tens of thousands in each ([12:49](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=kkqcPEj0-po&t=769s)).
- `[FUNNEL]` You need all three stages: top (attention), middle (trust/brand) and bottom (direct response). A great top and middle are worthless without a bottom. Over-asking for the sale "dries up the ocean". Never sell to every last person; keep brand equity in reserve because it compounds exponentially (MrBeast's subscriber curve) ([14:21](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=kkqcPEj0-po&t=861s)).
- `[ADS]` Organic plus paid ("2+2=10"): organic covers top and middle of funnel for free, so paid budget can go almost entirely to bottom-of-funnel conversion and retargeting. Use only creative that has already won organically in paid tests ([16:52](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=kkqcPEj0-po&t=1012s)).
- `[OFFER]` "Service" offers (trust in a person, needing long sales calls) vs "commodity" offers (clear like a plank of wood, sellable from a 30-second cold ad). Cole Gordon's offers sell cold. Organic-only coaches use paid only to retarget their existing audience because their offers can't sell cold ([17:53](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=kkqcPEj0-po&t=1073s)).
- `[OFFER]` Building an audience and building a product are opposite skills. Audience-only coaches are often poor operators. Market leaders do both well ([20:26](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=kkqcPEj0-po&t=1226s)).
- `[PRICING]` Don't use "value-based" pricing, which is subjective. Test by doubling the price ($1k to $2k) for a day or a week depending on sample size and watch volume. If you lose less than half the customers, double again. Losing exactly half gives the same revenue with half the fulfilment, overhead and admin, so much more profit ([22:28](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=kkqcPEj0-po&t=1348s)).
- `[KPIS]` For a long time they closed about 40% of organic calls and under 10% of paid calls. Set price for the paid (cold) audience, since that's the revenue you control. If strangers won't pay, improve the product rather than lean on fans ([24:31](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=kkqcPEj0-po&t=1471s)).
- `[OFFER]` Revenue mix as an offer test: if 90% comes from organic, the offer works only on warm audiences. If 99% comes from paid despite a big organic audience, the offer sells well cold ([27:04](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=kkqcPEj0-po&t=1624s)).
- `[FINANCE]` Self-funded and aggressive: he drained his savings and was $500k in debt at the start, and now reinvests all profit into ads. Spend went $800k to $1.2M to $1.5M a month, and revenue $1.4M to $3M to a hoped-for $5M ([28:36](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=kkqcPEj0-po&t=1716s)).
- `[ADS]` Sacrificing profit for speed gets you data faster than competitors (the biggest spender learns fastest and wins) and raises their costs. They make several dozen creatives a week, limited by how much budget they can put into testing ([29:38](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=kkqcPEj0-po&t=1778s)).
- `[CREATIVE]` Split test across about 100 videos in 2025: minimal editing (A-roll only, plain Arial text, no music or effects) beat heavy editing (B-roll, dynamic text, sound effects) both organically and in paid with the same creative. Test hypotheses organically for free first ([30:39](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=kkqcPEj0-po&t=1839s)).
- `[CREATIVE]` Not every organic hit makes a good ad. A top-of-funnel video built for views may have a great CTR and not convert. Judge it by the metric that matches its job ([34:14](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=kkqcPEj0-po&t=2054s)).
- `[KPIS]` Their cost benchmarks: about $450 per booked call, about $1,500 per qualified call that shows, and about 15% of those close. At the start it was $50 per call and about $100 per qualified lead. A "whale" spending $1M+/month pushes up auction costs for everyone in the niche ([35:15](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=kkqcPEj0-po&t=2115s)).
- `[KPIS]` The two metrics that matter: cost to acquire a customer (sales and marketing) against lifetime value (fulfilment, meaning how much value you can deliver and charge for). Their ratio drives profit ([37:17](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=kkqcPEj0-po&t=2237s)).
- `[LIFE]` Status symbols (Porsche, Dubai, Bali) wear off fast. His Porsche sits with a dead battery and he drives an 8-minute commute in a truck. Sophisticated buyers aren't impressed by flexing ([38:18](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=kkqcPEj0-po&t=2298s)).
- `[MINDSET]` If you feel like a fake "guru", charge less or do it free until you have enough results to charge confidently. Selling to beginners isn't unethical; they need help most ([40:50](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=kkqcPEj0-po&t=2450s)).
- `[OFFER]` Clients who pay more (especially as a share of their wealth) are more committed and easier to work with. Big B2B clients have lawyers, and contracts protect you: when clients try to steal, their lawyer recovers penalties ([42:51](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=kkqcPEj0-po&t=2571s)).
- `[OTHER]` To land enterprise clients, hire people who managed those accounts. Cold-pitching PayPal got him blacklisted. The mass market is far more scalable through ads ([43:53](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=kkqcPEj0-po&t=2633s)).
- `[SCALING-STAGES]` Growth path: exhaust direct paid ads (he thinks $10M/month is realistic doing the same thing), then brand building, then buying companies doing $1M/month to add revenue instantly (Neil Patel's roll-up of marketing agencies). Acquisitions need 1–2 years of audit and deal structuring ([45:55](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=kkqcPEj0-po&t=2755s)).
- `[ADS]` Channels aren't "exhausted" when one metric dips. He reads patterns across hundreds of KPIs. Before declaring a ceiling, try seriously for about a year and bring in new experts; a new head of paid media from e-commerce brought fresh testing ideas ([48:27](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=kkqcPEj0-po&t=2907s)).
- `[OBJECTIONS]` Refund rates track the market's demographics more than the product. They deliberately avoid certain geographic segments whose expectations don't fit ([50:31](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=kkqcPEj0-po&t=3031s)).
- `[ORGANIC]` They've ring-fenced two years of salaries for long-form YouTube. After 3 months: 6,000 followers, but connections (podcasts, collaborations) he used to pay $18–24k each to get. Long form is hard to fake, so know your subject first. They re-shot and re-edited a video three times rather than publish below standard ([52:03](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=kkqcPEj0-po&t=3123s)).
- `[OPERATIONS]` Speed: set up tests with fast feedback loops. Waiting months for results is less reliable than checking immediately ([56:07](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=kkqcPEj0-po&t=3367s)).
- `[KPIS]` "Moneyball" management: judge reps by revenue per lead, not by how they talk or look. Founders shouldn't impose their style if someone else gets the same result differently ([57:08](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=kkqcPEj0-po&t=3428s)).
- `[HIRING]` Measure cost-centre roles too. Before firing a recruiter on "feel", he counted critical errors in the last 30 days reported by colleagues. The legal team (about $30k/month budget) is tracked on open cases, cases resolved, money recovered, response time and quality ([59:10](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=kkqcPEj0-po&t=3550s)).
- `[LEADERSHIP]` "Everything is your problem": taking responsibility gives you the power to act. Even an ad-account ban is on you, because you published something that triggered it ([1:02:14](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=kkqcPEj0-po&t=3734s)).
- `[MINDSET]` His biggest investment regret: selling a multi-million-dollar real-estate portfolio (built by age 22) to buy an expensive primary home instead of investing in his own education five years sooner ([1:04:16](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=kkqcPEj0-po&t=3856s)).
- `[HIRING]` The progression: courses, then mentors (pay by the hour for specific questions), then hire experts (a 10-year paid-media veteran gives you ten years of experience overnight), then buy whole teams or companies (e.g. a PR firm) ([1:07:20](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=kkqcPEj0-po&t=4040s)).
- `[HIRING]` Hire for context. Candidates suited to a $50M/month company couldn't help them; a cheaper hire who had solved their exact CRM migration and Python problems could. Customer success comes from corporate account management, not social-media celebrities ([1:09:22](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=kkqcPEj0-po&t=4162s)).
- `[TEAM]` A sales rep with hundreds of thousands of followers failed at selling because prospects felt "you're too far ahead". As a brand representative she now produces dozens of ad creatives a month. Move stars into the role that fits ([1:12:27](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=kkqcPEj0-po&t=4347s)).
- `[OFFER]` Traffic and offer are linked: a better offer (a rare Ferrari vs a Prius) creates its own traffic and gets shared. Copying an offer fails without the fulfilment. A copycat's 7-day refund guarantee lasted about two weeks ([1:13:58](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=kkqcPEj0-po&t=4438s)).
- `[FULFILLMENT]` Back-end delivery is tailored to each client's needs (not one-size-fits-all), so it can't be copied by watching the front end. Real manual work wins ([1:17:02](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=kkqcPEj0-po&t=4622s)).
- `[ADS]` Openings for competitors: they spend $1M+/month on Meta but only tens of thousands testing YouTube/TikTok and nothing on LinkedIn, Pinterest, Snapchat or cold email. Compete on the platform the leader isn't using ([1:18:37](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=kkqcPEj0-po&t=4717s)).
- `[MINDSET]` Success comes from capital, innate talent and risk appetite. He started with a few hundred thousand dollars, borrowed hundreds of thousands more, and was willing to try for a decade ([1:20:40](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=kkqcPEj0-po&t=4840s)).
- `[PRICING]` Revenue share or equity deals only make sense with very large companies (Fortune 5000 / Intuit size). With small businesses they end in either no result or lawsuits ([1:22:10](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=kkqcPEj0-po&t=4930s)).
**Short quotes:**
- "you should never want to dry up this ocean" ([15:21](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=kkqcPEj0-po&t=921s))
- "The winner is the one who can spend the most on customer acquisition." ([30:08](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=kkqcPEj0-po&t=1808s))
**G&L angle:**
- Pick the market that already pays. Owners aged 55+ with $1–10M revenue businesses already pay brokers and advisors; would-be buyers with no capital resemble Daniel's broke coaches. Weight the ad budget toward audiences with money: sellers, and buyers who have $50k+ to deploy.
- Daniel's own long-term roadmap is to buy companies ($1M/month businesses, with 1–2 year audit and structuring), which supports G&L's thesis. Use his "buy instead of build" logic in seller and buyer messaging.
- Test price by doubling the advisory fee for a short window. If fewer than half of buyers drop off, raise it again. Set price on cold paid leads, not warm referrals.
- Track CAC vs LTV and the cost per booked, qualified-and-showed and closed call (Daniel: $450 booked, $1,500 qualified-and-showed, about 15% close). Budget 20% of ad spend for creative and audience tests.
- Hire account managers who match G&L's clients (former bankers, accountants, corporate development people) rather than flashy influencers, and use LinkedIn, where Daniel isn't, for owner and seller acquisition.

## What did I learn making $3M in one month? (Q&A) — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=YkEuo4P6vKs
**Gist:** A rapid-fire Q&A about hiring, how he allocates cash during hypergrowth, goals vs process, sunk costs and forecasting. The key admission is that he lost two reps and missed his $5M-month target because he staffed sales with no margin for churn.
**Nuggets:**
- `[HIRING]` If you have money and want aggressive growth, hire people better than you at the top of the market. They outperform from day one, while training juniors takes months or years. Only grow talent slowly if you have more time than money. ([0:30](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=YkEuo4P6vKs&t=30s))
- `[HIRING]` Rather than paying with his own time for coaching, he hires someone who already has the skill, or puts an employee through the coaching. ([1:01](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=YkEuo4P6vKs&t=61s))
- `[FINANCE]` His cash split: of about $2M coming in, about 50% goes back into ads (more next month if it grows). Of the remaining ~$1M, half goes to existing staff, a quarter to new hires, and a quarter to one-off costs like flying people in and training. This let them grow from $1.4M to $3M. ([2:03](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=YkEuo4P6vKs&t=123s))
- `[MINDSET]` Action beats reading. He read 50 books in 6 months and bought less real estate because of it. ([3:07](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=YkEuo4P6vKs&t=187s))
- `[SCALING-STAGES]` Turn a revenue goal into a chain of constraints. $10M a month needs about 3× ad spend → then about 2× sales reps to take the calls → then more CS and fulfillment staff so the revenue doesn't turn into refunds and chargebacks. Plan for the consequences further down the line too. ([3:38](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=YkEuo4P6vKs&t=218s))
- `[TOOLS]` He judges new software or programs by opportunity cost. The question isn't whether it would work, but whether the time spent implementing it beats doing more of what already works. ([5:40](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=YkEuo4P6vKs&t=340s))
- `[MINDSET]` Avoid the sunk-cost fallacy with offers, funnels and price points. "How it's always been done" is the least likely way to be optimal, so stay flexible. ([6:42](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=YkEuo4P6vKs&t=402s))
- `[SALES-MGMT]` Staff sales capacity above target to allow for churn. He staffed exactly enough reps for $5.1M, lost two of them (a fifth of capacity), and fell short. Build in buffer headcount. ([8:14](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=YkEuo4P6vKs&t=494s))
- `[KPIS]` The skill of a top operator is predicting outcomes with numbers. Improve it like a scientist: state a hypothesis, run it, compare the prediction with the result, and refine. ([9:16](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=YkEuo4P6vKs&t=556s))
**Short quotes:**
- "I staffed enough people to hit 5.1 million. I didn't staff enough people to hit 5.4 with margin for error." ([8:46](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=YkEuo4P6vKs&t=526s))
- "The ability to predict things in business quantitatively… is one of the strongest indicators of success for an operator." ([9:47](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=YkEuo4P6vKs&t=587s))
**G&L angle:**
- Build a constraint chain for G&L's revenue target: ad spend → applications → booked calls → closer capacity, and then deal-advisory capacity (analysts, deal managers, legal partners) so signed clients actually close deals.
- Hire closers with roughly 20% more capacity than the target needs, because rep churn is guaranteed.
- Write down a monthly forecast (leads, show rate, close rate, cash) and compare it with actuals to sharpen predictions.
- Put a fixed share of cash collected back into ads and hiring each month, as Daniel does.

## I lost millions learning these harsh business lessons — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=9XPfyJpWA6A
**Gist:** Five expensive mistakes from his past year: copying peers' CRM, hiring execs without set KPIs, selling two price points, not learning the customer well enough, and running only one creative style. Killing the downsell alone took them from $1.4M to $3M a month.
**Nuggets:**
- `[TOOLS]` Don't copy your friends' tech stack. He spent $100K over 6 months migrating to HubSpot, the sales team hated it, and he rolled back within 14 hours to Google Sheets plus GHL. Going back saved $10K a month in software and an estimated $1M+ a month in deals lost to slowness. Pick tools that fit your sales process, not the most expensive ones. ([0:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=9XPfyJpWA6A&t=0s))
- `[HIRING]` Never let a senior hire define their own KPIs. They choose easy targets or none. He did this twice, with $24K-a-month and $30K-a-month roles filled by proven people, and both failed. Set the KPIs before hiring. ([1:31](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=9XPfyJpWA6A&t=91s))
- `[HIRING]` Existing staff with context about the business often beat expensive outside execs. His team got similar results at a third of the budget. A billion-dollar company told him Fortune 50 hires failed them the same way. ([2:33](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=9XPfyJpWA6A&t=153s))
- `[OFFER]` Sell one price point. Having two products, one 6× the price of the other, confused prospects (they bought neither) and reps (unsure which to pitch). Removing the cheaper one raised revenue per lead 40% immediately, and the next month they went from $1.4M to $3M. ([3:03](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=9XPfyJpWA6A&t=183s))
- `[KPIS]` To test this yourself, have one rep (or part of the month) pitch only the single offer and compare revenue per lead with the rest of the team. ([4:06](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=9XPfyJpWA6A&t=246s))
- `[APPLICATION]` With enough data (thousands of applications reviewed, hundreds of clients served), application answers alone predict how much a lead will be worth before the call. This is an advantage that comes with scale. ([4:36](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=9XPfyJpWA6A&t=276s))
- `[OFFER]` Let lead data change your target market. Coaches and consultants turned out to be broke, so they moved to online business owners and then to local service businesses. Those have no social media competitors in their zip code, so results are easier (e.g. $500/day ads → 10× ROAS). ([5:06](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=9XPfyJpWA6A&t=306s))
- `[CREATIVE]` One ad style doesn't scale. At $1M+ a month in spend you need hundreds of fully unique creatives across formats (1:1, 9:16, carousel) and styles, with different people on camera: 2 full-time on-camera employees, contractor talent and dedicated graphic designers. They run 300+ active ads and aim for 1,000. A diverse creative pool lets Meta optimize and cuts costs. ([6:08](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=9XPfyJpWA6A&t=368s))
**Short quotes:**
- "Do not outsource your decision-making to people who do not have your best interests in mind." ([1:01](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=9XPfyJpWA6A&t=61s))
- "Simplicity scales at every level." ([3:03](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=9XPfyJpWA6A&t=183s))
**G&L angle:**
- Pick one core program per ICP and price for it. Avoid pitching a cheap "course" and a full advisory together on the same call. If you keep a downsell, A/B test it per rep on revenue per lead.
- Score applications on the factors that predict deal value: liquidity, industry, timeline, buyer vs seller, and the owner's business revenue/EBITDA. Use the scores to route leads and to keep refining your target market. For example, sellers with $1–5M EBITDA may be worth far more than first-time buyers.
- Set KPIs for every hire (deal manager, head of sales) before they start.
- Stay on a sales-friendly CRM (e.g. GHL) unless your multi-party deal flow genuinely needs HubSpot-style complexity.
- Build a creative engine: several faces (advisors, past buyers and sellers), formats and angles, not just founder talking heads.

## I failed... — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=aUgSDt1Kp4g
**Gist:** Daniel and his wife/CMO Noel break down why their November push from $3M to $5M/month failed: ad spend rose from $800K to $1.1M with zero extra revenue. Causes were an emotional, arbitrary goal set two weeks out, no creative pipeline, sales-rep capacity gaps and ignoring holiday seasonality; the fix was a planned maintenance phase, a creative team, rebuilt fulfillment and an always-on sales hiring pipeline.
**Nuggets:**
- `[LEADERSHIP]` Revenue = leads × conversion; marketing (Noel) owns leads, sales (Daniel) owns conversion, and between them they own all revenue. A goal set emotionally, without the underlying criteria worked out, puts the business at risk. ([0:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=aUgSDt1Kp4g&t=0s))
- `[SCALING-STAGES]` After doubling from $1.4M to $3M in 30 days they set a $5M goal two weeks before November. Spend went from $800K to $1.1M (+~30%), sales were flat, and ~$300K vanished. Big pushes should be planned months/quarters ahead, not sporadically. ([2:35](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=aUgSDt1Kp4g&t=155s))
- `[ADS]` More spend hits diminishing returns unless you prepare: they needed to roughly double creatives (an estimated 200–300 for that spend) — multiple faces, scripts, formats, lengths, image ads — which takes months, not two weeks. Plan creatives tested per week, account structure and the testing vs proven-creative budget split. ([4:07](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=aUgSDt1Kp4g&t=247s))
