- `[MINDSET]` Big goals break into ~50 sub-goals (the 500-lb deadlift needs years of training); set the goal *and* map the prerequisite work. ([6:12](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=aUgSDt1Kp4g&t=372s))
- `[SALES-MGMT]` He knew the required reps, calls per rep, revenue per lead and revenue per call (overall and by rep), but didn't plan for variance: a new rep was removed after about a week for low revenue per lead, leaving calendars full with nowhere for leads to go. ([7:15](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=aUgSDt1Kp4g&t=435s))
- `[FUNNEL]` Calendar availability drives lead cost: if prospects see no open slots they leave, raising CAC and forcing spend cuts. Sales capacity must lead marketing spend. ([8:17](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=aUgSDt1Kp4g&t=497s))
- `[SALES-HIRING]` Their tier-2 sales lead (~$700K topline the previous month, ~$30K commission) quit a week into the month — a third of the goal gone. Rep churn is predictable at some rate, so plan for it. ([8:47](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=aUgSDt1Kp4g&t=527s))
- `[LEADERSHIP]` Some things are outside your control but nothing has to be outside your responsibility; owning everything is what gives you control next time. ([9:19](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=aUgSDt1Kp4g&t=559s))
- `[OBJECTIONS]` Seasonality: Thanksgiving week was lost and revenue per call dropped mid-November. Leads wanted to start in the new year — more authority objections (stakeholders on holiday) and timing objections ("I'll pick this up in Q1"). ([9:50](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=aUgSDt1Kp4g&t=590s))
- `[KPIS]` Nov–Dec is rarely a growth window for professional/B2B services; moving up-market (B2C solopreneurs who bought on Black Friday → B2B companies doing $1–30M/yr) changed seasonal behaviour, so last year's data didn't transfer. ([11:22](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=aUgSDt1Kp4g&t=682s))
- `[SCALING-STAGES]` Not every month must be a growth month: use weak seasons as maintenance phases to prune, find efficiencies and fix the back end so later growth is sustainable — and that work can't be done "on the side" while pushing growth. ([16:04](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=aUgSDt1Kp4g&t=964s))
- `[FULFILLMENT]` In the maintenance month he rewrote a ~50-page account-management handbook, recorded tutorial videos for new reps, interviewed existing staff, promoted top performers and rebuilt comp — fulfillment cost fell to a third while top performers were paid more. ([17:06](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=aUgSDt1Kp4g&t=1026s))
- `[TEAM]` Marketing built an in-house creative engine (junior media buyers for non-Meta channels, a copywriter, a graphic designer) so 100–200 new creatives are always in the backlog for $1–1.5M/mo spend. ([18:07](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=aUgSDt1Kp4g&t=1087s))
- `[SALES-HIRING]` Uses a staffing company: one group interview per week with five highly vetted candidates, hiring 1–2 each time. Deliberately overstaff KPI-measurable roles like sales so new reps keep replacing the weakest and the team stays competitive. ([19:10](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=aUgSDt1Kp4g&t=1150s))
- `[MINDSET]` Levels: pattern recognition ("this happened, I failed") → problem solving ("here's what I'll do differently"). Put ego aside and make every problem yours so it becomes solvable. ([20:13](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=aUgSDt1Kp4g&t=1213s))
**Short quotes:**
- "There will always be things that happen outside of your control. But that doesn't mean there have to be things that happen outside of your responsibility." ([9:19](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=aUgSDt1Kp4g&t=559s))
- "Not every single month has to be a growth month. You have to have periods where you prune the tree." ([16:04](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=aUgSDt1Kp4g&t=964s))
**G&L angle:**
- Before raising G&L ad spend, plan backwards: creatives needed, rep hours and calendar slots, show/close rates — and check closer capacity *before* scaling spend, because full calendars raise CPL.
- Map G&L seasonality: owners 55+ and buyers often stall in late Nov–Dec (holidays, year-end accounts) and many are triggered in January or after tax year-end; time growth pushes for those windows and use quiet months for maintenance (playbooks, deal-process SOPs, comp redesign).
- Expect authority objections (spouse, accountant, business partner) and timing objections ("after year-end"); prepare scripts and get decision-makers on the call.
- Keep an always-on closer hiring pipeline (weekly group interviews) and slightly overstaff sales so one rep leaving doesn't wipe out the month.

## Building a $1,000,000 Business for a Stranger in 40 Minutes — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eWMz7IfiHbE
**Gist:** Daniel brings a community member (Colin, a video-production owner and LinkedIn creator with a $50/mo Skool community) into the office for a live business roast. He splits the advice into (1) a tight qualifying funnel for big-brand production clients and (2) a "free at the bottom, premium at the top" model for the coaching community, with stair-stepped high-ticket pricing.
**Nuggets:**
- `[SCALING-STAGES]` Project work that depends on big clients choosing you once a year (seasonal, Q3/Q4 spikes) is "free money" but not a scalable business — you don't control demand; prioritise the channel you can turn up (impressions, ads). ([4:37](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eWMz7IfiHbE&t=277s))
- `[SCALING-STAGES]` Viral Coach growth path: one-on-one pilot at $20–30K/mo → ~$120K first month → ~$300K+ second → ~$500K third, once offer packaging and audience acquisition were controlled. ([5:10](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eWMz7IfiHbE&t=310s))
- `[FUNNEL]` Build a separate, dedicated funnel per buyer type: "If you're a big business and want me to make ads, click here" → short form capturing what they want, budget, last-90-day marketing spend, current budget for this project, number of assets, whether they want testimonials. ([5:41](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eWMz7IfiHbE&t=341s))
- `[APPLICATION]` The application should financially qualify and pre-frame so nobody arrives on the call surprised by the price (e.g. $30K per video); include timeline questions ("When do you need it live? ~1 month turnaround — does that fit?"). ([6:13](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eWMz7IfiHbE&t=373s))
- `[FUNNEL]` Sophisticated buyers who already know what they want need *less* information and a shorter form; long educational funnels attract the unqualified "tell me what I need" buyer you don't want. ([7:16](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eWMz7IfiHbE&t=436s))
- `[OFFER]` Two scaling paths: aggressive (ads, sales team, admin, fulfilment — Daniel's route; usually requires a concrete, quantified outcome/guarantee in the offer) vs lean lifestyle (organic + cheap software, ~99% margins). Both are valid — pick deliberately. ([11:54](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eWMz7IfiHbE&t=714s))
- `[ORGANIC]` Too many bottom-of-funnel asks on any social platform erode trust and reach; keep public posts top/middle of funnel ("give in public") and sell in private via DMs. ([17:32](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eWMz7IfiHbE&t=1052s))
- `[ORGANIC]` Private DM pattern: "Hope you're getting value — here's a guide tied to my recent post… and here's a way to engage with me more." ([18:02](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eWMz7IfiHbE&t=1082s))
- `[PRICING]` For a sophisticated B2B audience a $50 price is a throwaway; test higher. Track impressions/week, link clicks/week and revenue/week on the page — double the price at half the conversions = same revenue, fewer people to serve. ([19:34](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eWMz7IfiHbE&t=1174s))
- `[FULFILLMENT]` Low price = low skin in the game = members who do nothing. Like SaaS onboarding, your #1 job is getting the customer to *use* the product early, because users stick. ([20:34](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eWMz7IfiHbE&t=1234s))
- `[PRICING]` Play both extremes: give as much as possible free (cheapest in the market) and sell privately at the highest price to the biggest buyers; avoid the crowded middle — unless you're new with a small audience, where the middle is safer. ([23:37](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eWMz7IfiHbE&t=1417s))
- `[PRICING]` High-ticket income instability comes from low sales volume (1 or 0 deals/month). Start at a price where you can reliably close 3–5/month, then stair-step up ~30% at a time (e.g. $12K → $18K → $36K), logging impressions, community size, outreach count and conversion at each price. ([26:09](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eWMz7IfiHbE&t=1569s))
- `[OFFER]` "Pseudo done-for-you": client participates (it's their brand) but you handle technical delivery, heavily leveraged with AI prompts/APIs trained on the client over the engagement; get their consent up-front and hand over the trained tool at the end. Lets you run 5–30+ DFY clients. ([27:42](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eWMz7IfiHbE&t=1662s))
- `[PRICING]` Rule: anything that costs you nothing to deliver, give away free; anything with a marginal cost (custom LLM, tokens, service fees) reserve for paid one-on-one clients at a premium. ([29:14](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eWMz7IfiHbE&t=1754s))
- `[PRICING]` High ticket without high touch only works with a strong personal brand; selling to a cold audience that thinks you're a nobody gives limited pricing power — hence Daniel's ads + sales-team complexity. ([30:46](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eWMz7IfiHbE&t=1846s))
- `[OFFER]` Next step: pilot a free tier (with locked modules teasing the paid tier), and double the existing high-ticket price (e.g. $2K → $6K full service); expect ~5 takers on first offer. ([31:48](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eWMz7IfiHbE&t=1908s))
- `[PRICING]` Announce scheduled price increases (e.g. every quarter) and actually stick to them — it drives urgency and is "great marketing." ([33:21](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eWMz7IfiHbE&t=2001s))
- `[EMAIL]` Do bottom-of-funnel selling inside the owned community (Skool lets you email all members ~every 72 hours) rather than on the public feed; public ask should be only "join the free group." ([34:22](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eWMz7IfiHbE&t=2062s))
- `[ORGANIC]` (Colin's LinkedIn playbook) Train an internal team member rather than an outside ghostwriter; have the whole team post ~3x/week with specifics and stakes; profile = intriguing headline (specific service for specific clients → specific outcome), banner that says "this is for me," About with social proof + CTA. People want "lighthouses, not teachers." ([35:24](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eWMz7IfiHbE&t=2124s))
- `[OFFER]` Daniel as buyer: would pay $20K for a 6-month engagement whose guaranteed outcome is a trained in-house copywriter on *his* payroll who keeps writing after — an asset he keeps, not just followers. ([37:57](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eWMz7IfiHbE&t=2277s))
**Short quotes:**
- "If you're nobody, or if you're selling to a cold audience that thinks you're a nobody, you have limited pricing power." ([30:46](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eWMz7IfiHbE&t=1846s))
- "Anything that I can give away for free, I give away for free. But if something costs me to deliver… I'm going to charge for that." ([28:43](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eWMz7IfiHbE&t=1723s))
**G&L angle:**
- Run two distinct funnels: buy-side (aspiring acquirers) and sell-side (owners 55+). Sell-side owners with real EBITDA know what they want — give them a short, high-qualification form (revenue, EBITDA, timeline to exit, advisor status) instead of the long educational funnel.
- Pre-qualify on the application with capital-available, target deal size and timeline so no one is shocked by the fee on the call.
- Price for 3–5 reliable closes/month, then stair-step the advisory fee ~30% at a time, and publicly announce quarterly price increases.
- Give the education (deal structuring basics, vendor-finance explainers) away free; charge premium for anything with marginal cost — deal sourcing, LOI drafting, valuation, done-with-you deal execution.
- Keep public content top/middle funnel; do selling via DMs and emails to an owned list/community.

## These 64 tools are the secret to my $3M per month business — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=sXQ0KLtz1kA
**Gist:** A department-by-department tour of Viral Coach's software stack at ~$3M/month (sales, HR/finance, marketing, account management, production, ops, tech ops, and Daniel's personal stack), including what they're migrating away from and why. Key operational lessons: record every call, combine contract + payment in one step, beware Stripe holds on refund-heavy businesses, and track VSL drop-off.
**Nuggets:**
- `[TOOLS]` Sales: Dialer.io for setters (replacing Kixie power dialer), Project Blue for blue/iMessage texts, Fathom notetaker to record every sales call (compliance) with instant playback, transcripts and AI search so ops/account managers can find what the client was promised. ([0:30](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=sXQ0KLtz1kA&t=30s))
- `[TOOLS]` CRM: GoHighLevel worked up to ~$3M/month but couldn't handle hundreds of automations (even with Zapier, both maxed out) — migrating to HubSpot for fast sales automations. ([1:01](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=sXQ0KLtz1kA&t=61s))
- `[OPERATIONS]` Slack for everything: 50+ internal channels for ~100 staff plus one-on-one channels per client for onboarding/delivery. ([2:34](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=sXQ0KLtz1kA&t=154s))
- `[FINANCE]` Track client payment status in a database (Airtable): delinquent, approved vs. unapproved pause, ghosting — visible to account managers. HiBob for payroll/HRIS (chosen after reviewing ~20 options); QuickBooks for reconciliation sent to the accountant. ([3:05](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=sXQ0KLtz1kA&t=185s))
- `[HIRING]` LinkedIn Recruiter to source talent; every video doubles as recruiting ("link below to apply"; general careers email for roles not listed). ([3:36](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=sXQ0KLtz1kA&t=216s))
- `[FULFILLMENT]` Account managers moving from Airtable + Chaser (Slack task tool) to ClickUp for client-progress views; decided by the 30+ person AM team after ~6 months testing both. ([5:09](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=sXQ0KLtz1kA&t=309s))
- `[CREATIVE]` "DART" — an internal ChatGPT-wrapper that takes a client's ICP data and outputs ~60 ad scripts; lightly edited, it's how hundreds of creatives ship monthly. ([6:12](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=sXQ0KLtz1kA&t=372s))
- `[TOOLS]` A custom GPT built on the API (~$20–30k to build, large context, trained on proprietary client data) for mass scripting — "hire a developer, do it right" rather than a basic in-app custom GPT; a lighter free version is given away as a lead magnet in their Skool group. ([7:44](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=sXQ0KLtz1kA&t=464s))
- `[FULFILLMENT]` Fathom recordings document every verbal client expectation, so disputes can be resolved by pointing to the exact moment. TextExpander for repetitive onboarding messages. ([7:14](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=sXQ0KLtz1kA&t=434s))
- `[OPERATIONS]` Never take client passwords (risk) — use a third-party platform (Vista Social) for posting/analytics. Frame.io for edit review loops; there must be a back-and-forth review step before anything goes live. ([9:18](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=sXQ0KLtz1kA&t=558s))
- `[OPERATIONS]` Legal protection: a Slack backup tool snapshots client conversations because clients can edit old Slack messages and later claim they asked for something. ([10:50](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=sXQ0KLtz1kA&t=650s))
- `[SALES-CALL]` Put contract and payment link on one page (HubSpot Quotes) instead of separate DocuSign + payment link — fewer post-call steps, fewer drop-offs. ([11:51](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=sXQ0KLtz1kA&t=711s))
- `[CASE-STUDIES]` Collect reviews on multiple platforms (Clutch, G2, Trustpilot) and employee reviews on Glassdoor. ([11:51](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=sXQ0KLtz1kA&t=711s))
- `[FINANCE]` Payment processing: Stripe flagged them for too many voluntary refunds (despite low disputes) and held a percentage of revenue for ~3 months; they moved via Zoho to HubSpot payments, negotiating a better rate at multi-million monthly volume, with better recurring-billing automation. ([13:52](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=sXQ0KLtz1kA&t=832s))
- `[OBJECTIONS]` Adding a B2B buy-now-pay-later financing provider for contracts to handle budget objections. ([14:55](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=sXQ0KLtz1kA&t=895s))
- `[VSL]` Host VSL and case-study videos on Vidalytics to see how much people watch and where they drop off. ([16:25](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=sXQ0KLtz1kA&t=985s))
- `[TOOLS]` Ops/tech: Scribe for auto-SOPs, Skool for training content, LastPass for password sharing, Framer (struggling; may move to WordPress/custom), Figma; tech stack includes n8n + scraper to pull competitors' top reels, Cloudflare, OpenAI/OpenRouter/Gemini, Notion, Zapier. ([12:52](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=sXQ0KLtz1kA&t=772s))
- `[LEADERSHIP]` CEO stack: Slack constantly, Google Sheets always open for reporting, Google Docs, and AI (Claude for copywriting) to argue against his messages and make them clearer before sending. ([16:55](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=sXQ0KLtz1kA&t=1015s))
**Short quotes:**
- "We have the ads manager, our million-dollar-per-month money furnace." ([4:39](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=sXQ0KLtz1kA&t=279s))
- "Anyone telling you that an editor should just be able to edit a video and make it go live has no clue what they're talking about." ([10:20](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=sXQ0KLtz1kA&t=620s))
**G&L angle:**
- Record every G&L sales and advisory call (Fathom or similar) — vital for compliance and for proving what was and wasn't promised about deal outcomes and seller finance.
- Send a single "quote" link with engagement letter + payment together at the end of the call; offer a financing/BNPL option for the advisory fee.
- Host the VSL on Vidalytics (or equivalent) and review drop-off points weekly to edit the script.
- Choose a processor ready for high-ticket, refund-capable B2B advisory fees (avoid reserve holds) before scaling volume.
- Track each client's deal pipeline/status (target sourced, LOI, due diligence, close; or seller exit-readiness stages) in ClickUp/HubSpot with dedicated client channels.

## A raw conversation about scaling to $5m/mo — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=CLJ9AnFuSZk
**Gist:** A rapid-fire Q&A in which Daniel (forecasting $5.2M that month) agrees or disagrees with common business advice. Topics: when to stop buying courses and start hiring, focus, positioning at the top or bottom of the market, anchor offers, guarantee economics, contract length, why he dropped downsells, close rate as a pricing signal, pre-call over-communication, ads vs referrals, Meta testing structure, the 15% rule against ad fatigue, email's small share of revenue, and hiring and culture through transparent stats.
**Nuggets:**
- `[SCALING-STAGES]` Revenue "plateaus" like the $1–3M/year "swamp" are made up and vary by industry. His media buyer (his wife) said $5k/day was impossible, then $10k, $20k, $30k, $40k; the budget is now $50k/day ([0:30](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=CLJ9AnFuSZk&t=30s)).
- `[HIRING]` Under about $100k/month, spend on your own education and learn the skills yourself, because good employees won't work for you yet and you can't afford them. Above $100k/month, stop buying courses and hire people who already have the skill, e.g. hire someone who knows TikTok ads instead of putting your marketing director through a $10k course and three weeks of her time ([9:10](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=CLJ9AnFuSZk&t=550s)).
- `[MINDSET]` He credits most of his success to a few hundred thousand dollars spent on education and mentors in the business's first 6 months. Spending tens of thousands on courses turned his economist wife into their media buyer ([7:08](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=CLJ9AnFuSZk&t=428s)).
- `[MINDSET]` People under $100k/month spend months thinking things over. Take action to get data. You can't build a good product without enough customers, and you are a single data point ([10:41](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=CLJ9AnFuSZk&t=641s)).
- `[MINDSET]` Rather than inventing from scratch, go work for (or study the clients of) someone already successful in the field, gather the proof ("I worked with 100–200 clients and ran these budgets"), then start your own business ([12:13](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=CLJ9AnFuSZk&t=733s)).
- `[HIRING]` For uncertain decisions (e.g. a hire), keep collecting information through extra trial tasks or interviews until you're confident. He says he is still wrong about half the time. If you decide fast, be willing to reverse fast ([13:44](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=CLJ9AnFuSZk&t=824s)).
- `[MINDSET]` You can only have one priority. Don't use a cash-flow business to fund a second core business like real estate. If the business is great, reinvest in it. If you have a short window (e.g. a fad info business), go all in and outsource personal chores ([16:47](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=CLJ9AnFuSZk&t=1007s)).
- `[PRICING]` Be the cheapest or the most expensive; the middle gets crushed. Sub-$100k businesses usually can't sell to the richest buyers until they have social proof, and the shortcut is borrowing proof from work at a leading competitor ([19:55](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=CLJ9AnFuSZk&t=1195s)).
- `[PRICING]` Create a realistic premium anchor offer. A 1% conversion on something 100x the price doubles revenue. Every category has a most-expensive option (e.g. Tiffany glassware) ([21:26](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=CLJ9AnFuSZk&t=1286s)).
- `[ADS]` Add new channels next to proven ones rather than replacing them, and hire a dedicated person. Diminishing returns on Meta at $40k+/day (5x ROAS) made TikTok at $3k/day (13x ROAS) worth adding ([24:30](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=CLJ9AnFuSZk&t=1470s)).
- `[ORGANIC]` He started YouTube to reach sophisticated owners and, above all, to recruit talent. A 60-minute video builds enough trust for someone to leave a job, which short-form never does ([26:33](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=CLJ9AnFuSZk&t=1593s)).
- `[OPERATIONS]` Hire agencies short-term to learn from and set up processes, then bring the work in-house. A sales agency can take you from $100k to $300–500k/month, at which point you can attract strong reps yourself ([28:35](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=CLJ9AnFuSZk&t=1715s)).
- `[OFFER]` Pitch the unique mechanism at your market's level of sophistication. Theirs is simply "social media marketing: we film you, the same way you found me through a video ad". Over-engineered mechanisms confuse buyers. It exists to help the buyer justify the purchase, not to make you sound clever ([30:36](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=CLJ9AnFuSZk&t=1836s)).
- `[GUARANTEE]` Guarantees exist because they make the business more money. Companies that offer them understand their outcome data better than customers do. Build in conditions (pays on time, posts X videos) based on hundreds of client results so failure is nearly impossible ([32:39](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=CLJ9AnFuSZk&t=1959s)).
- `[GUARANTEE]` They tested "10M views guaranteed" and went back to "1M" because 10M sounded like a scam to less sophisticated owners and lowered CTR and trust. Set the guarantee so about 80% of clients never claim, model the refund exposure (cash collected upfront vs payment plans, margins), and collect data before offering it ([34:10](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=CLJ9AnFuSZk&t=2050s)).
- `[GUARANTEE]` Guarantees support higher prices. A guarantee that sounds like a cheap gimmick attracts worse clients. Clients who pay more are usually better ([36:12](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=CLJ9AnFuSZk&t=2172s)).
- `[PRICING]` Contract structure: if average LTV is 5 months, set a 6-month minimum. Make monthly unattractive and offer discounts for 3, 6 and 12 months paid upfront. Example: an exit-planning consultant charging $6k/month whose clients left at month 5. They set a 6-month minimum: $36k monthly or $30k paid in full ([37:13](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=CLJ9AnFuSZk&t=2233s)).
- `[OFFER]` Removing the downsell from 40-minute Zoom sales calls didn't change the number of sales; they just sold everything at the higher price. A second price option makes prospects "go home and think". Upsells and downsells suit checkout pages or long enterprise cycles, not one-call closes ([38:45](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=CLJ9AnFuSZk&t=2325s)).
- `[SALES-HIRING]` Match reps to the buyer. Their young 20-something reps had about 5x lower revenue per day than "James Bond"-style reps (one is literally named Bond) who look and sound like the 30–40-year-old owners they sell to. Image-led purchases are decided on emotion and justified with logic ([41:18](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=CLJ9AnFuSZk&t=2478s)).
- `[SETTING]` The biggest show-up factor is how soon the call is (most people plan no more than 4–5 workdays ahead), not the setter's rapport. Don't text someone whose call is 30 minutes away, or someone who booked at 2am, as it lowers show-up. Use area codes to spot time zones ([44:22](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=CLJ9AnFuSZk&t=2662s)).
- `[SETTING]` One week of testing a heavy pre-call contact system popular on YouTube cost them about $300k. Heavy nurturing suits lower-income B2C buyers; it annoys multi-million-dollar owners ([45:54](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=CLJ9AnFuSZk&t=2754s)).
- `[KPIS]` Close rate depends on price and economics. He auto-rejects rep applicants who lead with a close-rate percentage. Realtors close 1–5% of showings and earn well. A 30–40% close rate means your price is too low ([46:55](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=CLJ9AnFuSZk&t=2815s)).
- `[PRICING]` The ceiling on price increases is rep morale. They raised prices until close rate fell to 6%, which was more profitable than 12%, but reps were getting about one deal a week and lost motivation, so they lowered the price again. Transactional reps need wins weekly or several times a week ([48:26](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=CLJ9AnFuSZk&t=2906s)).
- `[SALES-MGMT]` He doesn't believe in sales managers acting as "nannies" for reps' emotions. Reps earn $20–60k/month and must regulate themselves or be fired. Managers handle admin, process and basic training using data. Hire self-motivated "hunters" ([49:58](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=CLJ9AnFuSZk&t=2998s)).
- `[OFFER]` Don't under-promise and over-deliver. Promise accurately, charge for it and deliver accurately. Being transparent earns the most over time ([52:31](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=CLJ9AnFuSZk&t=3151s)).
- `[ADS]` Referrals are cheap but can't be scaled on demand. Paid ads took them from $1.4M straight to $3M/month (never a $2M month), and next he expects $3M to $5M. Ads cost about 20% of revenue, but you choose how much you'll make next month ([54:01](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=CLJ9AnFuSZk&t=3241s)).
- `[OTHER]` No cold DMs or cold email. Pick acquisition methods that match your natural strengths or a big market opportunity ("the current matters more than the boat"). Cold outreach doesn't use content skills or suit their buyer ([55:02](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=CLJ9AnFuSZk&t=3302s)).
- `[CREATIVE]` "Pollution tax" (credited to Caleb): annoying ads pollute the feed, so Meta charges more to show them. Ads that feel like organic content get cheap delivery, so learn organic first ([58:06](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=CLJ9AnFuSZk&t=3486s)).
- `[ADS]` Since Meta's Andromeda update (live around September), creative matters more than campaign structure, though structure still gives an edge. He encourages people to copy his proven ads; one copier got 300k organic views and a top-performing ad. But what happens after the ad (a 60+ person team) is what makes the money ([59:38](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=CLJ9AnFuSZk&t=3578s)).
- `[ADS]` Testing: don't throw everything into CBO, because Meta will push budget to 1–3 creatives and skip proper testing. Use ABO with a fixed budget per ad, spend a few thousand dollars testing each one, then move proven winners into CBO and let Meta scale ([1:01:08](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=CLJ9AnFuSZk&t=3668s)).
- `[ADS]` Pixel learning budget: for B2B calls costing $500–1,000 each, plan several thousand dollars. A $10 e-commerce product may need only a few hundred ([1:03:10](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=CLJ9AnFuSZk&t=3790s)).
- `[ADS]` Ad fatigue usually comes from over-concentrating budget. One bottom-of-funnel ad has taken about $2M over 18 months but works only below about 15% of total spend: $1.5k of $10k/day, now about $6k of $40k/day. Bottom-of-funnel ads should be about 20% of budget. His biggest full-funnel ad takes about 25% ($8–12k of about $41k/day) ([1:03:40](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=CLJ9AnFuSZk&t=3820s)).
- `[KPIS]` Judge ads by funnel stage. Top-of-funnel ads are judged on CTR (stop the scroll), mid-funnel ads build trust with deeper content, and bottom-of-funnel ads are direct calls to action judged on cost per call and ROAS. Keep some lower-ROAS ads running because ROAS isn't their job ([1:05:42](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=CLJ9AnFuSZk&t=3942s)).
- `[EMAIL]` Email brings under 5% of revenue despite about 50k active readers (roughly $150k in a $3M month). Emails are repurposed top social posts (e.g. a 100k-view short). For most small businesses an extra 5% isn't worth heavy investment in sequences or retargeting ([1:07:14](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=CLJ9AnFuSZk&t=4034s)).
- `[FUNNEL]` Current routing: founder-face ads go to the VSL, employee or creator ads go straight to the application form. He suspects turning off the VSL would lower overall ROAS because of delayed conversions, which could be tested with geographic holdouts ([1:08:16](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=CLJ9AnFuSZk&t=4096s)).
- `[SCALING-STAGES]` He started with a VSL funnel because a marketer he paid $20k told him to. He reached $1.4M/month with one funnel, one product, one price, one delivery method and one customer type. Adding a no-VSL funnel took them to $3M the next month ([1:09:47](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=CLJ9AnFuSZk&t=4187s)).
- `[HIRING]` Match the person to the role: data-driven roles (media buyer) need hard skills, team leadership needs charisma. Never hire someone dishonest or someone who learns badly. Ignore looks and style; a wider candidate pool finds smarter people ([1:10:48](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=CLJ9AnFuSZk&t=4248s)).
- `[HIRING]` Hire from both startups (fighters who work 60–80-hour weeks) and corporates (contracts, HR, negotiation, spotting flight risks) ([1:13:23](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=CLJ9AnFuSZk&t=4403s)).
- `[SALES-MGMT]` Radical transparency: every rep sees everyone's leads, calls, closes, revenue per lead and about 20 other stats. The bottom performers are let go monthly, and reps can see that coming weeks ahead. "My job is not to manage people but to create conditions where they succeed or leave" ([1:14:24](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=CLJ9AnFuSZk&t=4464s)).
- `[SALES-HIRING]` Sales hiring with almost no interview: watch a 5-minute Loom (30 seconds is often enough), give the rep $20k of leads as a test, and judge on KPIs. Roles that are hard to measure, like CTO, took nearly a year to fill ([1:15:25](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=CLJ9AnFuSZk&t=4525s)).
- `[LEADERSHIP]` Look for ways to take the blame yourself, because what you own you can fix. Culture shouldn't depend on daily CEO meetings. He doesn't know some employees by sight, and transparent systems run the culture ([1:16:26](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=CLJ9AnFuSZk&t=4586s)).
- `[TEAM]` Almost everyone on his team earns six figures, a model of small teams of smart people. The McDonald's model of many low-paid staff and great systems also works; pick your battle ([1:17:27](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=CLJ9AnFuSZk&t=4647s)).
**Short quotes:**
- "If I have a 30% or 40% close rate, this rather indicates that the price is too low" ([47:56](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=CLJ9AnFuSZk&t=2876s))
- "My job as employer—not to manage people, and to create conditions for employees could either to succeed, or leave." ([1:14:24](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=CLJ9AnFuSZk&t=4464s))
**G&L angle:**
- Exit-planning consultant example: G&L's seller engagement should have a minimum term set just above the average client lifetime (e.g. a 6–12 month exit-readiness programme). Monthly costs more than paid-in-full, and the success fee comes on top.
- Offer one price on the sales call with no downsell. Add a premium anchor (e.g. done-for-you acquisition search plus closing, priced 5–10x) where 1% uptake still pays.
- Guarantee: a conditional guarantee built on G&L's own data (e.g. "X vetted, financeable deals presented within 90 days if you complete the steps, or we keep working free"). Keep it believable for the audience, avoid scam-sounding numbers, and target about 80% of clients never claiming.
- Hire closers who resemble the buyer: experienced, credible advisors for 55+ owners, not 22-year-olds. Test candidates with a Loom plus a set budget of live leads, and show the whole team every rep's stats.
- On Meta, test new creatives in ABO with a fixed per-ad budget, then promote winners to CBO. Keep any single bottom-of-funnel ad under about 15% of spend. Don't over-invest in email (expect about 5% of revenue).

## How to make ads so good, clients throw money at you — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=htaJ8CkoWSk
**Gist:** After about $1M a month in ad tests, Daniel names five creative formats to run together: short looping text-on-screen videos, podcast clips, AI-varied image ads, short direct-offer videos and long "cold open case study" videos. He frames Meta as charging a "pollution tax" for ads that feel like ads, and stresses format diversity and covering top, middle and bottom of funnel with specialised creatives.
**Nuggets:**
- `[CREATIVE]` Format 1 — short looping iPhone video with carefully worded text on screen (neither a talking head nor a static image): their #3 ad, ~$250K spend in 60 days, made in under 5 minutes. Look for formats competitors aren't using in your niche. ([0:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=htaJ8CkoWSk&t=0s))
- `[ADS]` Mental model: Meta treats advertisers like polluting factories — every ad degrades the organic feed, so you pay based on how often and how badly you "pollute." Scummy, cringey ads cost more; organic-feeling ads cost less. Take what works in organic and run it as paid before competitors do. ([1:03](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=htaJ8CkoWSk&t=63s))
- `[HIRING]` YouTube content doubles as recruiting: "demonstrate excellence" to attract applicants (links to apply). ([2:04](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=htaJ8CkoWSk&t=124s))
- `[CREATIVE]` Format 2 — podcast-style clips: viewers who watch podcast clips organically also watch them as ads, so they pay less "pollution tax." Their newest podcast ad ran at nearly half the cost of the next-best ad in its first week. Vuori ran a fake-podcast ad about comfortable pants for a year. ([2:37](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=htaJ8CkoWSk&t=157s))
- `[CREATIVE]` Steal formats from unrelated industries (an e-com supplement company's image style adapted to their B2B offer). ([4:10](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=htaJ8CkoWSk&t=250s))
- `[TOOLS]` Format 3 — image ads made in seconds with Meta Ads Manager's built-in AI image generation (he says it uses the same tech as Midjourney): reuse proven copy, generate variations, discard the weird ones. E-com brands run 5,000+ image creatives per month by spinning ~10 variants off each proven creative. ([4:40](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=htaJ8CkoWSk&t=280s))
- `[ADS]` Image ads can beat video in a niche where everyone runs video, because they're less competitive. Meta feeds each user the formats they consume (carousel readers get more carousels), so always run a mix — images, carousels, short-form, long-form — to reach more of the audience more cheaply. ([6:13](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=htaJ8CkoWSk&t=373s))
- `[CREATIVE]` Format 4 — short bottom-of-funnel offer video: bold offer → how it works → click for more info. Their best ad last month, ~$250K spend in two months; aimed at people already looking for the solution. ([7:14](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=htaJ8CkoWSk&t=434s))
- `[CREATIVE]` Format 5 — "cold open case study": a broadly appealing result as the hook, then a long video explaining how you deliver it. Nearly $1M spend over ~2 years and still not fatigued. ([7:46](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=htaJ8CkoWSk&t=466s))
- `[ADS]` Long videos raise average view duration (AVD) — e.g. 30–60 seconds on a 10-minute video vs 5 seconds on a loop at the same watch percentage. High AVD tells Meta viewers want more, which makes bottom-of-funnel retargeting cheaper. ([8:16](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=htaJ8CkoWSk&t=496s))
- `[FUNNEL]` Make dedicated ads for top (awareness), middle (trust) and bottom (conversion) of funnel. Big personal brands often wrongly make more top-of-funnel comedy when they need conversion ads; brands without an audience only pitch directly and get cold calls/low volume because they lack middle and top. Specialised pieces beat trying to make every video full-funnel. ([8:46](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=htaJ8CkoWSk&t=526s))
- `[FUNNEL]` Most prospects forget your ad 10 seconds after becoming a lead — the funnel after the click must carry the conversion. ([9:47](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=htaJ8CkoWSk&t=587s))
**Short quotes:**
- "Facebook sees you like a polluting factory and they charge you a fee depending on how much you pollute." ([1:03](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=htaJ8CkoWSk&t=63s))
- "You should always be looking for a format of creative that is underutilized by other advertisers." ([0:32](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=htaJ8CkoWSk&t=32s))
**G&L angle:**
- Run all five formats for G&L: text-on-screen loops ("Bought a $2M HVAC company with 10% down — here's the structure"), podcast-style clips with an advisor and a buyer or seller, AI-varied image ads from proven copy, a short bottom-of-funnel offer ad, and a long cold-open deal case study.
- The long cold-open case study fits M&A well: lead with a broad result (e.g. "retired at 62 with a seven-figure exit plus 5 years of seller-note income"), then walk through how the deal was found, valued, financed and closed — then retarget high-AVD viewers.
- Check the Ads Library for which formats M&A/acquisition competitors *aren't* using (likely images, carousels, podcast clips) and lean into those.
- Plan creatives by funnel stage: top (succession crisis/boomer-exit stats), middle (education on seller finance), bottom (book a valuation/acquisition strategy call).

## We Paid Iman Gadzhi $18k Each. Here's What We Learned. (call w/ Ryan Clogg) — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ocPw7KvY710
**Gist:** Highlights of a conversation between Daniel and Ryan Clogg ($5.4M/month fitness-coaching marketer) about the advice each got from paid consulting with Iman Gadzhi. The lessons: start simple, low-production YouTube to attract high-level contacts and talent; collect as much cash upfront as possible instead of month-to-month; and kill lower-ticket offers that confuse closers and cannibalise the core offer.
**Nuggets:**
- `[ORGANIC]` Iman's advice to Ryan: "just talk about what you do". Plain Loom-style breakdowns, aim for 3–5K followers on X and YouTube, then monetise through private consulting for an extra $1–3M a year. Ryan executed head-down for 4 months and reached 11K subscribers. ([0:30](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ocPw7KvY710&t=30s), [1:01](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ocPw7KvY710&t=61s))
- `[ORGANIC]` In-depth, unpolished analysis attracts the right audience (higher-level business owners and A-level hires) because those people skip over-produced content. Daniel reached 6K subscribers the same way. ([1:32](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ocPw7KvY710&t=92s))
- `[ORGANIC]` Get real-world experience and proof first. High production value doesn't fix content that says nothing useful. ([2:35](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ocPw7KvY710&t=155s))
- `[OFFER]` Ryan prefers performance or percentage-based consulting (upside only if the client wins) over a monthly "inner circle", because he dislikes being paid $50K by someone who didn't make $50K back. He now consults through a payments-platform partner that earns on clients' processing volume. Brand name garbled in captions. ([3:37](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ocPw7KvY710&t=217s))
- `[PRICING]` Iman's advice that doubled Daniel's business the following month: month-to-month suits software like Disney+, but in marketing and online services you should "charge as much upfront as possible and expect to only get paid that ever." Viral Coach moved to 6-month terms with discounts for paying more upfront, since they roughly know what's collectable on the back end. ([4:41](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ocPw7KvY710&t=281s), [5:12](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ocPw7KvY710&t=312s))
- `[OFFER]` Kill the low-ticket downsell. A client stuck at a couple hundred thousand a month dropped a $3K offer next to high-ticket ones; ROAS jumped about 50% and the business doubled within weeks. ([5:42](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ocPw7KvY710&t=342s))
- `[OFFER]` Daniel's version: cutting a $6K offer for less-qualified leads took revenue from $1.4M to $3M the next month, skipping $2M entirely. That offer was about 80% of customers but only 20% of revenue (inverse Pareto). Purchase counts barely changed after the cut. ([6:13](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ocPw7KvY710&t=373s))
- `[SALES-CALL]` Why the downsell hurt: closers fumbled by "saving the sale", assuming a prospect wasn't qualified for the main offer and pitching the cheap one. One offer removes the rep's and the buyer's confusion, so they just buy. ([6:44](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ocPw7KvY710&t=404s))
- `[FINANCE]` With fixed-price MRR, LTV is purely a function of time (how long you keep them and stay relevant). Low-ticket MRR as a core model almost needs VC money because payback is too slow. Higher-priced B2B MRR with setup fees can be bootstrapped. ([7:14](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ocPw7KvY710&t=434s))
**Short quotes:**
- "You need to charge as much upfront as possible and expect to only get paid that ever." ([4:41](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ocPw7KvY710&t=281s))
- "Once we removed all that confusion and the customer wasn't confused about what they were buying, they just bought." ([6:44](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ocPw7KvY710&t=404s))
**G&L angle:**
- Collect the bulk of G&L's advisory fee upfront (paid-in-full discount vs instalments) rather than relying on monthly retainers or back-end success fees that may never arrive.
- Resist a cheap course or "lite" tier sold to unqualified callers. It can tempt closers to downsell, confuse buyers, and cap revenue. Keep one core offer per audience (buyer programme, seller exit programme).
- Consider a success-fee or percentage component (e.g. on deal close or on exit-value uplift) as an alignment kicker on top of upfront fees, not instead of them.
- Founders can grow organic reach with plain, expert deal breakdowns (Loom/YouTube) to attract sophisticated buyers, sellers and talent.

## Here's How We're Scaling to $10M/Month (Just Copy Us) — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=r6KhTStpbZc
**Gist:** Daniel talks shop with the operator of a fitness info-coaching business for women aged 40–60 (record $5.4M in July, then a drop to about $2M). Topics: why revenue swung (80% reliance on Meta and VSL funnels, a botched sales-comp change), comp design, ROAS thresholds against a $6,200 cash AOV, a low-ticket "direct-to-cart" front end to stabilise CAC, brand-face tests, hiring "setters" as professional client-support staff, W2 vs 1099 reps, and payment and financing structures (deferred paid-in-full, Splitit, matching financing term to programme term, removing cheap downsells).
**Nuggets:**
- `[SCALING-STAGES]` The guest's volatility came from about 80% reliance on Meta plus VSL call funnels, which they feel start to struggle around $3M/month. A month of pushing to win a public $5M/month bet (overspending, team incentives) was followed by a collapse in lead flow ([0:30](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=r6KhTStpbZc&t=30s)).
- `[ADS]` The goal is a stable "evergreen machine" booking exactly 150 calls in every 48-hour window, 24/7/365, so the sales team's calendar is 90% full. Paid ads are the controllable lever; organic mostly works mid and bottom of funnel ([3:02](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=r6KhTStpbZc&t=182s)).
- `[SALES-MGMT]` Changing sales comp after a sprint (lower percentages for middle performers) cost them their best closers and caused confusion. Unfilled calendars or calls at the wrong price stop revenue: when one lever fails you earn nothing, when both fail it's a disaster ([4:34](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=r6KhTStpbZc&t=274s)).
- `[SALES-MGMT]` Old comp: 8–10% of net new cash plus a points bonus pool (weekday dollars = 1 point, weekend dollars = 1.5 points; tiers 60k points = $3k, 120k = $6k, up to $12k). The nonlinear tiers got expensive in big weeks, and the total sales department (reps, managers, ops) cost 20–25% of net cash, plus about 20% employment overhead ([7:08](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=r6KhTStpbZc&t=428s)).
- `[KPIS]` Sales-per-booking (closes divided by all bookings, e.g. 50% show × 20% close = 10%) suits marketers but not reps. Marketing managers are paid salary plus tiered bonuses for lowering CPA on high-ticket sales, which aligns them with sales ([6:06](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=r6KhTStpbZc&t=366s)).
- `[KPIS]` Reps are paid on net new cash collected in the first 30 days, and they collect 85% of it within 30 days. "New-cash AOV" is about $6,200 per close. ROAS thresholds: 3x is the goal, 4x "printing money", 2.5x OK, 2x means relying on back-end money, below 2x "stealing from the back-end". With $6k AOV, CPA under $3k gives 2x and under $2k gives 3x ([9:41](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=r6KhTStpbZc&t=581s)).
- `[FUNNEL]` They ran $70–80M through a single funnel. Cutting CPA by about 60% through account restructuring made them think they needed more funnels, so they tested five (3–4 VSL opt-in variants, a direct application, a low-ticket funnel). The "everything feeds into one vortex" theory proved false: results were mixed and similar ([11:12](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=r6KhTStpbZc&t=672s)).
- `[FUNNEL]` Key-person risk was smaller than feared. New brand faces with their own landing pages and VSLs produced CPA similar to, and sometimes better than, the founder's ([12:13](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=r6KhTStpbZc&t=733s)).
- `[OFFER]` The shift is to a direct-to-cart front end ($50–$2,000) that liquidates ad spend, then upsell buyers to coaching. Buyers then talk to a qualifier and a closer (a two-call close) with 80–90% show rates ([13:15](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=r6KhTStpbZc&t=795s)).
- `[OFFER]` The old low-ticket product capped out at 30–50 sales a day; even pages that tested 15x better couldn't beat the ugly original, so it became a retargeting tool. They watch "net CPA" (e.g. $150 Meta CPA minus $50 AOV = $100). A reworked cold-audience version did 200+ sales on day one. Expect CPA to roughly double after a few weeks of scaling ([14:47](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=r6KhTStpbZc&t=887s)).
- `[PROFIT]` P&L is modular: fulfilment is roughly fixed, sales is a roughly fixed percentage, and marketing swings hugely. In July and August net profit was 45% of cash (minus 15–20% reserved for future coaching delivery). At $3–5M/month profit is huge, at $2M it's nerve-wracking, and below $2M is the danger zone ([18:21](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=r6KhTStpbZc&t=1101s)).
- `[SCALING-STAGES]` Reaching $10M/month comes down to CAC and LTV. Lower CAC by liquidating ad spend with direct-to-cart sales (e.g. $200k/day spend recovering 50%+). Raise LTV with retention: renewals, ascension and comeback campaigns, treating coaching as a lifestyle rather than a one-year contract ([19:53](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=r6KhTStpbZc&t=1193s)).
- `[CREATIVE]` Lead-gen and affiliate buyers spending hundreds of thousands a day test about 1,000 ads per vertical each week; the guest tested 30–50 and decided to double down on creative. They also ran a company-wide creative competition (20+ staff filmed ads, with prizes for the top three) ([22:26](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=r6KhTStpbZc&t=1346s)).
- `[PARTNERSHIP]` Untapped channels: TikTok, AppLovin and CPA/affiliate partnerships, e.g. pay a supplement or e-commerce brand a fixed fee per customer for a coaching back-end. Their attempt failed because $50 product buyers sent to a $7k VSL application had no warm-up, so you need a low-ticket bridge in between ([23:26](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=r6KhTStpbZc&t=1406s)).
- `[UGC]` Coach-made and client-testimonial UGC programmes failed through poor management (no feedback loop, no follow-through on collecting material), not because of the creators. They admit to starting many ideas and finishing few ([29:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=r6KhTStpbZc&t=1740s)).
- `[FUNNEL]` Rollout path for new brand faces: test their ads to the main founder funnel (CPC, cost per call, sales), then give them their own landing page with their photo, then their own VSL. All funnels merge at the application ([31:02](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=r6KhTStpbZc&t=1862s)).
- `[FUNNEL]` Daniel's data: about 80% of his traffic now goes from a creator's ad straight to a headline plus application with no VSL. Margo's ad leading into Daniel's VSL lost viewers ("who's this guy?"), and her own VSL failed because she doesn't know sales or the product as well. The no-VSL application converted best ([33:04](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=r6KhTStpbZc&t=1984s)).
- `[OFFER]` Daniel's plan to go from $5M to $10M: don't just improve ads; create a separate offer for a different market. Move from online businesses to the real-economy sector (local service businesses), where video beats competitors' static Google ads. He notes a peer launched a consulting unit at $5k/$40k/$150k and grew by targeting the broad market rather than coaches ([36:39](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=r6KhTStpbZc&t=2199s)).
- `[OPERATIONS]` Operational load: the guest needs about 600 high-ticket closes a month (each needing onboarding and coaching), where competitors at higher prices need about 200. They once ran about 300 calls a day in 30-minute slots for $1,500 info products ([38:41](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=r6KhTStpbZc&t=2321s)).
- `[ADS]` Platform expansion: Daniel's YouTube Shorts added 50M new viewers on top of 50M a month. Every platform has a ceiling for a narrow demographic. Next channels: YouTube/GDN, CTV/streaming, AppLovin. "Touch a prospect in four channels and they think you're the biggest company in the world" ([40:46](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=r6KhTStpbZc&t=2446s)).
- `[SALES-HIRING]` Weekend bonuses existed because 1099 contractors can't be told when to work. They moved reps to W2 at about $3–3.5M/month (heard it was needed for legal reasons and for a sale of the business), which cut take-home 30–40% and changed the culture. Daniel's view: you can still sell a business that uses contractors ([44:53](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=r6KhTStpbZc&t=2693s)).
- `[PRICING]` Discounts on the core offer only on Black Friday/Cyber Monday. Otherwise discounts only on re-sales and renewals. They tested a 90-day option with mandatory upsell calls ([45:56](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=r6KhTStpbZc&t=2756s)).
- `[LEADERSHIP]` A culture "stuck in the middle", neither mission-driven nor standards-enforced, creates mediocrity. Gaps such as weekend roles nobody covered, ignored task blocks and Slack channels need clear standards and accountability ([47:28](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=r6KhTStpbZc&t=2848s)).
- `[SALES-HIRING]` Early reps came from their own customer base: believers who'd bought the $1k info product, about 400 rep IDs over time. Their best reps are fitness-focused, driven women who share the mission. Moving from $1k info products to annual coaching required real closers. Promoting one rep to sales manager lost half the team overnight ([52:02](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=r6KhTStpbZc&t=3122s)).
- `[SETTING]` Both renamed "setter" (seen as the industry's bottom rung) to a client-support or admin-coordinator role. They hire law students and people from professional-services admin as W2 staff with KPIs who confirm calls and run 30-minute Zoom calls. They "tear it up" because they're more professional than teenage setters selling to millionaires. The guest hired about 20–30 this way ([55:07](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=r6KhTStpbZc&t=3307s)).
- `[SALES-CALL]` ROI justification is the key to high-ticket B2B: if assets and content make the buyer believe "it's $10–50k but I'll make $200–900k", the deal is done ([58:44](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=r6KhTStpbZc&t=3524s)).
- `[HIRING]` Fast scaling hire process: group interviews (inbound and outbound pipeline), then shortlist, then 1:1 with the manager, then start date and cohort onboarding owned by one dedicated person. Screen for culture basics (how they show up on camera, speed, attention to detail). Teach skills, give people a chance, and judge after two weeks or a set number of calls. Best practice is a career ladder (e.g. note-taker to tier-1 closer to manager or fulfilment) ([59:14](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=r6KhTStpbZc&t=3554s)).
- `[ORGANIC]` After paying about $18–25k for coaching, Daniel was told to "just talk about what you do on Loom/YouTube". Unpolished expert content works when you know the topic. "Before trying to do something on social media, do something in real life" ([1:03:19](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=r6KhTStpbZc&t=3799s)).
- `[FINANCE]` Financing-term trap: 24-month loans on 12-month programmes meant about 50% of monthly cash came from loans clients kept paying after coaching ended, which killed renewals and likely got them dropped by Affirm. Fix: financing term equals programme term. That cut financing to 20–30% and then 10% of cash ([1:09:55](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=r6KhTStpbZc&t=4195s)).
- `[PRICING]` Price financing so net cash is equal after fees: $10k by card at 3% nets $9,700, financing at 15% nets $8,500, so set a higher financed price. Internal plans cost a few thousand more in total (e.g. 2 × $4,100 = $8,200 vs $7,000 paid in full). The minimum internal plan is quarterly to cover the high onboarding cost ([1:12:28](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=r6KhTStpbZc&t=4348s)).
- `[PRICING]` Daniel's monthly cancel-anytime billing (copied from SaaS/Skool trends) was a mistake for a marketing service. His advisor said "Disney+ can do monthly; you need to collect as much upfront as possible and assume that's all you'll get". Switching to 6-month terms with paid-upfront discounts instantly doubled results ([1:13:30](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=r6KhTStpbZc&t=4410s)).
- `[OFFER]` Remove cheap downsells. A Nick Fisher (SubTo) group member stuck at a couple of hundred thousand a month removed a $3k offer and grew about 50% in weeks. Daniel removed his $6k downsell (about 20% of revenue but about 80% of customer count) and went from $1.4M to $3M the next month; total purchases stayed about the same but at the higher price, because reps stopped hedging ([1:15:01](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=r6KhTStpbZc&t=4501s)).
- `[OFFER]` Low-price front-ends work only with a clear built-in ascension (e.g. publishing.com's $2k auto-webinar, then an upsell on the onboarding call: "this is $30k, pay $24k now"), not as a menu of options on one call ([1:18:04](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=r6KhTStpbZc&t=4684s)).
- `[FINANCE]` Benchmark: about 30% of total contract value is collected in the first 30 days. The guest sits at 28–31%; Daniel collects about 85%. Rule: if you collect too much upfront, raise the price. The guest's close rate is about 13% of shows ([1:24:40](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=r6KhTStpbZc&t=5080s)).
- `[TOOLS]` Splitit splits the payment against the buyer's existing card limit with no interest for 12 months, so the buyer pays the same price. Klarna and similar can carry 20–30% fees. Present financing as an optional convenience service with its own fee ([1:19:35](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=r6KhTStpbZc&t=4775s)).
- `[SALES-MGMT]` "Deferred PIF": let reps convert a payment-plan buyer to paid-in-full within 30 days (e.g. $2,300 down on a quarterly plan, then "pay $4,900 now to lock in the discount") to speed up cash. Daniel stopped paying reps extra commission for PIF upgrades and saw no drop, because tiered pricing lets reps justify paying in full on its own merits ([1:26:12](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=r6KhTStpbZc&t=5172s)).
**Short quotes:**
- "Monthly payment is cool for software if you're Disney Plus... you need to collect as much as possible upfront" ([1:14:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=r6KhTStpbZc&t=4440s))
- "High ticket is the only place where a company with million-dollar monthly turnover has a teenager as the first contact person." ([57:42](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=r6KhTStpbZc&t=3462s))
**G&L angle:**
- Collect as much as possible upfront. Avoid monthly cancel-anytime billing; offer 6–12 month programmes with paid-upfront discounts, financing whose term matches the programme, and a deferred paid-in-full upgrade within 30 days.
- Remove any cheap downsell from the strategy call. One G&L offer at one price, with low-ticket products (deal-analysis templates, a buyer bootcamp) sold only through a separate front end that ascends into the call.
- Hire "client success coordinators" (law or finance graduates, ex-paralegals, banking admin) instead of teenage setters to confirm calls with 55+ business owners. Professionalism matters to that audience.
- Set ROAS and CPA thresholds against G&L's 30-day cash AOV, e.g. if cash AOV is $8k, a CPA under $2.7k gives 3x. Pay marketing on CPA and sales on 30-day new cash.
- A direct-to-cart low-ticket front end (e.g. a $97–$497 "Buy Your First Business" course or a valuation report for sellers) can liquidate ad spend and feed warmer buyers into a two-call close with a higher show rate.

## I Roasted a 6-Figure Business Owner to Get Him to 7-Figures — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=cZQ4hJNpW7o
**Gist:** Daniel and his team roast Colin's business: a ~$700k/year video production company with a $30k offer, alongside an unrelated LinkedIn-storytelling community. They tell him to stack paid on top of the platform he already wins on, focus on one congruent offer, log BANT loss reasons on discovery calls, hire a setter, and replace the sales-page-in-a-Typeform with a VSL, a short form, and sales calls.
**Nuggets:**
- `[ADS]` Organic plus paid together is "2+2=10". If organic works on a platform (e.g. LinkedIn), add paid on that same platform rather than switching to Google or Meta, which have different best practices ([0:31](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=cZQ4hJNpW7o&t=31s), [1:02](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=cZQ4hJNpW7o&t=62s))
- `[SALES-CALL]` Write down why every lost discovery call was lost, and don't hang up until you have a reason. Use BANT: Budget, Authority (CFO, spouse, CMO), Need (mismatch or unqualified), Timing ([1:33](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=cZQ4hJNpW7o&t=93s), [2:03](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=cZQ4hJNpW7o&t=123s))
- `[OBJECTIONS]` Fix recurring loss reasons structurally. For frequent timing objections, either target people closer to a decision or offer something like a free month for signing now to close the gap ([2:34](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=cZQ4hJNpW7o&t=154s))
- `[OFFER]` Don't run incongruent offers side by side (a $30k production service plus a $50/month LinkedIn school). Focus on the main revenue driver, or sell info about the business you already run ([3:06](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=cZQ4hJNpW7o&t=186s), [5:13](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=cZQ4hJNpW7o&t=313s))
- `[SETTING]` At $700k/year, a founder taking discovery calls is a low-skill use of time. Hire a setter (about $60–80k/year) to pre-handle BANT, including asking "who else needs to be on this call?", before the founder ("big boss") sells ([3:06](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=cZQ4hJNpW7o&t=186s), [3:37](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=cZQ4hJNpW7o&t=217s))
- `[APPLICATION]` Don't disqualify in copy ("this is for you if you spend $100k/month"). Ask the question, e.g. ad spend under or over $100k, and silently route non-fits to free content or a low-ticket community downsell. That lets you monetise the rest of the audience ([5:44](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=cZQ4hJNpW7o&t=344s), [6:14](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=cZQ4hJNpW7o&t=374s), [6:45](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=cZQ4hJNpW7o&t=405s))
- `[APPLICATION]` Halving the application's discovery questions gave about a 30% lift in total applicants. Keep questions quick and easy, not a 20-minute process ([7:47](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=cZQ4hJNpW7o&t=467s))
- `[VSL]` Deliver the pitch and information in a VSL with the form beneath it, not inside a Typeform that functions as a sales page ([7:47](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=cZQ4hJNpW7o&t=467s))
- `[SALES-CALL]` Don't try to sell anything over about $2k from a web page alone. Sales calls exist to customise the marketing, and the more customised the offer (or the more it seems so), the more you can charge, as in enterprise SaaS's long multi-stakeholder discovery ([8:18](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=cZQ4hJNpW7o&t=498s), [8:48](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=cZQ4hJNpW7o&t=528s))
- `[FUNNEL]` When a prospect says "I want to buy", give them a way to pay immediately, not another case study ([9:21](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=cZQ4hJNpW7o&t=561s), [9:51](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=cZQ4hJNpW7o&t=591s))
- `[APPLICATION]` Collect contact details (email, phone) at the start of the form so drop-offs can still be called, emailed and retargeted. Typeform's partial-response capture needs a paid plan, roughly $100/month ([10:22](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=cZQ4hJNpW7o&t=622s), [10:53](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=cZQ4hJNpW7o&t=653s))
- `[HIRING]` Daniel uses roast videos partly as recruiting: great operators who show up in content get job offers ([4:42](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=cZQ4hJNpW7o&t=282s))
**Short quotes:**
- "Whenever I say I want to buy, give me a way to pay." ([9:21](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=cZQ4hJNpW7o&t=561s))
- "The more you can customize the product or at least make it seem customized, the more money you can charge." ([9:21](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=cZQ4hJNpW7o&t=561s))
**G&L angle:**
- Have closers log a BANT loss reason on every G&L call. Authority is likely common (spouse, business partner, accountant), so setters should ask "who else is involved in this decision?" and get them on the call.
- Capture name, email and phone on the first step of the application, then ask qualifying questions. Route non-qualified buyers silently to a lower-ticket course or community (e.g. Owners Academy) instead of rejecting them in copy.
- Keep the G&L application short. Test cutting questions, since one cut produced a 30% lift in applications.
- At the moment of yes, have a payment link and one-page agreement ready on the call.
- Keep G&L's offers congruent. The buy-side and sell-side offers should share one brand story (the M&A advisor) rather than look like unrelated businesses.

## How to create a $10,000,000 VSL — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=_3-WQstsN5I
**Gist:** A line-by-line teardown of the Viral Coach VSL ($10M in 90 days). Every line serves Promise, Proof or Plan. Get to the "recipe" quickly, disqualify bad fits explicitly, pile on fast case studies, pre-handle objections taken from lost sales calls, push away then pull in, and end with a direct CTA to a short application. He adds that a plain-text landing page with no VSL now gets 80% of their traffic.
**Nuggets:**
- `[VSL]` The three Ps: every part of the VSL delivers Promise, Proof or Plan ("we can do this specifically for you, here's how") at different levels of awareness. ([0:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=_3-WQstsN5I&t=0s))
- `[VSL]` Hook = promise plus proof in one line, through the guarantee: "We'll get you a million views or you don't pay." Add a visual open loop ("1 million views = how much revenue?") so viewers want an answer they don't have yet. ([0:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=_3-WQstsN5I&t=0s), [0:30](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=_3-WQstsN5I&t=30s))
- `[GUARANTEE]` A guarantee on an intermediate metric (views) invites the "views don't pay bills" objection, so the VSL immediately ties views to revenue. ([1:01](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=_3-WQstsN5I&t=61s))
- `[VSL]` Don't open at the top of the funnel and reach the details after 20 minutes (the "recipe blog" problem). Give the recipe early to earn trust. ([1:32](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=_3-WQstsN5I&t=92s))
- `[VSL]` Always include who you can't help ("if you're not an expert or don't have a business to convert views, this isn't for you"), as specifically as possible and more than once. It makes ideal prospects feel the offer was built for them. ([2:04](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=_3-WQstsN5I&t=124s), [2:36](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=_3-WQstsN5I&t=156s))
- `[VSL]` Plan section: lead with the single strongest reason your mechanism beats what they've tried before (for them: organic social has near-zero delivery cost plus compounding audience growth). ([3:07](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=_3-WQstsN5I&t=187s))
- `[VSL]` Source every line from what made money on sales calls. "Every line in here was once said on a sales call that made us money." Nothing is speculative. ([3:38](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=_3-WQstsN5I&t=218s))
- `[VSL]` Call out the specific audience in detail and level with them about problems only they've had (spending heavily on traditional marketing for a few hundred site visits), then restate why you're different. ([4:40](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=_3-WQstsN5I&t=280s), [5:10](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=_3-WQstsN5I&t=310s))
- `[CASE-STUDIES]` "Pollute" the audience's perception with testimonials, but in the VSL run through them quickly: one-line results such as "turned off $60K/month in ads", "2K→200K average views in 90 days", "9,000 leads first week". Then give the average result (15.5M views after 3 months, the average, not the best). ([5:40](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=_3-WQstsN5I&t=340s), [6:11](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=_3-WQstsN5I&t=371s))
- `[OBJECTIONS]` Make the "how it works" section a sequence of objection answers. Custom strategy for your niche (answers "is it cookie-cutter?"). Attention → nurture → convert (what am I paying for?). A trained editor staffed for you, so you film once or twice a month (answers "I don't have time"). Everything else gets easier (answers "is it worth it?"). ([7:13](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=_3-WQstsN5I&t=433s), [7:44](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=_3-WQstsN5I&t=464s))
- `[VSL]` Log why you lose every sales call and rebuild the VSL to answer the most common reasons. ([8:15](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=_3-WQstsN5I&t=495s))
- `[VSL]` Persuasion devices he uses deliberately: appeal to authority (2.5M followers; ads for Amazon, PayPal, Robinhood, Dr Pepper), a dig at agencies that never got results themselves, circular proof ("this works because it got you here"), pre-empting budget objections ("not the cheapest, but best ROI"), and push-away/pull-in ("never expect results from someone who hasn't gotten them"). ([8:45](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=_3-WQstsN5I&t=525s), [9:48](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=_3-WQstsN5I&t=588s))
- `[APPLICATION]` CTA: the most direct pitch, then "a short application below to see if you qualify, we'll map out the process on a call". Goodwill fallback: non-fits are pointed to free training. Make it nothing-to-lose. ([10:18](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=_3-WQstsN5I&t=618s), [10:49](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=_3-WQstsN5I&t=649s))
- `[TOOLS]` Viral Coach offers a free custom GPT that drafts a VSL from these best practices. ([4:08](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=_3-WQstsN5I&t=248s))
- `[LANDING-PAGE]` The most profitable "VSL" is no VSL: a simple text landing page now takes 80% of monthly traffic, with diverse ads doing most of the selling. ([11:51](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=_3-WQstsN5I&t=711s))
**Short quotes:**
- "Every line in here was once said on a sales call that made us money. None of this is speculative." ([4:08](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=_3-WQstsN5I&t=248s))
- "Never expect results from someone that has never gotten those results themselves." ([9:48](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=_3-WQstsN5I&t=588s))
**G&L angle:**
- Structure the G&L VSL as Promise/Proof/Plan. Hook with a promise-plus-proof line (for example, a guarantee-style statement on deal flow or a valuation outcome) and an open loop like "what's a $1M-EBITDA business worth with seller finance?"
- Spell out who G&L is not for (no operating experience and no willingness to work; sellers under X revenue; people wanting passive income with zero involvement) to make qualified buyers and sellers feel targeted.
- Mine lost-call notes and turn the top objections ("I don't have the capital", "sellers won't finance", "my business isn't sellable", "I'm not ready to retire") into the "how it works" section.
- Run a fast case-study reel of closed deals (purchase price, cash down, time to close) plus an honest average outcome.
- Test a plain-text, VSL-less landing page fed by many diverse ads, especially for ads fronted by multiple faces (partners, clients).

## 5 mistakes costing you $30k/mo — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=9o5csXRJXeU
**Gist:** A recorded Q&A from Daniel's one-to-one B2B marketing coaching. Members ask about organic content funnels, stalled top-of-funnel growth and launching a high-ticket done-for-you offer. His core answers: once offer and sales are proven, use paid ads for predictable volume; match marketing to a short sales cycle; organic reach on Meta lowers paid ad costs.
**Nuggets:**
- `[KPIS]` Separate revenue by traffic source (paid vs organic) with first/last-touch attribution tools such as Triple Whale or Hyros, or simple link tracking like Linktree or bit.ly ([1:32](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=9o5csXRJXeU&t=92s), [2:04](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=9o5csXRJXeU&t=124s))
- `[ORGANIC]` Find which part of your funnel is underdeveloped (top, middle or bottom), pick one goal, and make daily progress on only that rather than trying to improve everything at once ([4:09](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=9o5csXRJXeU&t=249s), [5:11](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=9o5csXRJXeU&t=311s))
- `[ORGANIC]` Let the audience pick your content pillars from reach, engagement and especially comment quality. A low-view video that drew multi-paragraph comments is worth more than a shallow viral hit ([6:44](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=9o5csXRJXeU&t=404s), [7:15](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=9o5csXRJXeU&t=435s))
- `[ORGANIC]` Personal stories land best when they're applicable: "here's how I did it so you can too," not "look at me" ([8:17](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=9o5csXRJXeU&t=497s))
- `[ADS]` Paid ads are for people who already have revenue, a consistent sales process, dialled-in pricing and a solid offer. With those in place, paid ads buy predictable lead volume instead of hoping for a viral hit ([11:21](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=9o5csXRJXeU&t=681s), [11:52](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=9o5csXRJXeU&t=712s))
- `[ADS]` Price the cost of inaction. One member's ads produced 5–15 quality applications a day versus about 1 organically. Losing the hacked ad account cost about $30k/month, or $1k/day. Open a new ad account immediately with two-factor authentication rather than waiting on recovery ([12:22](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=9o5csXRJXeU&t=742s), [15:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=9o5csXRJXeU&t=900s))
- `[MINDSET]` Hockey analogy: if you're already a great shooter (content), drill the skill you're weakest at (ads) rather than squeezing more out of your strength ([13:56](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=9o5csXRJXeU&t=836s))
- `[SCALING-STAGES]` If you had to double the business next month, the growth would come from paid ads, not organic alone ([16:01](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=9o5csXRJXeU&t=961s))
- `[FUNNEL]` For high ticket, ads are easier and more predictable than organic, because every ad is a bottom-of-funnel direct pitch ([17:03](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=9o5csXRJXeU&t=1023s), [17:35](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=9o5csXRJXeU&t=1055s))
- `[PRICING]` For a new offer, charge something even at the exploratory stage, start lower and raise the price as demand fills. Free gives no real signal. Daniel had his first client pull out a credit card for a $24k offer, then gave it free so he could learn on him ([20:11](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=9o5csXRJXeU&t=1211s), [20:42](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=9o5csXRJXeU&t=1242s))
- `[PRICING]` Early low-price clients produce case studies and testimonials. Then raise the price, which also adds friction to the funnel ([21:14](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=9o5csXRJXeU&t=1274s))
- `[FUNNEL]` Calls vs webinars comes down to unit economics. One-to-one calls convert better, so you need fewer leads and can pay more per lead and sell a higher ticket. Webinars need more, cheaper leads and support a lower ticket. Pick one and build the whole process around it. At $10–25k he'd use one-to-one calls ([21:44](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=9o5csXRJXeU&t=1304s), [22:15](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=9o5csXRJXeU&t=1335s))
- `[FUNNEL]` Match marketing to the real sales cycle. In his B2B business most leads decide within 72 hours, so weeks of organic hype before a launch cools off. Pack the persuasion into the 48–72 hours after a lead comes in ([22:47](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=9o5csXRJXeU&t=1367s), [23:48](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=9o5csXRJXeU&t=1428s))
- `[ORGANIC]` Sophisticated buyers check your social profile after booking to see if you're legit. Organic content does that credibility work before the call ([24:19](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=9o5csXRJXeU&t=1459s))
- `[ADS]` Organic Meta views make paid ads cheaper. In months when Daniel gets 10M+ organic views, ROAS improves about 5–10%; with fewer views, ads cost more. They spend about $800k/month on Meta. Andromeda favours creators with organic traction, so making relevant top- and middle-of-funnel content is his top tip for lowering ad costs ([26:24](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=9o5csXRJXeU&t=1584s), [26:55](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=9o5csXRJXeU&t=1615s), [28:29](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=9o5csXRJXeU&t=1709s))
- `[ORGANIC]` It's never too early to build brand trust (problem and solution awareness, expert positioning), but with a short sales cycle, don't hype a product until you can take payment ([29:31](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=9o5csXRJXeU&t=1771s))
- `[OFFER]` Before you have a program, turn audience questions into content. Charge for 30- or 60-minute consults by hourly rate via a Calendly or Stripe link, and use those questions to shape the eventual paid program ([31:03](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=9o5csXRJXeU&t=1863s))
- `[ORGANIC]` Build long-form explainers out of recurring community and coaching questions. List the decision criteria so the answer applies broadly ([33:07](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=9o5csXRJXeU&t=1987s))
**Short quotes:**
- "It's costing you about $1,000 a day to not set up a Facebook ad account." ([15:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=9o5csXRJXeU&t=900s))
- "You literally just pay money to get customers like a real business." ([11:52](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=9o5csXRJXeU&t=712s))
**G&L angle:**
- Keep Meta organic going (reels on vendor-finance deal breakdowns and exit stories) even while running paid. It warms the audience and should make the paid VSL ads cheaper. Track monthly organic reach against cost per booked call.
- Measure G&L's real decision window from application to sale. If most deals close within 72 hours, front-load the pre-call emails, SMS and case-study content into that window instead of running long nurture campaigns.
- Set up backup ad accounts and Business Manager admins with 2FA now. Losing the account would cost G&L thousands a day in booked calls.
- At G&L's ticket size, stay with one-to-one sales calls rather than webinars. It justifies a higher cost per lead because each call converts better.
- When testing a new tier (e.g. the 55+ exit-readiness offer), charge something from day one and raise the price as case studies accumulate.

## Got fit - started making $3M per month (the truth) — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=V9cHZkESVPo
**Gist:** A personal video. A year earlier Daniel was 50 lb heavier, $500k in unsecured business debt, with $400 in the bank. He argues peak fitness and peak financial success are competing uses of time, and that getting rich first makes fitness easier. He discloses using GLP-1 and other peptides to lose the weight.
**Nuggets:**
- `[LIFE]` One year earlier: 50 lb heavier, $500k unsecured business debt, $400 in the bank. The business turnaround happened during his unhealthiest period (fast food, drinking, months without training) by conscious choice ([0:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=V9cHZkESVPo&t=0s), [4:10](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=V9cHZkESVPo&t=250s))
- `[MINDSET]` You can't optimise for two peaks if you're serious about either. Every hour in the gym or meal prepping is an hour not working. You can't have two number-one priorities ([3:38](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=V9cHZkESVPo&t=218s), [5:12](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=V9cHZkESVPo&t=312s), [6:46](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=V9cHZkESVPo&t=406s))
- `[MINDSET]` Discipline doesn't automatically transfer between domains. Abs don't mean discipline, and no abs doesn't mean a lack of it ([7:17](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=V9cHZkESVPo&t=437s), [15:10](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=V9cHZkESVPo&t=910s))
- `[LIFE]` Building wealth from zero usually means temporarily sacrificing health. Keep a baseline of ~30 minutes of walking or light exercise rather than 2–3 hours of physique work ([7:48](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=V9cHZkESVPo&t=468s))
- `[LIFE]` Get rich first, then fitness gets easy: a chef, nanny, housekeeper, personal assistant, home gym, premium food. He uses GLP-1s and recovery peptides and is open about it, and says to talk to a doctor ([8:50](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=V9cHZkESVPo&t=530s), [9:21](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=V9cHZkESVPo&t=561s), [10:56](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=V9cHZkESVPo&t=656s))
- `[ORGANIC]` He scrapped his first cut of this video because it was all about him. He re-filmed it around what the viewer can learn from his experience ([0:31](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=V9cHZkESVPo&t=31s))
- `[MINDSET]` Shortcuts exist and the playing field isn't equal. Make a conscious trade-off about what you're giving up instead of defaulting into it ([14:38](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=V9cHZkESVPo&t=878s), [16:41](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=V9cHZkESVPo&t=1001s))
**Short quotes:**
- "You cannot have it all. There is always opportunity cost." ([16:41](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=V9cHZkESVPo&t=1001s))
- "Peak fitness is not a requirement for peak financial success." ([6:16](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=V9cHZkESVPo&t=376s))
**G&L angle:**
