- Founders: pick the single number-one priority for this build phase (e.g. ad-driven booked calls and closed deals) and consciously deprioritise the rest, while keeping a minimum health baseline.
- Personal-story content should be framed around what the viewer can apply, e.g. "how I went from X to buying my first business", not a vanity story.
- Open, honest disclosure (debt, struggles, shortcuts) builds trust. A G&L founder story about past debt or failed deals can be a strong VSL or organic hook.

## A message to my competitors... — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=t30Xo5uFkGk
**Gist:** Competitors copying Daniel's ad scripts, funnel and offer word-for-word miss what actually matters — ad budget behind them, a sales team closing $1M+/rep, and strong fulfillment. He advises copycats to compete on channels he isn't dominating, or better, join his team, since building from scratch carries risk most underestimate.
**Nuggets:**
- `[CREATIVE]` Copying a competitor's ads/funnel/offer word-for-word rarely works: the moat is the invisible machine — hundreds of thousands in spend driving thousands of leads, reps closing $1M+/month, and a strong fulfillment team. ([0:32](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=t30Xo5uFkGk&t=32s))
- `[ADS]` Cloned ads look like knock-offs to an audience that sees the bigger brand's ads far more often — no real shortcut. ([1:34](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=t30Xo5uFkGk&t=94s))
- `[ADS]` Don't fight a dominant spender on their home platform: he had ~$1.2M allocated for next month's Meta ads and says identical ads will drive up copycats' costs. Compete on channels the leader isn't mastering — cold email, LinkedIn, long-form YouTube, blogs, Google Ads. ([2:05](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=t30Xo5uFkGk&t=125s))
- `[HIRING]` Recruit from competitors/agencies: pitch skilled specialists (copywriting, funnels, video, social consulting) on doing their one thing in a higher-leverage seat; he's absorbed entire agencies' talent this way. ([3:07](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=t30Xo5uFkGk&t=187s))
- `[HIRING]` Recruiting pitch: compare your last 12 months of actual take-home (net of payouts and ad inefficiency), not hypothetical gross, to the team's on-track earnings (some staff make $50k/month). ([4:09](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=t30Xo5uFkGk&t=249s))
- `[MINDSET]` He entered with five years' experience and ~$1M he was prepared to lose, then went $500k further into debt — most underestimate the capital and risk of starting from scratch. ([5:42](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=t30Xo5uFkGk&t=342s))
- `[MINDSET]` Alternative path: work years inside an established business, build skills and a war chest (set some aside), then launch with knowledge someone else paid for. ([7:46](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=t30Xo5uFkGk&t=466s))
- `[LEADERSHIP]` The owner as "employee": he can't out-sell his best rep or out-manage his best account manager, so he runs sales changes past the sales lead and client changes past the director of client experience first; specialists beat generalists. ([9:23](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=t30Xo5uFkGk&t=563s))
- `[TEAM]` If employees leave to start businesses, congratulate and empower them. ([10:24](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=t30Xo5uFkGk&t=624s))
**Short quotes:**
- "All the stuff that you can't copy… is what's actually important." ([0:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=t30Xo5uFkGk&t=0s))
- "It's an awesome thing to own your own business. But… taking all the risk with none of the knowledge behind it — I would never wish that upon my worst enemy." ([10:24](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=t30Xo5uFkGk&t=624s))
**G&L angle:**
- Don't clone a bigger acquisition-education player's Meta ads; differentiate with G&L's own angle (advisory + vendor finance + 55+ exit readiness) and test channels larger competitors underuse — LinkedIn and cold email to 55+ owners, long-form YouTube deal breakdowns, broker/accountant partnerships.
- The moat to build: closers who can close high-ticket on cold traffic, and a fulfillment team that actually gets deals done — invest there, not in copying creatives.
- Recruit experienced brokers/advisors and closers from competitors by showing real take-home earnings and steady lead flow.
- Founder should defer to specialist leads (sales lead, head of deal execution) on changes in their area.

## I make $3M/month - Why easy businesses win — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=i4mWmsC0mTA
**Gist:** Daniel's "blue ocean" isn't a brand-new idea. It's importing proven practices from other industries (jet charter, timeshare, hospitals, software, private equity) and being the first in your own industry to use them, then competing only where you have an unfair advantage.
**Nuggets:**
- `[MINDSET]` The best new ideas come from existing successful businesses in other industries. Being first to apply one in your industry makes competitors look like they're doing something different. ([0:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=i4mWmsC0mTA&t=0s))
- `[SALES-MGMT]` From private-jet charter (short cycle, six-figure tickets): when a prospect declines, the director of sales emails "How could we have made this better?" That gives immediate feedback on every lost deal for every rep. Viral Coach adopted it. ([0:30](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=i4mWmsC0mTA&t=30s))
- `[SALES-MGMT]` From timeshare: don't burn an expensive lead after one rep's "no." Pass it to a rep with a different style (low-pressure "here's the price, accept or decline" vs high-leadership negotiator), since different buyers respond to different reps. Combined with lost-deal feedback, you capture more from every lead (being tested). ([1:32](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=i4mWmsC0mTA&t=92s))
- `[FULFILLMENT]` From hospitals: a specialist "doctor" spends brief, high-leverage time diagnosing and prescribing, then nurses and assistants execute and monitor. This contrasts with agencies that hand clients to a minimum-wage generalist account manager. It elevates the specialist's status and leverages scarce skill while keeping care high. ([3:36](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=i4mWmsC0mTA&t=216s))
- `[GUARANTEE]` From software: a 7-day free trial, unheard of for agencies. It suits clients who are problem-aware but solution-unaware: they can't judge a solution from a pitch, so demonstrate expertise and onboarding in the first 7 days. ([5:40](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=i4mWmsC0mTA&t=340s))
- `[OPERATIONS]` From private equity / M&A legal practice: have strong IP-protection lawyers. Clients stole IP, charged back or stopped paying after receiving work; a signed contract alone means little without enforcement. ([6:41](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=i4mWmsC0mTA&t=401s))
- `[MINDSET]` Business is combining best practices from many industries. Buy competitors' and other industries' products, pay for information and experience their processes; not learning from others is idiotic. ([7:42](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=i4mWmsC0mTA&t=462s))
- `[OFFER]` Only enter markets where you have an inherent advantage (his: ads for Amazon, PayPal, Dr Pepper, Robinhood; millions of followers; a 50-person team). If competing against a leader, pick a different channel (cold email, outbound, LinkedIn) rather than their home turf. ([8:13](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=i4mWmsC0mTA&t=493s))
**Short quotes:**
- "Becoming blue ocean isn't about having a new idea… It's about taking an idea that has definitely been done… and being the first person in your industry to present it." ([3:36](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=i4mWmsC0mTA&t=216s))
- "There's always one thing that you can do better than anyone else, and you have to make that your competitive advantage." ([9:15](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=i4mWmsC0mTA&t=555s))
**G&L angle:**
- Add a lost-deal feedback email from a "Director of Sales" ("How could we have made this better?") to every G&L no-sale, and offer a second call with a different-style advisor before burning the lead (e.g. a calmer, senior advisor for cautious 55+ owners).
- Structure fulfilment like a hospital: a senior M&A specialist diagnoses deal structure and valuation in short high-leverage sessions, while analysts and deal coordinators execute (sourcing, data rooms, follow-ups).
- Borrow freely from PE, business brokerage, real-estate investing and SaaS (trials, onboarding sequences). Few M&A advisors run a direct-response funnel, so that itself is G&L's blue ocean.
- Protect IP and fees contractually (deal-introduction non-circumvention, success-fee tails), since clients may try to go around you once they've seen the deal or method.
- Lead with G&L's genuine edge (e.g. the 3C model, vendor-finance expertise) and compete on channels where traditional brokers aren't present.

## How I Would Hire $3m/Mo Sales Reps (if I had to start over) — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=M0Cc-4RO2fA
**Gist:** Daniel's closer-hiring system: source from referrals, social posts and job boards; screen with a ≤5-minute Loom intro judged on visuals, then audio, then content; hire on the spot; train for a week; then give a two-week, ~$20K "half the leads" trial and cut anyone who isn't winning.
**Nuggets:**
- `[SALES-HIRING]` Scale: 100+ rep applications in 30 days; 12 reps; top reps make $50–70K commission months and some close $1M+/month. ([0:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=M0Cc-4RO2fA&t=0s))
- `[SALES-HIRING]` Source #1 is referrals: 7 of 12 reps came from them ("good reps know good reps"). Optionally pay a $5,000 referral bonus if the hire stays 90 days; a bad hire burns more than that in leads. Prospect for talent like you prospect for clients. ([0:32](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=M0Cc-4RO2fA&t=32s))
- `[SALES-HIRING]` Source #2 is social: a simple text-on-screen story post asking candidates to email a video intro brings 100+ inbound applications/month (one post overnight produced 7 good ones). A 20-second unedited reel recruited two of his best employees, and YouTube videos about rep earnings attract talent. ([1:32](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=M0Cc-4RO2fA&t=92s))
- `[SALES-HIRING]` Source #3 (from scratch) is job boards: post on ZipRecruiter, LinkedIn, Indeed and every other board, and budget several thousand dollars for your first rep. Expect hundreds of applications to find 1–2 solid reps. ([3:05](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=M0Cc-4RO2fA&t=185s))
- `[SALES-HIRING]` Write a detailed job description so candidates self-disqualify, and anchor on-target earnings (OTE) high. If you're unknown, overpay ("the tax for being unknown"; expect to pay double what he does). ([3:35](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=M0Cc-4RO2fA&t=215s))
- `[SALES-HIRING]` Set OTE honestly from your own numbers: take last month's sales you closed × ~10% commission (standard for remote sales). Expect a hire to work as hard as you but get about half your results. ([4:06](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=M0Cc-4RO2fA&t=246s))
- `[SALES-HIRING]` Don't do one-on-one interviews with hundreds of applicants (get a full-time recruiter before that), and avoid group hiring calls. Group calls look unprofessional, annoy star reps and waste time. Keep it private, over email. ([5:08](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=M0Cc-4RO2fA&t=308s))
- `[SALES-HIRING]` Screen with a ≤5-minute Loom intro (the free tier caps at 5 min). Sending a Google Drive link or another tool is an instant DQ, since it shows lack of tech skill. ([6:41](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=M0Cc-4RO2fA&t=401s))
- `[SALES-HIRING]` Visual screen from the email thumbnail alone: DQ shaky handheld phone, old webcam, bedroom, blank wall, messy background or FaceTime-style framing. A multi-six-figure business owner must trust the person with tens of thousands of dollars. Tip for candidates: record in a nice apartment lobby or a public library and get a haircut. ([7:45](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=M0Cc-4RO2fA&t=465s))
- `[SALES-HIRING]` Audio screen: ~1 in 5 submissions is deleted for bad audio (coffee shops, noise-filtering headphones). A basic microphone is required. ([10:18](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=M0Cc-4RO2fA&t=618s))
- `[SALES-HIRING]` Content screen: ignore age, "entrepreneurial since a teen" and "I work harder than anyone." Look for proof they've made a company money through sales, or an honest account of how relevant experience lets them ramp fast. You want professional, highly motivated and coachable. ([11:19](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=M0Cc-4RO2fA&t=679s))
- `[SALES-HIRING]` Decisions are made in the first 30 seconds (watching at 1.5×), so nail the opening. Example: a low-experience applicant who had studied Daniel's content, pitched himself as a future brand evangelist and did extra research closed ~$2M in 90 days and made $20K commission in his first two weeks. Overconfident openers ("why should you even bother listening…") are a no. ([12:51](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=M0Cc-4RO2fA&t=771s))
- `[SALES-HIRING]` Often there's no interview: if the Loom is clear, he hires on the spot. ([14:57](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=M0Cc-4RO2fA&t=897s))
- `[SALES-MGMT]` Onboarding week: tools setup, handbook, reviewing successful sales calls and training videos, learning the pitch, then integrating with the sales lead. ([15:29](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=M0Cc-4RO2fA&t=929s))
- `[SALES-MGMT]` After week one, give the new rep half the normal lead flow for ~2 weeks (costs ~$20K = one week of leads), so they treat each lead carefully. No wins after ~3 weeks: let them go. Cut losses at one week's lead cost. ([15:29](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=M0Cc-4RO2fA&t=929s))
- `[SALES-MGMT]` Unless you're a sales-training company, your job isn't training reps. Hire reps who train themselves. His first dozen reps burned hundreds of thousands of dollars while he hoped they'd improve in months 2–5; they didn't. ([15:59](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=M0Cc-4RO2fA&t=959s))
- `[KPIS]` He "moneyballs" the sales team with 20+ KPIs, judging performance from numbers without reviewing calls (covered in a separate video). ([16:31](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=M0Cc-4RO2fA&t=991s))
**Short quotes:**
- "If you don't have a reputation yet, you are going to have to overpay for your talent. That is the tax for being unknown." ([3:35](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=M0Cc-4RO2fA&t=215s))
- "Unless you are a sales training company, your job should not be training sales reps." ([15:59](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=M0Cc-4RO2fA&t=959s))
**G&L angle:**
- Hire closers for G&L with a ≤5-minute Loom screen. For 55+ business owners, require professional setting, clean audio and gravitas; a rep must credibly discuss multi-million-dollar exits and seller finance.
- Set OTE from Bendik's own close data (e.g. last month's cash collected × ~10%), advertise it high, and run referral bonuses (~$5K at 90 days) through M&A, brokerage and finance networks, where good reps know good reps.
- Prefer candidates with B2B/finance/brokerage sales track records, or who prove they've studied G&L content (an "evangelist" signal).
- Onboard for one week (handbook, recorded winning calls, VSL/pitch), then a fixed two-week, half-lead-flow trial with a hard cut-off on lead cost if there are no closes.
- Use social stories and YouTube about rep earnings to build a constant rep pipeline.

## I told my employees to create an ad. They made me $1m. — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=fFmGhsryRiI
**Gist:** Daniel ran an internal ad-creation competition (41 ads from 13 staff) with a strict three-phase budget-scaling test to find creatives that hold KPI above $1,000/day. Most spend went to losers, but the winners cut cost per qualified call ~53% and revealed a new winning format: text-on-screen B-roll loops with heavy copy.
**Nuggets:**
- `[KPIS]` Context: ~$3M revenue from ~$825K/mo Meta spend, pushed through only ~13 active ads; one ad spent $10K/day, another $2M+ lifetime. Goal is a few "golden goose" ads that scale to thousands per day. ([0:31](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=fFmGhsryRiI&t=31s))
- `[ADS]` At their spend level, ads that can't scale past ~$600/day are of little use; post-Andromeda, 15 near-identical ads with tweaked hooks at $100/day doesn't work — Meta rewards genuinely varied ads for different awareness levels and publicly discourages AI-generated sameness. ([1:01](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=fFmGhsryRiI&t=61s))
- `[TEAM]` Internal competition: ~$8.5K prize pool ($5K / $2.5K / $1K), ~$50K expected test budget. Timeline: announce 2 weeks out, raw files due 1 week out, team edits, then a 7-day test; winners announced day 8. ([2:35](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=fFmGhsryRiI&t=155s))
- `[ADS]` Test protocol: same copy and headline for all ads, same funnel (no VSL), same starting budget, each run to ~$2,100 spend; KPI = cost per *qualified* call, with the control set at the prior 30-day average (~$450). ([3:05](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=fFmGhsryRiI&t=185s))
- `[KPIS]` High cost per call is fine when deal value is high: they sell only to multi-millionaire owners, talk to just ~9% of applicants due to strict qualification, and earn tens of thousands per sale. Don't compare your CPC/CPL to other niches. ([3:35](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=fFmGhsryRiI&t=215s))
- `[ADS]` Phase 1 (48h): 41 ads at $600/day (~$1,200 each); expected 2–3 calls if on KPI; ads with <1 call cut. Whole $51K budget spent in 2 days because Meta liked the ads enough to spend the full budget. ([5:08](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=fFmGhsryRiI&t=308s))
- `[ADS]` Phase 2 (2 days): 18 survivors scaled 50% to $900/day (~$3,000 cumulative); fewer than 6 calls = cut. Top 5 averaged $237 per qualified call, staying flat despite the overnight 50% increase. ([6:40](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=fFmGhsryRiI&t=400s))
- `[ADS]` Phase 3 (3 days): $1,350/day (~$7,000 cumulative), needed 15–16 calls; 11 remained as permanent assets, top five at $239/call (53% below control) even after spend rose 56%. Weak ads ballooned to ~3× KPI when scaled and were killed. ([8:13](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=fFmGhsryRiI&t=493s))
- `[ADS]` Outcome: ~$76K spent eliminating losers, ~60% of spend on ads never reused, 30% of ads booked zero calls; ~7% of submissions (about three creatives) drove most results. "Failure tax" is the cost of buying confidence fast — especially right after an algorithm change. ([9:13](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=fFmGhsryRiI&t=553s))
- `[TEAM]` It worked because sales reps (who talk to prospects daily) and account managers (who make content daily) made the ads; without that skill expect perhaps a tenth of the result and need hundreds of creatives over months. ([10:46](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=fFmGhsryRiI&t=646s))
- `[HIRING]` Uses YouTube partly as a recruiting channel: candidates email a video intro plus resume. ([11:47](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=fFmGhsryRiI&t=707s))
- `[CREATIVE]` Winning hooks were pain-driven, in prospect language: "Your current agency is charging more, your CAC's getting worse, and you're still stuck with inconsistent leads" — lifted straight from what clients say on calls. ([15:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=fFmGhsryRiI&t=900s))
- `[CREATIVE]` "You hire us, then you fire us" positioning (build the system, no endless retainer) — that ad closed a $30K paid-in-full deal in its first week. ([16:32](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=fFmGhsryRiI&t=992s))
- `[CREATIVE]` Heavy editing can overpower the script: an over-produced ad was fun but prospects couldn't digest the message to book. Aspirational setting/outfit (suit, premium office) that matches how the target buyer wants to see themselves works, as long as the talking points still shine. ([20:12](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=fFmGhsryRiI&t=1212s))
- `[ADS]` Slow starters can win: the third-place ad nearly got cut in phase 1, then took off once Meta figured out who to show it to. ([21:44](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=fFmGhsryRiI&t=1304s))
- `[UGC]` Plain UGC-style scripted pitches ("12 million views — that's what our average client gets after 3 months of posting") work; sometimes people just want to hear the pitch, stripped down. Second place was the creator's first-ever recorded video. ([22:47](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=fFmGhsryRiI&t=1367s))
- `[CREATIVE]` Big learning: B-roll loops with heavy text-on-screen copy (guarantee + how it works + CTA) took 1st and 3rd. Readable without sound and enough info to explain guarantee and mechanism; earlier text reels failed because they had *too little* copy. Plan: make ~50 more. ([24:21](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=fFmGhsryRiI&t=1461s))
**Short quotes:**
- "With ads, the failure tax is what you have to pay to buy confidence." ([9:44](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=fFmGhsryRiI&t=584s))
- "He still lets the script shine... if you don't have those [talking points], you're not going to book a call." ([20:12](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=fFmGhsryRiI&t=1212s))
**G&L angle:**
- Run a G&L creative competition: setters, closers and deal team each script and film an ad using the exact objections/pains they hear (e.g. "Your broker wants 10% and two years, and you still might not sell"); offer cash prizes judged on cost per qualified call.
- Copy the 3-phase test: fixed copy/headline/funnel, cost per *qualified* call KPI set from the last 30 days, scale survivors +50% per phase and cut on hard call thresholds — so you find ads that hold KPI at higher budgets.
- Expect a high cost per call: G&L sells to well-off owners and buyers with large fees, so judge against LTV, not coaching-niche benchmarks.
- Test text-on-screen B-roll ads (business-for-sale imagery, "Buy a cash-flowing business with seller finance — here's how it works") that spell out the guarantee/mechanism in readable copy.
- Use aspirational visuals for buyers (owner-operator, office, deal signing) without letting editing bury the core message.

## These 6 minutes will make you more successful — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ZDs39zefkiM
**Gist:** A personal motivation piece: Daniel ($3M last month, pacing toward ~$5M) recalls being an awkward kid making cringe videos he hid from his wife. Embarrassment tolerance is a competitive advantage because most people never do what's embarrassing; trying is the only way to lose less.
**Nuggets:**
- `[MINDSET]` Revenue context: $3M last month, high-$3Ms this month, pacing ~$5M next month, "a lot of that is profit." ([0:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ZDs39zefkiM&t=0s))
- `[ORGANIC]` Content is "weird until it works." His first ~30 YouTube videos were so bad he filmed them while his wife was at work and hid them. You get used to anything you do for years. ([0:31](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ZDs39zefkiM&t=31s))
- `[MINDSET]` Success comes from doing what everyone else is too embarrassed to do. Most people haven't felt enough embarrassment to build tolerance for it. ([1:34](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ZDs39zefkiM&t=94s))
- `[MINDSET]` Confidence is built by doing awkward things over and over. Of course you change; that's the point. ([3:07](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ZDs39zefkiM&t=187s))
- `[MINDSET]` Make "do something different and get experience" the goal rather than "be successful," and every attempt becomes a win. Results you've never had require doing things you've never done. ([4:08](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ZDs39zefkiM&t=248s))
- `[MINDSET]` "No one is stopping you from trying": if you lose, you lose to yourself by giving up. It takes years; be comfortable with yourself because you'll be you forever. ([4:40](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ZDs39zefkiM&t=280s))
**Short quotes:**
- "That was weird, but it was weird until it works." ([0:31](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ZDs39zefkiM&t=31s))
- "What you do tomorrow is always going to be more important than what happened to you yesterday." ([5:44](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ZDs39zefkiM&t=344s))
**G&L angle:**
- Get Bendik and the G&L advisors on camera now (ad creatives, VSL, organic YouTube on deal breakdowns). Early videos will be awkward, but few M&A advisors are willing to do it, which is the edge.
- Commit to a long content runway (years). Personal-brand trust is what lets a high-ticket advisory command premium fees from 55+ owners.

## Every number I tracked to hit $3m/mo — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=jgkuFYLRLnA
**Gist:** Daniel "moneyballs" his sales team with 20+ KPIs per rep in a shared sheet, anchored on revenue per lead, show-to-qualified rate, qualified close rate and an "operational drag" figure comparing each rep with the best rep in the same lead tier. Managing purely by these numbers, against team and tier averages, took them from $1.4M to $3M/month in 30 days.
**Nuggets:**
- `[SALES-MGMT]` Backstory: $500K in debt, $400 in the bank, half the sales team laid off. As a former accountant with no sales background, he stopped trying to manage on sales psychology and managed on objective numbers instead. ([0:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=jgkuFYLRLnA&t=0s))
- `[KPIS]` Per-rep sheet columns: leads allocated, calls booked, shows, call-to-show %, qualified, show-to-qualified %, deals won, qualified close %, show-to-close %, lead-to-close ratio, revenue per sale, total revenue, revenue per call, revenue per lead, commission month-to-date, estimated monthly commission, effective hourly rate, operational cost — plus team and tier averages. (Template offered free.) ([1:33](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=jgkuFYLRLnA&t=93s))
- `[SALES-MGMT]` Kill the "leads suck" excuse with team/tier averages: reps in the same tier get the same lead quality, so a rep whose show-to-qualified rate is far below peers has a confidence problem, not a lead problem. ([2:04](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=jgkuFYLRLnA&t=124s))
- `[KPIS]` Revenue per call (learned from Gym Launch's sales lead) = rep revenue ÷ calls taken. Better still is revenue per lead, because it captures whether the rep follows up and gets allocated leads onto calls — the company pays for every lead, not just every call. ([3:05](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=jgkuFYLRLnA&t=185s))
- `[KPIS]` Operational drag/cost = (best rep's revenue per lead in the tier − this rep's revenue per lead) × leads allocated to this rep. Example: ~$1K/lead gap × 177 leads ≈ $150–185K/month lost versus giving those leads to the #1 rep; a rep ~$2K/lead worse cost ~$250K. ([4:08](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=jgkuFYLRLnA&t=248s))
- `[SALES-MGMT]` Volume can hide inefficiency: one tier-2 rep had among the highest revenue and the highest commission in his group, but took almost double the leads and had the lowest revenue per lead — about $600K of operational drag. Most teams would call him a top performer. ([5:40](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=jgkuFYLRLnA&t=340s))
- `[SALES-MGMT]` Call-to-show % shows who is good at pre-call communication: an 84% rep vs a much lower one means the weak rep is losing ~35% of the leads the better rep gets — coach him to copy the top rep's pre-call messaging. Pre-call follow-up off the call can double a rep's commission. ([7:41](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=jgkuFYLRLnA&t=461s))
- `[SALES-MGMT]` Show-to-qualified % measures a rep's confidence in the leads (qualified is judged after discovery, so it's subjective). Example: a 73% rep vs peers at 91% is "disqualifying" people he could have pitched — likely to blame marketing; send him to watch peers' calls. Tier 2 shows similar spreads (80% vs 50%). ([8:11](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=jgkuFYLRLnA&t=491s))
- `[KPIS]` Qualified close % = deals won ÷ prospects the rep themselves called qualified; a low figure means the rep accepts the leads are good but isn't closing them. Show-to-close is the broader version. ([9:44](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=jgkuFYLRLnA&t=584s))
- `[KPIS]` Lead-to-close ratio = leads marketing must supply for one deal (best rep 10:1 vs another 25:1). Track average revenue per sale when packages/price points vary. ([10:46](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=jgkuFYLRLnA&t=646s))
- `[KPIS]` Top reps closed $852K (part-time, with a week off) and targets of $1.2–1.5M/rep/month; multiple reps on pace for $1M+ in a month. ([11:17](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=jgkuFYLRLnA&t=677s))
- `[FINANCE]` Forecasting: marketing spend allocated per rep ÷ CPL = leads; leads × that rep's revenue per lead (or calls × revenue per call) = forecast revenue. That's how they planned a $3.9M month. ([11:48](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=jgkuFYLRLnA&t=708s))
- `[SALES-MGMT]` Mid-month estimated-commission projection (based on open calendar hours) lets reps choose to take time off or open Saturdays/Sundays to hit their income goal. ([12:50](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=jgkuFYLRLnA&t=770s))
- `[SALES-HIRING]` Effective hourly rate = commission month-to-date ÷ showed appointments (each call rounded up to one hour). Mainly a recruiting tool — showing it in interviews is very compelling to strong, KPI-loving reps. ([13:20](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=jgkuFYLRLnA&t=800s))
- `[SALES-MGMT]` Lead tiers (colour-coded) with their own averages let you value a lead before the call (e.g. a Gmail address vs an enterprise email is worth a known, different amount) and KPI reps against their tier, not the whole team. ([14:22](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=jgkuFYLRLnA&t=862s))
**Short quotes:**
- "The leads suck argument is absolutely not true." ([2:34](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=jgkuFYLRLnA&t=154s))
- "I was going to stop trying to win a game that I didn't understand... and I was just going to moneyball the whole thing." ([0:31](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=jgkuFYLRLnA&t=31s))
**G&L angle:**
- Build the G&L rep scorecard with these columns, split by lead tier (e.g. seller with $1M+ EBITDA, seller under $1M, funded buyer, unfunded buyer) so each closer is benchmarked against peers on the same lead quality.
- Make revenue per lead the primary rep metric and compute operational drag monthly to decide who gets the premium seller leads; rewards efficiency, not lead hoarding.
- Watch show-to-qualified %: closers who "DQ" too many aspiring buyers for low capital may be missing creative vendor-finance structures — have them review top reps' calls.
- Forecast monthly revenue from ad budget per rep ÷ CPL × revenue per lead; publish effective hourly rate when recruiting commission-only closers.

## How I Built My $3M/Month Team (so my business runs without me) — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Ds1rAvgIzrA
**Gist:** Daniel walks through his ~50-person org chart at $3M/month in hiring order: a founding trifecta (get clients, keep clients, keep everyone out of jail), then alternating hiring waves between sales and fulfillment, then splitting ops into specialists. He explains a light-touch sales lead model, ~6% sales cost on topline, well-paid six-figure roles, an outsourced production partner, and the next hires he forecasts for $5M/month.
**Nuggets:**
- `[TEAM]` Founding trifecta (from Dan Kennedy): one person gets clients (CMO — his wife, running ads), one keeps clients (CEO — fulfillment at first), one "keeps the other two out of jail" (COO). Every service business needs this core three, even pre-revenue. ([0:30](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Ds1rAvgIzrA&t=30s))
- `[HIRING]` First hires freed the founder from fulfillment: an experienced account manager (a friend) to lead client accounts, plus a senior production manager and a video editor, so he could focus on strategy and sales. ([1:31](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Ds1rAvgIzrA&t=91s))
- `[SALES-HIRING]` While he was the only closer, his first sales hire was a sales development rep/assistant to manage his calendar, DQ non-fits and handle pre-call communication. Then four more closers plus another SDR — headcount 9 by month two selling. ([2:03](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Ds1rAvgIzrA&t=123s))
- `[SCALING-STAGES]` Revenue ramp: $250K in month two (reps still ramping), $500K in month three once reps were full-time, ~$1M/month by the next wave, then $3M at ~50 people. ([2:34](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Ds1rAvgIzrA&t=154s))
- `[SCALING-STAGES]` Hire in alternating waves — sales/acquisition, then fulfillment, then legal/ops — "flip-flopping" focus. Hire a role only when tasks previously done by the COO/CMO become overwhelming enough to fill full-time jobs. ([3:06](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Ds1rAvgIzrA&t=186s))
- `[SALES-MGMT]` Sales team lead = player-coach with integrity and management experience, not a trainer: a once-a-week huddle (mostly admin and marketing changes), onboarding new hires, sounding board and spokesperson for new initiatives. No daily training or heavy call review. ([3:36](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Ds1rAvgIzrA&t=216s))
- `[SALES-HIRING]` Hire experienced reps who can close hundreds of thousands to $1M/month out of the gate rather than training novices. Sales costs ~6% of topline, yet some reps are on track for ~$500K/year in commissions. ([4:06](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Ds1rAvgIzrA&t=246s))
- `[FULFILLMENT]` At ~$1M/month, the next five hires were account managers and associate AMs (direct assistants to AMs). Fulfillment needed to double from 7 to 14 people. ([5:06](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Ds1rAvgIzrA&t=306s))
- `[LEADERSHIP]` He promoted an internal AM to CXO (chief experience officer) to own and build client experience; the department now grows on its own with better QC, while he keeps weekly visibility through the CXO. ([5:37](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Ds1rAvgIzrA&t=337s))
- `[OPERATIONS]` Split the COO's generalist load into specialists: an operations specialist (cross-department liaison), a controller (payroll, tax compliance, bookkeeping) and general counsel. Specialists outperform founder-generalists — the business felt easier. ([6:07](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Ds1rAvgIzrA&t=367s))
- `[SCALING-STAGES]` A bigger business really is easier to run: the tipping point was ~30 employees, when every base was covered by someone smarter than him in that role. ([7:42](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Ds1rAvgIzrA&t=462s))
- `[HIRING]` Pay well: all "yellow" org-chart roles are six-figure positions (blue = offshore/entry-level); no $2/hour VAs for core ops. Many roles out-earn the business owners they serve — they've hired staff away from clients, and reps out-earning prospects carry authority on calls. ([8:44](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Ds1rAvgIzrA&t=524s))
- `[PARTNERSHIP]` Production is outsourced to a long-time collaborator's company under a seven-figure contract — a vendor model where they deal only with that firm's CEO/CFO/COO, who manage 30+ staff. ([9:47](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Ds1rAvgIzrA&t=587s))
- `[HIRING]` An internal recruiter was a "game changer" — staffing sales and fulfillment faster and more compliantly than founders or department heads. Next planned hire: director of tech (previously done by the COO), then dedicated assistants/mini-departments for the C-suite and general counsel. ([10:18](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Ds1rAvgIzrA&t=618s))
- `[SCALING-STAGES]` Forecast headcount from revenue: going from $3M to $5M/month needs about 4 more sales reps and 8 more account managers, with operations staying structurally similar. ([11:21](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Ds1rAvgIzrA&t=681s))
**Short quotes:**
- "You need one person to get the clients, one person to keep the clients, and then one person to keep the other two people out of jail." ([1:01](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Ds1rAvgIzrA&t=61s))
- "I have people working on those issues that are smarter than the people that are complaining about them." ([9:47](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Ds1rAvgIzrA&t=587s))
**G&L angle:**
- Map G&L's trifecta explicitly: acquisition (ads/VSL/sales), delivery (deal advisors running valuations, buyer search, structuring, diligence) and compliance/ops (legal, contracts, finance) — and know who owns each.
- First sales hire: an SDR/setter to run the calendar, DQ and do pre-call contact while the founder closes; then experienced high-ticket closers who need little training, with a player-coach lead running one weekly huddle.
- Alternate hiring waves between closers and deal-delivery staff so signed mandates don't stall; forecast the advisor-to-revenue ratio in advance.
- Outsource specialist work (legal, valuation, accounting/QoE) to partner firms on contract instead of building large in-house teams; pay core roles well — closers who are clearly successful carry authority with business owners.

## I make $3m/mo. Here are 5 business lessons I wish I knew earlier. — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=J0YiARB-zXY
**Gist:** Five costly lessons. Email is only about 3% of revenue, so delay long nurture sequences until $1M/month. Always hire at least two people per role so you can compare them. Hire people better than you. Understand the customer through audience, positioning and psychology (including a 7-day guarantee). Above all, control customer acquisition.
**Nuggets:**
- `[EMAIL]` Email has consistently been 2–3% of top-line revenue at every stage of scale, with about a 0.02% conversion rate even on proven content. Set up only call reminders and cart-abandon emails until about $1M/month, then think about long nurture sequences. ([0:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=J0YiARB-zXY&t=0s), [0:30](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=J0YiARB-zXY&t=30s))
- `[HIRING]` Assume no employee is doing their job well until you can compare. Always hire at least two people for any new position (the "compare fruit side by side" analogy). ([0:30](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=J0YiARB-zXY&t=30s), [1:01](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=J0YiARB-zXY&t=61s))
- `[KPIS]` KPIs by role. Sales: total revenue, revenue per lead, refunds, LTV per sale. Fulfilment: refunds, support tickets, client results, and frequency of client communication (a leading indicator). Hard-to-KPI cost centres (accounting, legal, support management): be deeply involved or put a trusted manager over them, and don't scale those teams until you can. ([1:32](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=J0YiARB-zXY&t=92s))
- `[HIRING]` Keep hiring until everyone is better than you at their role. If you, the distracted generalist, outperform your specialist, that's a failure, not a flex (broom vs vacuum). If you can't find reps who outsell you, the business is capped at your skill. ([2:02](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=J0YiARB-zXY&t=122s), [2:33](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=J0YiARB-zXY&t=153s))
- `[OFFER]` Audience shift: they moved away from the coaches-and-consultants niche (hard to afford, not serious) toward traditional businesses (plumbers, dentists, contractors, doctors) that already spend on marketing. The pitch became reallocating existing marketing budget, not spending new money. ([3:04](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=J0YiARB-zXY&t=184s), [3:35](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=J0YiARB-zXY&t=215s))
- `[OFFER]` Positioning shift: from "a social media company that does marketing" to "a marketing company that specialises in social media". New things sold under a label buyers already trust. ([4:05](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=J0YiARB-zXY&t=245s))
- `[GUARANTEE]` For problem-aware but solution-unaware buyers, who can't judge whether a solution is legitimate, add a 7-day money-back guarantee or trial so they don't need to fully understand it to start. ([5:06](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=J0YiARB-zXY&t=306s), [5:38](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=J0YiARB-zXY&t=338s))
- `[OBJECTIONS]` "I need to think about it" → "Perfect, that's exactly why we offer a week, so you have something real to think about. After 7 days you'll be confident either way." ([5:38](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=J0YiARB-zXY&t=338s))
- `[SCALING-STAGES]` The #1 mistake was not tracking and controlling where revenue came from. He nearly went bankrupt ($400 in the bank). Acquisition comes first, before back-end systems, branding or automations. Once controllable, they went from $80K to $1M a month in 4 months. ([6:09](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=J0YiARB-zXY&t=369s), [6:39](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=J0YiARB-zXY&t=399s))
- `[MINDSET]` Bonus: you often win simply by being willing to risk more than anyone else. ([6:39](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=J0YiARB-zXY&t=399s))
**Short quotes:**
- "You should be able to choose how much money you make per month." ([6:39](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=J0YiARB-zXY&t=399s))
- "We became a marketing company that specializes in social media." ([4:35](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=J0YiARB-zXY&t=275s))
**G&L angle:**
- Don't over-build email nurture early. Get call reminders, application-abandon and no-show emails right, and put the rest of the effort into ads and sales.
- Hire setters and closers (and deal analysts) in pairs, and judge them on revenue per lead, refunds and client outcomes.
- Position G&L inside a category buyers already trust ("M&A advisory that specialises in vendor-financed acquisitions" or "exit planning for owners 55+") rather than a new-sounding concept, and target people who already spend on advisers, brokers or accountants.
- Many prospects don't know what seller finance is (solution-unaware). Consider a low-risk start, such as a deal-assessment sprint or an initial-period guarantee, to answer "let me think about it".
- Make acquisition predictable first. Fix the ads → application → call funnel economics before investing in CRM or automation polish.

## Give me 4 minutes and you will never need motivation again — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=aeU9bODy7yQ
**Gist:** A short motivational piece: a year after having $400 in the bank, his company does ~$210–280K/day. He attributes it to aggressive pursuit of progress, framing every day below his $10M/month potential (~$330K/day) as an opportunity cost, and to relying on a clear reference point of what's possible rather than on motivation.
**Nuggets:**
- `[MINDSET]` From $400 in the bank to $210–280K revenue days within a year; what got him out was being "incredibly aggressive about progress" — up at 4am, furious at not having it figured out yet. ([0:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=aeU9bODy7yQ&t=0s))
- `[LEADERSHIP]` He respects work-life balance but sets the expectation that people in KPI-measured roles will be pushed to improve every day — throughput, competitiveness, no wasted time. ([1:04](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=aeU9bODy7yQ&t=64s))
- `[MINDSET]` Opportunity-cost framing: he believes the company can do $10M/month (~$330K/day), so every day below that is a measurable loss — and a cost to employees whose commission is capped by broken tools or bad processes he hasn't fixed. ([1:35](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=aeU9bODy7yQ&t=95s))
- `[MINDSET]` "There is no neutral": standing still means falling behind while others move. Motivation comes and goes; use it when it appears to fuel something else, and anchor on the pain of the gap between your current and possible life. ([2:38](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=aeU9bODy7yQ&t=158s))
- `[MINDSET]` You don't need motivation, just one reference point proving that what you want is possible for someone else. ([3:41](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=aeU9bODy7yQ&t=221s))
**Short quotes:**
- "In life, there is no neutral. There's no taking a break. You can't hit pause." ([2:38](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=aeU9bODy7yQ&t=158s))
- "You just need one point of reference to see what you want is actually possible for someone else." ([4:12](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=aeU9bODy7yQ&t=252s))
**G&L angle:**
- Define G&L's "full potential" daily revenue (capacity of closers × revenue per call) and show the daily gap to the team, so process fixes are framed as lost commission for reps.
- Use "reference point" messaging in creative: show real buyers who acquired with seller finance and owners who exited well, because a believable peer example motivates prospects more than hype.

## How I scaled my ads from $0 to $1M/mo (step-by-step) — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=qyBmpjamXWU
**Gist:** A tier-by-tier playbook for Meta ad spend ($0–10K, $10–50K, $50–180K, $180–300K, $360–750K, $1M+/month): how many new and proven creatives, angles, funnels and brand faces each tier needs, and what breaks at each level. The core thesis is that creative and offer, not media buying, drive success, and that profit beats spend.
**Nuggets:**
- `[ADS]` Track per month and per dollar of spend: number of individual creatives, new creatives/month, angles, funnels, and (at high spend) brand faces. Media buying is now easy ("allocate budget, Facebook decides"); creative strategy and offer make the money. ([0:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=qyBmpjamXWU&t=0s))
- `[ADS]` Below $10K/month most businesses are wasting money. Spending >$10K/month already puts you in the top 25% of Meta spenders (per Databox). ([1:02](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=qyBmpjamXWU&t=62s))
- `[ADS]` $10–50K/mo ($300–1,670/day): 5–10 new creatives/month on top of 5–10 running, 1–3 angles, ONE funnel (one offer to one demographic), optimised. The challenge is validating the offer with cold traffic that doesn't know you. ([2:03](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=qyBmpjamXWU&t=123s))
- `[CREATIVE]` At the starting tier, how clearly the ad communicates what you do to the target demographic drives ~95% of front-end success. Find which niche or customer type responds and double down on that offer, message or demographic. ([4:07](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=qyBmpjamXWU&t=247s))
- `[ADS]` Testing math: test each creative at 3–5× your target CPA. With a $1,000 CAC and $30K/mo spread over 120 creatives (~$250 each), you're $2,750–4,750 short per creative, so validating one batch takes 12–20 months. Most companies go bankrupt testing. Make fewer, better creatives or spend more per creative. ([6:11](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=qyBmpjamXWU&t=371s))
- `[KPIS]` Optimise for revenue, not CTR, opt-ins, cheapest calls or shows. The cheapest leads are often non-buyers, which nearly bankrupted him (~$500K unsecured debt). A media buyer who doesn't own bottom-line revenue is doing you a disservice. ([7:13](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=qyBmpjamXWU&t=433s))
- `[ADS]` $50–180K/mo ($1,600–6,000/day): 10–15 new creatives + 10–20 proven, 3–4 proven angles, 1–2 funnels. Above ~$5K/day you must broaden targeting (e.g. niche plumbers → all home-services owners, US + Canada) and colder, more skeptical prospects show up. ([8:14](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=qyBmpjamXWU&t=494s))
- `[CREATIVE]` Use Eugene Schwartz's levels of awareness. Aware audiences (low spend) respond to guarantees, claims, proof and discounts; unaware audiences (high spend) need stories and secrets. ([9:17](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=qyBmpjamXWU&t=557s))
- `[CREATIVE]` If you're new to making content ads, expect 100–150 creatives/month (3–5 videos/day) to get through the $50–180K stage. ([10:18](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=qyBmpjamXWU&t=618s))
- `[ADS]` $180–300K/mo: 15–30 new + 20–50 proven creatives, at least 5 angles (aim for 10, keep testing new ones), 1–2 funnels with low-budget tests of new funnels. "Creative fatigue isn't real": you've exhausted a small audience and now need ads that appeal to wide, uninformed audiences. ([10:49](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=qyBmpjamXWU&t=649s))
- `[FUNNEL]` Make ads funnel-specific. Each ad should know which funnel, offer part, VSL/landing page and action it's for. Example: every guarantee-angle ad goes to a dedicated guarantee funnel; "scale your business" (unaware, story) ads go to the congruent scale funnel. ([11:50](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=qyBmpjamXWU&t=710s))
- `[PROFIT]` Profit over spend. $300K spend at 20% CAC making $240K profit beats $600K at 40% CAC: the profit looks the same but you get double the clients, fulfilment cost, complexity and overhead, so you actually net less. ([12:52](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=qyBmpjamXWU&t=772s))
- `[KPIS]` Rule: when ad spend creeps above 25% of revenue, pull back testing and reallocate to previous winners. ([13:24](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=qyBmpjamXWU&t=804s))
- `[ADS]` $360–750K/mo (~$25K/day): 30–80 new + 30–60 proven creatives, several angles and funnels, plus multiple brand faces (they tested ~16 brand faces with great success). ([13:55](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=qyBmpjamXWU&t=835s))
- `[OPERATIONS]` At this level you need a data analyst reviewing numbers daily, a unique identifier on every creative, decisions made only from data ("creatives are cogs"), and daily micromanagement of the account. You can't just blast it and let Facebook figure it out. ([14:25](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=qyBmpjamXWU&t=865s))
- `[UGC]` Test 1–2 new brand faces/month; when a face fatigues (costs rise), rotate in a new spokesperson for part of the budget. ([15:28](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=qyBmpjamXWU&t=928s))
- `[VSL]` The "VSL problem" with brand faces: either the UGC creator records their own VSL and pre-call video and delivers it as well as you, or (harder but simpler to run) build an offer that converts without a VSL so dozens of UGC creators can make ads. They cracked the latter. ([15:28](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=qyBmpjamXWU&t=928s))
- `[ADS]` $1M+/mo (~$33K/day): rising CPA, a mass-market offer, very wide demographic, 70–80+ genuinely new concepts/month (not hook variations). ([16:30](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=qyBmpjamXWU&t=990s))
- `[FINANCE]` "Recover CAC on the front end and roll it over" breaks around $1M/mo spend. You must be able to break even or lose on the front end and fund ads from future receivables, upsells and continuity, so know your true LTV beyond the first 30 days. ([17:32](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=qyBmpjamXWU&t=1052s))
**Short quotes:**
- "So much of the success is on the creative and the offer, not the actual media buying." ([0:31](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=qyBmpjamXWU&t=31s))
- "Scaling doesn't matter if you're not profitable." ([13:55](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=qyBmpjamXWU&t=835s))
**G&L angle:**
- At sub-$50K/mo spend, run ONE funnel to ONE avatar (e.g. aspiring acquirers, or 55+ owners — not both at once), with 5–10 high-quality new creatives/month across 1–3 angles (vendor finance/no money down, "buy a cash-flowing business," exit-ready).
- Budget creative tests at 3–5× target CPA. If a booked qualified call is worth ~$X, don't spread spend over dozens of weak ads. Judge ads on closed revenue, not cheapest leads or calls.
- Build angle-congruent funnels: a "seller finance explained" angle → buy-side VSL; an "exit in 3 years at a premium" angle → sell-side VSL.
- Cap ad spend around 25% of revenue; when it creeps over, stop testing and scale proven winners.
- Advisory retainers plus success fees produce back-end revenue, so model true LTV (retainer + success fee + post-acquisition services) to know how much you can afford to lose on the front end.

## I spent 700k on Facebook ads - here's how much I made — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=7u3gnQT-IDo
**Gist:** A full allocation breakdown of a $700K/month Meta ad spend that produced about $3M in new revenue: creative counts and spend per creative, eight offer angles, five congruent funnels, multiple brand faces, split ad accounts, an ad naming convention, and how the top three ads took 63% of spend.
**Nuggets:**
- `[KPIS]` August: $700K ad spend → just under $3M in new revenue (plus recurring and upsells). Ad spend was 24% of top-line revenue, about 4× ROAS. Daniel tracks ad spend as a percentage of revenue. ([0:31](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=7u3gnQT-IDo&t=31s))
- `[CREATIVE]` 96 new unique videos (hundreds of variations) plus 50 older creatives = 146 live, so about $7,300 of spend per new creative and about $5K per total creative. Rule of thumb: at least one really good video per $5K of spend. Less-skilled advertisers may need about one per $400 of spend (roughly 100× more creative). ([1:02](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=7u3gnQT-IDo&t=62s), [2:05](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=7u3gnQT-IDo&t=125s))
- `[CREATIVE]` Angles are different use-cases of the same offer (the towel analogy: drying hands vs emergency bandage). Eight angles in August: guarantee, time savings, new vs legacy media, staffing/training an editor, views, leads and clients, scaling the business, client testimonials. ([2:36](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=7u3gnQT-IDo&t=156s), [3:38](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=7u3gnQT-IDo&t=218s))
- `[OFFER]` Sell a component on its own as an entry angle (e.g. "staff a professional editor"), then sell the strategy the editor needs once they're interested. ([3:38](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=7u3gnQT-IDo&t=218s))
- `[FUNNEL]` Keep ad and funnel congruent: five funnels (million-view guarantee, Daniel-fronted, Nico-fronted, Margot-fronted, and one with no VSL). Whoever is the spokesperson in the ad also presents in the funnel. ([4:41](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=7u3gnQT-IDo&t=281s))
- `[CREATIVE]` Multiple brand faces with budget split by face. Daniel took about 75% in August, dropping to about 65%. Margot about 20%, Nico about 3% (cut back for weaker reception), Josh ramping toward 20%. The plan for October was 16 brand reps. ([5:11](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=7u3gnQT-IDo&t=311s))
- `[UGC]` The unlock that allows 16+ (even 1,600) brand reps: a funnel page that converts without a VSL, so each face doesn't need their own 10-minute VSL. It's UGC run the way consumer-packaged-goods brands do it, applied to a service business. ([6:14](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=7u3gnQT-IDo&t=374s))
- `[ADS]` Spread spend across 2–3 ad accounts on the same pixel to reduce ban risk (50+ families on payroll). Anecdotally, a single account seems to go too broad past a certain spend. ([7:16](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=7u3gnQT-IDo&t=436s))
- `[TOOLS]` Ad naming convention, tracked in Airtable: ad number per creator, creator name, abbreviated hook, length (m:ss), format (vertical / 1:1 / horizontal), and the MMYY when it was scripted (not published), so you remember what season of the business produced the idea. Example: "Ad160_Daniel_10M views or you don't pay_1:12_vertical_0425". ([8:17](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=7u3gnQT-IDo&t=497s), [9:18](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=7u3gnQT-IDo&t=558s))
- `[CREATIVE]` Top ad (Ad 160, "10 million views or you don't pay", 1:12, vertical) spent about $325K in one month at 3.27× ROAS. A different angle ("if you're paying an employee", long-form video 21, ad #10) spent $55K for $376K revenue (6.7× ROAS), and $2M lifetime. ([9:49](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=7u3gnQT-IDo&t=589s), [10:19](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=7u3gnQT-IDo&t=619s))
- `[ADS]` Power law: three videos took 63% of the $700K budget, and the rest went to testing and scaling 143 others. Finding one more winner of that calibre is worth about $1M a month in profitable revenue, so the job is to keep testing for it. ([10:49](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=7u3gnQT-IDo&t=649s))
- `[MINDSET]` They were $500K in debt while figuring out the paid acquisition funnel. ([11:50](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=7u3gnQT-IDo&t=710s))
**Short quotes:**
- "Know that you're going to have to create at least one really good video per $5,000 of spend." ([1:34](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=7u3gnQT-IDo&t=94s))
- "If you can make a good enough ad, you can scale that ad to literally millions of dollars in spend." ([11:20](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=7u3gnQT-IDo&t=680s))
**G&L angle:**
- Track ad spend as a percentage of new cash collected (target around 25%) and budget creative production at roughly one solid new video per $5K of spend (more early on).
- Map G&L angles as use cases. Buyers: buy a business with no or low money down, escape a corporate job, roll-up/portfolio building, vendor-finance explained, acquisition vs startup risk. Sellers: retire on your terms, maximise sale price, keep a legacy, earn interest on vendor finance, avoid a fire sale. Build a congruent funnel per major angle (at minimum separate buyer and seller funnels).
- Develop several brand faces (partners, client testimonials, UGC actors matching the 55+ seller demographic) and a no-VSL text landing page so new faces can be tested cheaply.
- Adopt the naming convention in Airtable or a sheet, and split spend across at least two ad accounts once spend is meaningful.

## How I went broke trying to get rich... and then got rich. — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=XwERThzQQBU
**Gist:** Daniel recounts how Viral Coach hit $120k → $350k → $500k → $700k/month in topline while actually losing money, because he lacked real attribution: paid ads weren't profitable (CAC ~150% of revenue) and the stable $150–300k/month base was coming from his organic content. He cut ads to ~$100/day, shrank the team, doubled down on organic, then rebuilt the paid funnel properly — going from $80k to $1M/month in about four months and later $3M+.
**Nuggets:**
- `[MINDSET]` Choose a business where you control the means of income — client acquisition you can turn up at will — rather than sponsorships/word of mouth that arrive once or twice a year. ([5:14](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=XwERThzQQBU&t=314s))
- `[SCALING-STAGES]` Topline growth hid the problem: $120k, $350k, $500k, then ~$700k in month 5 — while customer acquisition cost was ~150% of topline revenue. ([7:46](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=XwERThzQQBU&t=466s))
- `[KPIS]` Track CAC relative to lifetime value and cash actually collected — he had large accounts receivable without knowing which clients would really pay. ([8:17](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=XwERThzQQBU&t=497s))
- `[ADS]` Don't scale ad spend on a thin or unverified margin; cost per call held flat as spend rose, yet revenue didn't follow — a sign the paid funnel itself wasn't producing the closes. ([9:20](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=XwERThzQQBU&t=560s))
- `[KPIS]` Attribution to the level of certainty matters: first-touch/last-touch existed but wasn't precise enough. A six-figure client trying to back out revealed he'd never seen an ad — he came from organic content (15 posts that month). ([10:22](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=XwERThzQQBU&t=622s))
- `[ORGANIC]` Organic social was quietly producing a $150–300k/month base with zero CAC — measure the organic channel separately so you know what's really driving revenue. ([11:24](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=XwERThzQQBU&t=684s))
- `[OFFER]` If revenue only works on warm/organic traffic, conclude honestly that "the offer does not work on cold traffic" and fix it before buying more ads. ([11:54](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=XwERThzQQBU&t=714s))
- `[TEAM]` Overhead built for paid-ads growth ($700k topline with ~$200k/month payroll) leaves razor-thin margins; cutting later costs you people you respect and can't rehire. ([12:56](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=XwERThzQQBU&t=776s))
- `[FINANCE]` Illiquid assets (plane, Porsche, home equity — refi would take 30 days and was lowballed) don't save a business bleeding $200–300k/month; keep liquidity and don't run the company on unsecured debt. ([14:30](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=XwERThzQQBU&t=870s))
- `[OPERATIONS]` Neglecting a profitable side business (publishing) while chasing the new one got it shut down over unanswered regulatory emails — he lost his safety net. ([17:08](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=XwERThzQQBU&t=1028s))
- `[SALES-MGMT]` While diagnosing, he reviewed sales calls ("game tape review") and financials nightly — but the answer came from asking a client how they actually found the company. Ask every new client their source. ([19:14](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=XwERThzQQBU&t=1154s))
- `[ADS]` Turnaround playbook: cut ad spend to ~$100/day, double down on what's proven (organic), reduce staff, then rebuild understanding of the paid acquisition funnel — $80k → $1M/month in ~4 months. ([22:50](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=XwERThzQQBU&t=1370s))
- `[TEAM]` Commission-based acquisition and fulfillment roles let the team survive lean months, but staff have nothing to do when marketing is off. ([23:22](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=XwERThzQQBU&t=1402s))
- `[HIRING]` Growth then gets capped by fulfillment staffing — they paced $3M → $5M and hired a full-time recruiter to keep up. ([24:56](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=XwERThzQQBU&t=1496s))
- `[MINDSET]` Learn from others' mistakes rather than your own — failure isn't a prerequisite; surviving the worst does make day-to-day problems (staff errors, theft, legal) feel small. ([26:30](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=XwERThzQQBU&t=1590s))
- `[FINANCE]` There's a floor on how much expense you can cut, but no ceiling on income — focus on controlling acquisition (income). ([30:07](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=XwERThzQQBU&t=1807s))
**Short quotes:**
- "If I cannot control the means of production in my business… that is a game that I don't even want to play." ([5:14](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=XwERThzQQBU&t=314s))
- "There's only a limit to how much expense you can control… there's no limit to how much income you can make." ([30:07](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=XwERThzQQBU&t=1807s))
**G&L angle:**
- Add a mandatory "How did you hear about us?" field on the application plus UTM/first- and last-touch tracking, and report revenue by source (paid Meta vs. organic/YouTube/podcast vs. referral) weekly — don't assume ads are driving closes.
- Track CAC vs. cash collected (not contract value) per closed client; with seller-finance and milestone-based fees, receivables can mask a losing funnel.
- Test whether the G&L offer converts on cold traffic before scaling spend or hiring advisors/closers ahead of revenue.
- Keep ad spend scaling tied to verified profitable cohorts; keep liquidity rather than funding growth on unsecured debt.

## 8 questions improved my business by 2,629% — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=LkCQwWRMlIs
**Gist:** Daniel goes through every application-form field and pre-call signal, with real revenue-per-call data showing how each is used to disqualify, route leads to the best closers, and train the Meta pixel on the "rich kind" of lead. Takeaways: richer isn't always better, booking 3–4 days out isn't bad, and you should measure your own data.
**Nuggets:**
- `[APPLICATION]` Form fields: first name, last name, business email, phone, "tell us about your business", monthly revenue, historic marketing spend, years in business and start date. Put a dollar value (revenue per call) on every answer ([0:31](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=LkCQwWRMlIs&t=31s))
- `[APPLICATION]` Custom-domain business emails have much higher revenue per call than Gmail, Yahoo or Hotmail. Common emails still get through but are routed to weaker reps, while custom-domain leads go to the best closers. This alone improved throughput about 250% ([1:01](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=LkCQwWRMlIs&t=61s), [1:32](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=LkCQwWRMlIs&t=92s))
- `[ADS]` Feed only high-quality lead events back to Meta so the pixel learns to find more of them ("tell the pixel to get us the rich kind"). Better routing pays off today and every day after through a better-trained pixel ([2:02](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=LkCQwWRMlIs&t=122s))
- `[APPLICATION]` Phone area code predicts value. Australian leads had higher revenue per call than US or UK leads. Prioritise them without shutting off the others, since UK leads still bring about $83k/month. Area-code routing produced a 278% difference ([2:33](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=LkCQwWRMlIs&t=153s), [3:04](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=LkCQwWRMlIs&t=184s))
- `[APPLICATION]` The open-ended "tell us about your business" answer is scanned for disqualifying keywords (D2C jewelry/cosmetics, auto shops/dealerships, salons, clothing, real estate agents) and judged on length. One to three words means low quality; a paragraph means invested. Reps can decline calls. DQing these removed 12% of leads ([3:35](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=LkCQwWRMlIs&t=215s), [4:05](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=LkCQwWRMlIs&t=245s))
- `[KPIS]` Under-$10k/month revenue bracket: 53% show, only 43% of those are qualified, and call-to-close is 3%. It was worth about $115k top line across 179 calls but not the team's time, so they are cutting it. Exceptions include founders with a big prior exit ([4:37](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=LkCQwWRMlIs&t=277s), [5:08](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=LkCQwWRMlIs&t=308s), [5:39](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=LkCQwWRMlIs&t=339s))
- `[KPIS]` Higher revenue brackets close at 10–12% call-to-close, but revenue per call stops rising above $250k/month. Richer doesn't mean more able to buy. Revenue routing gave a 643% difference ([6:11](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=LkCQwWRMlIs&t=371s), [6:42](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=LkCQwWRMlIs&t=402s))
- `[APPLICATION]` Ask about budget historically ("what have you spent trying to solve this?"), never "what will you spend", because past spend predicts future spend. The same applies to fitness, relationship or coaching offers ([7:12](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=LkCQwWRMlIs&t=432s), [7:43](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=LkCQwWRMlIs&t=463s))
- `[APPLICATION]` Tier questions together. Leads with under $10k revenue and under $1k spend produce basically zero revenue and are always declined. Low budget paired with good revenue is often someone trying not to be upsold, so keep them ([8:15](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=LkCQwWRMlIs&t=495s), [8:45](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=LkCQwWRMlIs&t=525s))
- `[KPIS]` Historic marketing spend of $5–50k/month closes at 11–14%, worth thousands per call. Above $50k/month, revenue per call drops: these prospects expect two- or three-call cycles and lose interest. A $5k budget is about as valuable as $500k for a one-call-close process ([9:16](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=LkCQwWRMlIs&t=556s), [9:48](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=LkCQwWRMlIs&t=588s), [10:18](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=LkCQwWRMlIs&t=618s))
- `[MINDSET]` Don't copy competitors' minimums (some require $1M/year spend). Measure what fits your offer and sales style. His richest-only guess would have cost millions ([10:51](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=LkCQwWRMlIs&t=651s), [11:22](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=LkCQwWRMlIs&t=682s))
- `[APPLICATION]` Time in business: under 1 year is worth far less per call than 1–3 years, a 248% difference. It's used for routing, pixel feedback and setting rep expectations ([11:22](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=LkCQwWRMlIs&t=682s), [11:53](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=LkCQwWRMlIs&t=713s))
- `[APPLICATION]` A start-date question ("when will you start?") weeds out window shoppers. Anyone 30+ days out is DQ'd and sent free training. That's only about 8% of leads, but it adds timing compliance on the call ([12:23](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=LkCQwWRMlIs&t=743s), [12:54](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=LkCQwWRMlIs&t=774s))
- `[SALES-CALL]` Open the call by confirming each application answer ("You said 1–5k budget, right? 3–5 years in business? Starting within 30 days?"). This pre-empts the timing objection at the close because the prospect committed in writing ([13:26](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=LkCQwWRMlIs&t=806s))
- `[SETTING]` Pre-call responsiveness predicts quality. Not opening the reminder email means a 16% chance of showing up qualified; not replying to the text, 14%; both, 42%. Lead reps only take calls where the lead did both. The ~20% who do neither are deleted after a few hours, which gives reps back about 20% of their day ([13:56](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=LkCQwWRMlIs&t=836s), [14:27](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=LkCQwWRMlIs&t=867s), [14:57](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=LkCQwWRMlIs&t=897s))
- `[FUNNEL]` Booking lead time: calls booked 1–6 hours out average only $49 revenue per call ("found you two hours ago, need a couple weeks"), because there's no time for pre-call warming. 6–72 hours is much better, and 72–96+ hours can be worth even more. Don't restrict booking windows as gurus advise. This gave a 737% difference ([15:28](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=LkCQwWRMlIs&t=928s), [15:59](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=LkCQwWRMlIs&t=959s), [16:29](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=LkCQwWRMlIs&t=989s))
**Short quotes:**
- "We literally have a choice of telling the Meta pixel what type of prospect we want and we're going to tell them to get us the rich kind." ([2:02](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=LkCQwWRMlIs&t=122s))
- "It doesn't matter what anyone says. Don't even listen to me. Measure your own data." ([17:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=LkCQwWRMlIs&t=1020s))
**G&L angle:**
- Track revenue per call for every G&L application answer: email domain, country/area code, buyer vs seller, liquid capital, business revenue/EBITDA (for sellers), age/timeline to exit, and "have you tried to buy or sell before?" Route the top segments to the best closer.
- Ask about money historically, e.g. "What have you invested in courses, coaching or advisors to buy or sell a business?" or "How much have you set aside or spent on past deals?", not "What's your budget?"
- Send Meta CAPI only for qualified-booked or showed-and-qualified events (e.g. owners with $1M+ revenue or buyers with $X liquid), so the pixel learns to find them.
- Add a start-timeline question ("When do you want to complete an acquisition/exit?") and confirm it at the top of the call to defuse "I'll do it next year".
- Require confirmation by text reply plus email open before a lead reaches senior closers, and don't cap booking windows to 24–48 hours without testing it on G&L's own data.

## How this funnel made me $3 million  (last month) — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=o1i_mFCOJZI
**Gist:** A walkthrough of Daniel's $3M/month, 50%+ margin funnel. Traffic is about 80% ads and 20% organic by volume. It uses angle- and presenter-matched landing pages, a scored application routed to tiered closers, a pre-call SMS/email sequence, SDR follow-up for unbooked and no-show leads, a 22-email/45-day nurture, and onboarding on the call, all of which supports a 7-day money-back guarantee.
**Nuggets:**
- `[KPIS]` Lead-source mix: about 80% of sales volume from ads and 20% from organic, but organic brings 20–40% of revenue. Referrals are under 1% and outbound under 0.01% ([0:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=o1i_mFCOJZI&t=0s)).
- `[FUNNEL]` Angle-specific funnels widen the buyer pool past the $100–200k or $1M/month ceiling. Each offer angle gets its own funnel: getting views/setup, providing an editor, monetising existing content, and the "10 million view guarantee" ([0:33](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=o1i_mFCOJZI&t=33s)).
- `[FUNNEL]` Match the presenter from ad to page. A female presenter's ad leading to a male presenter's VSL caused a big conversion drop, so each creator's ads go to a landing page and VSL featuring that same person. This matters once you're above about $100k/month ([2:04](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=o1i_mFCOJZI&t=124s)).
- `[APPLICATION]` Application scores route each lead to one of three booking pages: tier 1 to the best closers ("B2B One"), mid-tier, third-tier, or SDR/disqualified. Sending the best leads to the best reps raised revenue per lead from $500 to $1,000 overnight and doubled the business ([2:35](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=o1i_mFCOJZI&t=155s)).
- `[LANDING-PAGE]` 99% of traffic is mobile, so optimise for mobile and near-instant load. Page order: brand name, Trustpilot social proof, angle-specific description, angle-specific VSL, more proof, and a "See if you qualify" button that jumps to the application. The application must be visible above the fold ([4:09](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=o1i_mFCOJZI&t=249s)).
- `[APPLICATION]` Fields: first name, last name, business email. The email domain (business vs Gmail) and phone area code help score lead value. Then "Tell us about your business" (keyword-filtered), monthly revenue brackets, last-30-days marketing spend (ask what they actually spent, not their budget, to get honest answers), time in business, and when they plan to start ([5:10](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=o1i_mFCOJZI&t=310s)).
- `[APPLICATION]` For B2C, find out ability to pay indirectly. A dating offer might ask how long they've been looking or how much they'd spend on clothes ([6:43](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=o1i_mFCOJZI&t=403s)).
- `[APPLICATION]` Use deliberately wide brackets (e.g. $50–100k) so the setter or closer has a reason to ask "closer to 50 or 100?". People give more exact answers in conversation ([7:17](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=o1i_mFCOJZI&t=437s)).
- `[LANDING-PAGE]` Below the application: heavy social proof (headline case study of turning off $60k/month ads, dozens of testimonials across industries) and a live Trustpilot feed that includes negative reviews, so skeptics can do their own due diligence ([7:47](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=o1i_mFCOJZI&t=467s)).
- `[OBJECTIONS]` Answer bad reviews publicly and openly. A senior person replies, not offshore support. Point out that you send prep material before calls, refuse to sell to people who aren't ready, and offer a refund guarantee ([8:18](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=o1i_mFCOJZI&t=498s)).
- `[SALES-MGMT]` Strict rule: reps don't sell to anyone not ready to start. A 7-day money-back guarantee catches oversold clients. Reps lose the commission on refunds and are fined if too many clients back out within the window, so they have no reason to oversell ([10:24](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=o1i_mFCOJZI&t=624s)).
- `[LANDING-PAGE]` A "Show More Proof" button leads to case studies, Clutch and G2. Sending savvy B2B prospects to more proof off-page helps. He judges page changes by revenue per website visitor, which captures both conversion rate and brand trust ([12:03](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=o1i_mFCOJZI&t=723s)).
- `[FUNNEL]` The calendar shows slots about 3 days out with a timezone confirmation. The thank-you page has a short prep video setting expectations for the call, plus more testimonials ([13:34](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=o1i_mFCOJZI&t=814s)).
- `[SETTING]` Pre-call sequence: instant confirmation email. Four minutes later, three automated iMessages "from" the assigned rep ("Hey, this is Jared… can you send me your social links?") plus the prep video as a YouTube link, which lifted video views about 25% versus the page alone ([14:35](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=o1i_mFCOJZI&t=875s)).
- `[SETTING]` No reply to the rep's text within 30 minutes signals a weaker lead and lowers its priority. Responders are removed from the extra confirmation steps ([15:05](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=o1i_mFCOJZI&t=905s)).
- `[SETTING]` Reminders alternate channels: an email 24 hours before (if booked further out than that), an SMS 4 hours before, and a channel switch 1 hour before. Sending 5–8 emails in 24 hours reduced interest. B2C (fitness, dating) needs more touches; only $100k+ enterprise deals should send fewer ([15:35](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=o1i_mFCOJZI&t=935s)).
- `[SETTING]` Put a reschedule link in every message. Without one, people cancel and never rebook ([17:07](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=o1i_mFCOJZI&t=1027s)).
- `[SALES-HIRING]` They reached $3M/month with closers only. He doesn't think setters/SDRs are needed before about $3M/month and is now piloting them for efficiency ([17:37](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=o1i_mFCOJZI&t=1057s)).
- `[SETTING]` About 20% of applicants finish the application but don't book. If they haven't booked within 5 minutes, a task goes to an SDR, who texts and double-dials within 5 minutes, tries again later that day, then once every 24 hours for 3 days, then moves them to long-term nurture ([18:10](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=o1i_mFCOJZI&t=1090s)).
- `[SETTING]` Setters contact cancels and no-shows daily for 5 days to rebook, then remove or nurture them. Setters also confirm calls by phone or text and dig into vague application answers, e.g. "you said real estate: brokerage, agent or course?", so the closer can bring matching case studies ([18:40](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=o1i_mFCOJZI&t=1120s)).
- `[EMAIL]` Long-term nurture: 24-hour gap, then 22 emails over 45 days. Response fell off sharply after 45 days. The emails were built from the highest-converting content and best parts of the programme, written by Daniel and his CMO ([20:45](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=o1i_mFCOJZI&t=1245s)).
- `[FUNNEL]` Unqualified leads (e.g. under $50k/month revenue) go to free training. Never sell services to people who aren't qualified ([21:45](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=o1i_mFCOJZI&t=1305s)).
- `[GUARANTEE]` A conditional 7-day money-back guarantee from the payment date, offered to some prospects. It protects the reps and the brand, and works because onboarding delivers value within minutes ([22:16](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=o1i_mFCOJZI&t=1336s)).
- `[OPERATIONS]` Payment on the call: wire plus screenshot, or a HubSpot card link (card triggers automatic onboarding; wire means the rep onboards manually). The client gets DocuSign, a welcome page with intake survey, joins Slack and books the AM onboarding call, all before the sales call ends (5–10 minutes) ([23:17](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=o1i_mFCOJZI&t=1397s)).
- `[FULFILLMENT]` Handoff: an automatic ping in the onboarding channel. Ops tags the associate AM and AM. The rep records a voice memo of about a minute summarising the call and suggesting the right AM. The AM introduces themselves by voice note in the client channel within the hour, often within 5 minutes. Onboarding call within 24–48 hours ([23:47](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=o1i_mFCOJZI&t=1427s)).
**Short quotes:**
- "When we started giving the best leads to our best reps, our efficiency went from $500 to $1,000 per lead overnight." ([3:38](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=o1i_mFCOJZI&t=218s))
- "under no circumstances do we sell our services to those who are not qualified to work with us" ([22:16](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=o1i_mFCOJZI&t=1336s))
**G&L angle:**
- Build separate angle funnels: "Buy a business with no money down" (buyer), "Exit-ready in 12 months" (owner 55+), and possibly "Your first acquisition" vs "Add-on acquisition for existing owners". Match each ad presenter to the same presenter on the landing page.
- G&L application fields: buyers give liquid capital available, current income/job, industry experience, target deal size, and timeline (ask for past behaviour, e.g. "have you made an offer on a business before?"). Sellers give annual revenue/EBITDA bracket, years owned, age/retirement timeline, and whether they've had a valuation. Use wide brackets so setters have a reason to ask follow-ups.
- Route high-capital buyers and larger-EBITDA sellers to the most senior advisor. Low-capital or very early buyers go to free training or low-ticket content.
- Copy the pre-call stack: rep-branded iMessage/SMS 4 minutes after booking with a prep video (deal-structure explainer + case study), alternating email/SMS reminders at 24h/4h/1h, reschedule links everywhere, SDR 5-minute speed-to-lead for unbooked applicants, and a 45-day/22-email nurture.
- Penalise oversold clients (commission clawback) and consider a short refund window backed by instant onboarding (deal-sourcing portal, intake survey, Slack/WhatsApp group, advisor voice note within an hour).

## My $3 Million per month sales script — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eMrkPt0gqFM
**Gist:** Daniel walks through his team's discovery flowchart: six key questions built on BANT (Budget, Authority, Need, Timeline). Objections are prevented during discovery rather than fought at the close. Reps classify each answer as goal-based or problem-based, "double down" until they reach the real problem, and are told to disqualify early. The team closes only 1.8% of all inbound leads and still does $3M/month.
**Nuggets:**
- `[SALES-CALL]` Structure discovery around BANT so all four classic objections (budget, authority, need, timing) are dealt with before the close.
